## [Topological Symmetry Index Decodes Critical Orbits in Neural Network Loss Landscapes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28864) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a topological symmetry index based on equivariant degree theory to systematically characterize symmetries and critical orbits in neural network loss landscapes. It applies to arbitrary architectures, including degenerate critical orbits, and demonstrates computations on softplus MLPs, Transformers, and equivariant neural networks. This provides a general mathematical tool to analyze the global structure of loss landscapes, which could deepen understanding of training dynamics and generalization in deep learning. It bridges equivariant topology with deep learning theory, potentially impacting how researchers study symmetries across diverse architectures. The index detects the isotropy of critical points and quantifies their net topological contributions, with methods specifically designed for degenerate critical orbits. The framework is illustrated on softplus MLPs, Transformers, and equivariant networks, showing its broad applicability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Neural network loss landscapes are high-dimensional and complex, making their global structure hard to analyze. Symmetries from architecture and objectives create critical orbits—sets of critical points related by symmetry—and equivariant degree theory is a topological tool for studying such symmetric functions. This work extends those methods to provide a systematic index for critical orbits in deep learning.

## [inr2action: Robot Episodes as Neural Implicit Weight Deltas for Memory and Action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a parameter-space memory framework where each robot episode is stored as a fixed-size weight delta obtained by fitting a neural implicit representation to episode video from a shared, meta-learned initialization. A learned projector maps this delta into token embeddings that condition a language model for question answering and a decoder for joint command regression, achieving a mean R² of 0.51 on the RoboMME benchmark without frame retrieval at query time. This approach decouples memory cost from time horizon, enabling scalable streaming memory for long-horizon robotic operation where episodes may span hundreds to hundreds of thousands of frames. It offers a novel alternative to frame-retrieval-based memory systems and could impact the design of vision-language-action models that require efficient recall of past experiences. Ablation studies show that replacing the weight delta with matched noise drops performance by 11.4 points, and random-origin weights are non-decodable, proving the meta-learned initialization is essential for embedding episode content. The framework is evaluated on RoboMME, a benchmark for memory-augmented robotic manipulation, and the language model exceeds chance across all question types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Neural implicit representations (INRs) encode signals such as images or 3D scenes as continuous functions parameterized by neural networks, and have been widely used in robotics for representing geometry, grasps, and scenes. Meta-learning seeks to learn a good initialization from which a model can quickly adapt to new tasks with few gradient steps. RoboMME is a large-scale benchmark for evaluating memory-augmented robotic manipulation, assessing how well models remember, reason, and act across temporal, spatial, and object-centric memory demands. This paper combines these ideas by using a meta-learned initialization to fit episode videos, storing the resulting weight delta as a compact memory.

## [RL-IF: Non-local Data Influence for Online Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28883) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RL-LOO, a trajectory-level leave-one-out quantity that measures how a training sample affects a downstream target after multiple online updates, and derives RL-IF, a first-order estimator that propagates influence through both optimization and policy-induced sampling effects. It further shows that the optimization effect alone often approximates the full estimator well, leading to RINSE, a practical data filtering method that improves training efficiency and final performance over PPO and local attribution baselines. This work addresses a genuine gap in data-centric reinforcement learning by extending data attribution from single-round local influence to non-local, multi-round influence, which is essential for understanding and improving sample efficiency in online RL. The proposed RINSE method demonstrates practical benefits in control benchmarks and an RLHF-style toxicity-mitigation setting, suggesting potential impact on experience selection and policy interpretation in real-world RL applications. RL-IF provides theoretical guarantees on approximation accuracy under smoothness assumptions, and the analysis disentangles optimization and sampling effects, showing both theoretically and empirically that the full estimator can often be well approximated by the optimization effect alone. Experiments are conducted on standard control benchmarks and an RLHF-style toxicity-mitigation setting, comparing against standard PPO and local attribution baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Online reinforcement learning trains agents through sequential interactions, and its performance is highly sensitive to the quality of training experience. Data attribution methods, such as influence functions, estimate how individual training samples affect model predictions, but existing approaches for online RL focus on local influence within a single training round. This paper extends attribution to non-local influence across multiple rounds, accounting for how updated policies change future data sampling.

## [SANE: Automated Scorer-Specific Metamorphic Testing for LLM Judges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28886) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SANE is an agent-based framework that automatically derives scorer-specific metamorphic perturbation recipes and expected score relations to test the reliability of LLM judges. It generates 3.7 times as many validated test families as the Judge Reliability Harness (JRH) across five domains and achieves a Spearman rho of 0.852 with human-labeled FaithBench performance, compared to JRH's 0.100. LLM judges are increasingly used for benchmark evaluation, model selection, and data curation, so ensuring their reliability is critical. SANE addresses a key gap by automating the generation of scorer-specific test recipes, which could lead to more trustworthy and scalable evaluation pipelines. SANE encodes behavioral requirements as reusable agent instructions and includes a scorer-independent validation framework to check whether recipes agree with scorer intent and whether test cases realize the expected relations. It detects scorer deficiencies in every domain, including professionalism and toxicity where JRH detects none.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge is a paradigm where a large language model evaluates text by returning a score, label, or preference, and it is widely used in AI evaluation. Metamorphic testing is a software testing methodology that validates relations between inputs and outputs rather than checking exact expected outputs, which helps address the oracle problem. Existing LLM-judge testing harnesses rely on human-authored perturbation recipes, limiting automation and adaptability to new scorer types.

Researchers have released JaxMARL-BC, a JAX-accelerated simulator that trains multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) macroeconomic models in seconds, achieving 75x to 724x speedups over the reference implementation and scaling to 200,000 independent learning households. The work verifies correctness against known DSGE limits and demonstrates a proof-of-concept calibration of an extended model with heterogeneous returns on capital in a two-hour experiment that would previously have taken over a month. The proof-of-concept calibration of an extended model with heterogeneous returns on capital took two hours instead of over a month, highlighting the practical impact of the speedup.

## [StyleStream 2.0 halves streaming voice style conversion latency to 520 ms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StyleStream 2.0 is a fast, controllable streaming voice style conversion framework that reduces end-to-end latency from about 1 second to 520 ms on a consumer GPU, a 2x speedup over StyleStream 1.0, while maintaining conversion quality. It also adds a text-based interface for voice design and instruction-based voice editing by routing target style information through a compact style embedding and training a unified text-conditioned flow matching model. This work brings zero-shot streaming voice style conversion closer to real-time interactive use, which could benefit live dubbing, voice assistants, and accessibility tools. The added text-based controllability makes it easier for non-experts to design or edit voices using natural language, expanding the practical applicability of voice conversion systems. Latency reduction comes from a few-step pixel MeanFlow model fine-tuned with autoregressive feedback inspired by Self Forcing, which improves robustness under streaming inference. The system removes mel-spectrogram context in favor of a compact style embedding, and experiments show it achieves the strongest target style fidelity on VSC, voice design, and voice editing while remaining competitive on intelligibility and non-edited attribute preservation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Voice style conversion (VSC) transforms a source utterance to match the timbre, accent, and emotion of a target voice while preserving linguistic content. Streaming VSC requires low latency so it can run in real time, and earlier systems like StyleStream 1.0 achieved state-of-the-art quality but had about 1 second latency and no text-based control. Pixel MeanFlow is a one-step, latent-free generative modeling approach based on flow matching, while Self Forcing is a training paradigm that simulates autoregressive inference during training to reduce the train-test gap.

SLoSa introduces a neuro-symbolic compression and fine-tuning framework that builds safety-aware foundation models for robotic task planning, using classical planners, a teacher LLM, and formal verifiers to create a safety-aware dataset, followed by supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning guided by a verification-based reward machine. Evaluated across four PDDL domains, two household simulators, edge platforms, and a desktop robotic arm, verifier-guided post-training raises success rates of compact 4B–14B planners to 70.5–76.0%, with INT4 deployment on a Jetson Orin consuming 17.85–26.41 W average power. This work addresses a key deployment barrier for LLM-based robot planners by combining the formal safety guarantees of classical planners with the flexibility of LLMs, enabling local execution on resource-constrained robots. It could impact robotics applications where safety and power efficiency are critical, such as household robots and edge devices. The framework uses a verification-based reward machine to enforce safety alignment during RL fine-tuning, and the evaluation spans PDDL domains, household simulators, and a physical robotic arm. ## [ReMAS: RL-based black-box red-teaming for multi-agent LLM systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28966) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ReMAS, the first reinforcement learning-based red-teaming framework specifically designed for multi-agent LLM systems, which fine-tunes attacker LLMs to extract system prompts through a two-stage process of prompt rewriting and template generation. Experiments show it substantially outperforms existing black-box attacks and transfers across different backend LLMs and MAS structures. This work exposes a critical security gap: single-agent red-teaming methods fail in multi-agent systems where sub-agents may not directly interact with users, leaving system prompts vulnerable. As MAS deployments grow for complex tasks, ReMAS highlights the need for stronger defenses against adaptive black-box adversaries. ReMAS uses a two-step approach: a rewrite stage that refines base attack prompts to boost extraction success, and a template generation stage that constructs attack templates to invoke specific sub-agents and reveal their system prompts. The method is validated on an arXiv preprint (2610.28966v1) and demonstrates transferability, though no peer review or community discussion is yet available.

Red-teaming is a security practice where attackers simulate adversarial attacks to find vulnerabilities; black-box red-teaming assumes no access to model internals. System prompt extraction attacks aim to reveal confidential instructions given to an LLM, which can expose sensitive logic or data.

## [Weather Foundation Models Tested for Enhanced Rock Weathering CO2 Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28973) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluates five weather foundation models as frozen encoders for quantifying CO2 removal in Enhanced Rock Weathering (ERW), using a leave-one-field-out protocol on 5,192 chronosequences from 264 fields in Brazil and the US. The best model, TerraNova-ERW built on Aurora 1.5, can order measurements within unseen fields but cannot yet predict a new field's mean removal zero-shot. ERW is a promising carbon removal approach that currently requires costly and dense soil sampling to quantify CO2 removal. This work shows that weather foundation models can transfer to ERW and support few-shot prediction, potentially reducing sampling needs by 28-98% on dense fields and lowering monitoring costs for operational deployments. The study finds that zero-shot prediction of a new field's mean removal is not yet feasible, attributed to data scale: the same architecture excels on ~10^5 natural-weathering sequences, indicating ERW data must reach similar scale. Few-shot prediction with two samples per field recovers over 60% of the highest-removal fields and roughly halves the samples needed to estimate a field's mean removal.

**Background**: Enhanced Rock Weathering (ERW) is a carbon dioxide removal technique that spreads crushed silicate rocks on agricultural soils to accelerate natural weathering, which captures CO2 and stores it as bicarbonate. Quantifying the removed CO2 typically requires dense soil sampling and laboratory analysis, which is expensive and limits scalability. Weather foundation models are large AI models pretrained on vast atmospheric data, and this study explores whether their learned representations can be repurposed to predict ERW carbon removal.

## [SSMCGM-Stream: Streaming State-Space Model for Personalized Diabetes Management](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28976) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SSMCGM-Stream introduces a streaming state-space model that maintains a personalized hidden state updated with each glucose reading, enabling constant-time forecasting, counterfactual reasoning, and interpretable forecasts via hidden-state attribution. It is paired with SSMCGM-Agent, a guarded LLM interface that translates forecasts and explanations into risk-aware natural-language decision support, evaluated on the T1DEXI cohort. This work addresses the need for accurate, interpretable, and computationally efficient glucose forecasting for real-time on-device diabetes management, potentially enabling continuous decision support without cloud dependency. It also demonstrates how streaming state-space models can combine forecasting with counterfactual reasoning and LLM-based interfaces for healthcare applications. The model achieves forecasting performance comparable to strong neural baselines while substantially reducing computational cost, and its interpretability comes from attributing forecasts to the hidden state. The LLM-based agent is described as 'guarded,' suggesting safety constraints, but the abstract does not specify the guardrail mechanisms or the size of the T1DEXI cohort.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: State-space models (SSMs) are a class of sequence models that represent a system's latent state and update it over time, offering linear or constant-time complexity compared to the quadratic cost of Transformer attention. Continuous glucose monitoring (CGM) devices collect frequent glucose readings, and forecasting future levels is crucial for diabetes management, but models must be lightweight enough for on-device use. Counterfactual reasoning involves simulating outcomes of alternative actions (e.g., insulin doses or meals) to support decision-making, while hidden-state attribution explains predictions by analyzing the model's internal state.

## [TimeFT: A Frequency-Based Tokenizer Eliminating Spectral Distortion in Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies that existing time series tokenization strategies inherently introduce spectral distortions, and proposes TimeFT, a nearly distortion-free frequency-based tokenizer that uses frequency-domain partitioning, frequency shifting, and Nyquist sampling. TimeFT is parameter-free, incurs negligible computational cost, and can serve as a drop-in replacement for existing tokenizers. This work provides a theoretical explanation for why common tokenization harms time series models and offers a practical, lightweight fix that consistently improves forecasting, classification, and anomaly detection. It could influence the design of future time series foundation models and representation learning pipelines. The authors derive boundary conditions under which spectral distortion occurs, and TimeFT achieves near-distortion-free tokenization without learnable parameters, making it easy to integrate into existing architectures. Experiments across diverse tasks show consistent and significant performance gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Tokenization, which splits time series into subsequences, is a foundational step in modern time series modeling, analogous to tokenization in NLP. However, partitioning in the time domain can distort the frequency content of the signal, affecting the model's ability to capture trends, periodicity, and seasonality. Nyquist sampling is a classical signal processing principle that determines the minimum sampling rate needed to avoid aliasing and preserve frequency information.

## [VeriEvolve: Evaluator-First Evolution Triples Agent Success on MCPMark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29005) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VeriEvolve introduces an evaluator-first evolution framework that synthesizes executable evaluators and instruction-grounded challenge states from task instructions, then uses the frozen evaluator as a fitness signal to evolve tool-using agent workflows. On the 30 MCPMark Filesystem tasks, it built 30 evaluators with 141 challenge states, achieving 141/141 agreement on generated suites and 113/122 agreement on held-out states, and raised externally verified success from 6/30 to 18/30 for GLM-5.2 and from 27/30 to 30/30 for Claude. Reliable fitness signals are a key bottleneck for self-improving agents on realistic stateful tasks, and VeriEvolve shows that executable evaluators can be synthesized and used to guide search when success is observable and an external verifier is available for calibration. This could make automated workflow evolution more practical for MCP-based tool-use agents across environments like Filesystem, PostgreSQL, Playwright, Notion, and GitHub. The evaluator is frozen during each evolution round and revised between rounds by converting end states of strong and weak runs into counterfactual test cases; refinement removed all three false positives exposed by the weak executor. Cross-environment studies on PostgreSQL, Playwright, Notion, and GitHub reveal complementary limits from observability, state representation, and search/execution capacity, and the work remains a single arXiv preprint without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: MCPMark is a benchmark of 127 realistic tasks across Notion, GitHub, Filesystem, PostgreSQL, and Playwright that stress-tests whether LLM agents can plan, call tools via the Model Context Protocol (MCP), and recover from failures in multi-step, environment-rich workflows. Self-improving agents typically rely on a fitness signal to guide evolutionary search over workflows, but such signals are hard to obtain for stateful tasks where success depends on the final environment state rather than a simple output. VeriEvolve addresses this by building an executable evaluator that checks challenge states before optimizing the agent.

## [PHD-Hand: A 3D-Printable Humanoid Dexterous Hand with Linkage-Driven 3-DOA Digits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29030) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present PHD-Hand, a fully linkage-driven, 3D-printable humanoid dexterous hand that achieves three degrees of actuation (DOA) per digit using a novel flexure hinge and elastic drive rods made of spring steel. Unlike comparable linkage-driven designs, it avoids many miniature moving parts, allowing fabrication on a budget 3D printer and sizing close to a human hand. Dexterous hands are widely seen as the final hardware bottleneck preventing generalist robots from physically assisting people at home and work, and conventional tendon-driven designs suffer from high cost, maintenance demands, and poor durability. By lowering the barrier to entry for building and developing dexterous hands, this design could accelerate research and adoption of humanoid manipulation. The hand simplifies linkage drivetrains through a unique flexure hinge combined with elastic spring-steel drive rods, and the paper reports a prototype evaluated for grasping and object manipulation along with new findings on kinematics and control. It is an arXiv preprint (2610.29030) and has not yet undergone peer review.

**Background**: Dexterous robot hands are typically tendon-driven, using cables to actuate joints, but these systems are costly, high-maintenance, and prone to wear. Linkage-driven alternatives use rigid mechanical linkages instead, but integrating three or more independent actuation degrees per finger with linkages is difficult to design and fabricate. A flexure hinge is a compliant joint that bends elastically rather than using a pin, which simplifies mechanisms and enables single-piece fabrication.

## [In-house LoRA judge triages 148K coding sessions at 1% of frontier cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29052) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29052) reports an in-house System 1 'typed decision' judge that uses a frozen backbone with a rank-16 LoRA adapter and a pointer head to triage about 148K agentic coding session records in a single forward pass. It replaces a frontier LLM judge that cost roughly $1,000 per pass, while the new judge processes 1,000 sessions in about three minutes on one GPU for about $0.06, roughly 1% of the cost. This shows that a small, self-hosted typed-decision model can replace expensive frontier LLM judges in agentic coding data pipelines, cutting cost and latency by two orders of magnitude while keeping sensitive session data inside the organization. It also provides evidence that parameter-free readouts over LoRA-adapted backbones can be competitive on public benchmarks like JevBench, which could lower the barrier to building custom judges. The in-house judge answers three typed questions in one forward pass and its stored labels matched a fresh two-judge consensus on only 26.9% of sessions where the two judges agreed, highlighting reliability issues with the frontier judge. In Part II, a single-seed 35B model scores 79.3% on the 111 public hard items of JevBench (not significantly different from the commercial reference at 73.0%, unadjusted p=0.12), while a 2B model beats all four kev checkpoints; an ablation finds no detectable benefit from training the 26 letter rows of the output layer, and a retrain on a later corpus moves the 2B's hard-tier accuracy by 11.7 points, showing how little small evaluations resolve.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge uses a large language model to evaluate text by generating a score or label, but this is costly and slow at scale. Typed decision models instead compute a probability for each allowed option in a single forward pass by reading the model's logits or a small head over hidden states, avoiding text generation and parsing. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes the pre-trained weights and injects small trainable rank decomposition matrices, enabling cheap adaptation of large models. A pointer head scores hidden states at option positions against a decision marker, producing option probabilities without a full language-model head.

## [Role-Enhanced Zero-Shot CoT Prompting Boosts LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29073) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes Role-Enhanced Zero-Shot Chain-of-Thought (RE-ZS-CoT) prompting, which integrates domain-specific expert roles either statically (predefined roles) or dynamically (model-selected roles) without task-specific examples. Evaluated across 20 LLMs and seven benchmarks, RE-ZS-CoT frequently outperforms zero-shot and zero-shot-CoT baselines, with gains of up to 44 percentage points. This large-scale empirical study demonstrates that combining role prompting with zero-shot chain-of-thought can yield substantial performance gains across diverse models and tasks, offering a practical, resource-efficient alternative to few-shot prompting. The findings are relevant for prompt engineering research and for practitioners seeking to improve LLM reasoning without task-specific examples. The study compares static role selection (eight predefined expert roles) with dynamic role selection (model autonomously picks the most suitable role per benchmark), finding that static roles achieve peak performance while dynamic roles show strong correspondence in where improvements occur. Ablation studies reveal that unconventional roles degrade performance, and scalability is validated across model sizes from 2B to 14B parameters, with smaller models like Granite3.3-8B excelling in arithmetic reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Zero-shot chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting, introduced by Kojima et al. (2022), improves LLM reasoning by simply appending 'Let's think step by step' to a prompt, without needing task-specific examples. Role prompting is another technique where the model is instructed to adopt a specific persona (e.g., 'You are a math expert') to condition its responses. RE-ZS-CoT combines these two ideas by assigning expert roles to guide step-by-step reasoning in a zero-shot setting.

## [ReSeg: Retraining-free zero-shot abdominal organ segmentation via synthetic data pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29090) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReSeg introduces a two-stage, retraining-free framework for zero-shot abdominal organ segmentation that uses domain-randomized synthetic data to pretrain a Generalizable Semantic Representation Network (GSRNet), followed by training an Organ-specific Encoder-decoder Segmentation Network (OESNet) initialized with those weights. Evaluated on seven abdominal organ segmentation datasets across CT, MRI, and PET, ReSeg consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods without requiring any real training images. This work addresses two major barriers in medical imaging AI: poor generalization across unseen real image domains and privacy risks associated with large-scale clinical data collection. By eliminating the need for real training images and dataset-specific finetuning, ReSeg could enable broader deployment of abdominal organ segmentation across institutions and modalities. ReSeg relies on two dedicated data engines: one generates semantically and visually diverse synthetic images for organ-agnostic pretraining, and the other produces diversified visual images while strictly preserving the inherent spatial layout of abdominal organs for segmentation training. The framework is evaluated across three modalities (CT, MRI, PET) and seven datasets, though it remains an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Domain-randomized synthetic data pretraining is a technique that trains deep neural networks on synthetic images with randomized intensities and anatomical content to improve generalization to real images. Zero-shot segmentation aims to segment organs in unseen domains without any target-domain training data, which is particularly valuable in medical imaging where annotated data is scarce and privacy-sensitive. Existing approaches either underperform on abdominal organs or require dataset-specific finetuning, motivating retraining-free frameworks like ReSeg.

## [MixAgent: Agentic AI Pipeline for Reference-Guided Music Mixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29093) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce MixAgent, an agentic pipeline that follows audio engineers' workflow to create interpretable, editable mixing plans guided by a reference song. It includes a new MixCritic dataset of 45,657 repair critiques and a critic trained with supervised fine-tuning plus preference optimization, reducing mix error by 19% on MedleyDB. This work addresses a key weakness of current multimodal models—unreliable mix correction—by combining a structured agentic workflow with a preference-optimized critic, potentially making AI-assisted music post-production more reliable and editable for audio engineers. It also contributes a substantial new dataset that could spur further research in reference-based automatic mixing. The pipeline uses a grouping agent to route tracks into subgroups, a planning agent to select and order effects using retrieved examples, and a detector-critic pair to propose numerical parameter adjustments. In a human listening test, MixAgent received higher quality ratings than mixes produced by Qwen3-Omni, Gemini 3.8 Flash, and GPT-4o-audio.

## [GRAFT fixes rare-word collapse in continuous diffusion language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29136) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29136) shows that continuous diffusion language models like ELF systematically underrepresent rare words, generating them at only 0.30x the human rate, and proposes GRAFT, a training-free method that restores the rare-word share from 0.42-0.77x to 0.82-1.10x of the human rate across three ELF scales and on LangFlow. This identifies a concrete failure mode—lexical long-tail collapse—in a promising class of diffusion language models that otherwise match autoregressive quality with far fewer sampling steps, and offers a practical fix without retraining, which matters for researchers and practitioners relying on these models for diverse or rare vocabulary. The gap persists from 105M to 652M parameters and cannot be closed by any sampler setting the models expose; GRAFT works by committing rare words while the model is still undecided, then regenerating the sequence with those commitments held in place, improving the overall word-frequency profile by 2.7x on ELF-B and 12x on LangFlow.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Continuous diffusion language models generate text by iteratively denoising in a continuous embedding space and only map to discrete tokens at the final step, unlike discrete-token diffusion models that discretize at every step. ELF (Embedded Language Flows), introduced in May 2026, is a prominent example that uses continuous-time Flow Matching and a weight-tied unembedding for the final decode. Autoregressive models like GPT-2 instead sample one token at a time, which the paper argues preserves rare-word diversity.

## [ORSMT uses SMT solvers to verify and repair LLM-generated optimization models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29145) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ORSMT introduces a typed semantic interface that uses SMT solvers to perform exact relation queries, verifying and repairing LLM-generated optimization artifacts across 3,034 problems and six generator sizes. Protected selection raises the model-code match rate from 43.2% to 88.2% and the share of outputs that are both correct and matched from 25.2% to 40.2%. This addresses a critical reliability gap in LLM-based optimization modeling, where generated mathematics, solver code, and reports can be mutually inconsistent or omit source requirements. The principled SMT-based approach could become essential infrastructure for deploying LLM-driven optimization in real-world decision-making. ORSMT aligns observable decisions, projects auxiliary variables, and proves or refutes feasible region and objective relations, localizing refutations to specific rows or domains. On 1,905 refuted outputs, certified voting under a shared fallback makes 50.9% correct and matched, outperforming ReLoop (44.8%) and Opt-Verifier (49.4%), and a one-round source revision restores model-code match for 84.4% of refuted pairs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT) solvers determine whether mathematical formulas are satisfiable, extending SAT solving to handle arithmetic and other theories. In LLM-based optimization, models translate natural language problems into mathematical programs, solver code, and reports, but verifying consistency across these artifacts is challenging because equivalent models can use different auxiliary variables and consistent artifacts can share the same omission. ORSMT leverages SMT solvers to provide exact semantic comparison and repair, ensuring that adopted candidates pass required relation checks.

## [Study Localizes Timbre Information Across Speech Signal Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29150) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29150) systematically probes where speaker/timbre information resides in speech by analyzing frequency bands, phase/magnitude structure, WORLD source-filter decomposition, voiced/unvoiced regions, self-supervised learning (SSL) layers, and EnCodec/HuBERT tokens, supplemented by a 45-listener perceptual test across Mandarin, English, and Japanese speech and singing. The study finds that 1–8 kHz magnitude and vocal-tract spectral-envelope structure dominate perception, while above-8 kHz bands remain machine-discriminative and shallow SSL layers expose compact speaker information. This localization study provides actionable guidance for generative speech systems such as voice conversion, speaker-cloning TTS, and zero-shot speech models, indicating where to condition on or transform speaker information and where content, codec, and token pipelines should audit residual speaker leakage. It also offers insights for representation learning and the design of privacy-aware speech processing. The findings show that speaker information is distributed rather than concentrated: 1–8 kHz magnitude and vocal-tract spectral-envelope structure dominate human perception, above-8 kHz bands remain machine-discriminative, shallow SSL layers carry compact speaker information, and token leakage depends on the choice of tokenizer and layer. The perceptual test involved 45 listeners across three languages, adding cross-linguistic validity to the conclusions.

The study uses speaker identity as a measurable proxy and employs tools like the WORLD vocoder, which parameterizes speech into pitch, spectral envelope, and aperiodicity, and SSL models like HuBERT, which learn representations from unlabeled audio. A new arXiv paper presents an AI-driven toolchain that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with Model-Driven Engineering (MDE) to automatically transform natural-language requirements into system architectures, executable software, and validation artifacts for Software-Defined Vehicles (SDVs). The approach, developed within the Central Car Server (CeCaS) and follow-up EU-funded projects, was validated on a centralized vehicle computing platform supporting adaptive cruise control and emergency braking using Hardware-in-the-Loop (HiL) and Software-in-the-Loop (SiL) testing. This work demonstrates a practical path toward automating safety-critical automotive software development, potentially reducing manual effort and errors while improving traceability and consistency. If successful, it could accelerate the adoption of SDV architectures across the automotive industry, affecting OEMs, suppliers, and tool vendors. The toolchain emphasizes traceability, consistency, and compliance with safety-critical automotive development requirements, and the paper discusses current capabilities, practical limitations, and future directions toward increasingly automated development with minimal human intervention. Validation was performed on a centralized vehicle computing platform, indicating a focus on next-generation zonal or central computing architectures.

Model-Driven Engineering (MDE) is a software development methodology that uses domain models as first-class artifacts to improve productivity and quality. Hardware-in-the-Loop (HiL) and Software-in-the-Loop (SiL) are simulation-based testing techniques used to validate complex real-time embedded systems, with HiL involving actual hardware and SiL using only software models.

<a id="item-118"></a>
## [AI-generated text is harder to understand due to psycholinguistic complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29162) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv study (2610.29162) finds that AI-generated text across nine frontier models uses more psycholinguistically difficult features—such as inanimate subjects with animate verbs, low-frequency n-grams, and longer syntactic dependencies—than human writing. The authors hypothesize that models optimized for math, code, and reasoning prioritize information density over communicative success, and they provide a proof-of-concept by fine-tuning a model with a length penalty that diverges from human-like language. This work identifies specific axes of misalignment between human processing demands and LLM outputs, which could inform readability improvements, better human-AI interaction, and NLP evaluation metrics. It also suggests that optimizing models for reasoning tasks may inadvertently degrade their communicative naturalness. The study uses a novel corpus of AI-generated text across diverse domains and compares nine frontier and near-frontier models, finding substantial variation across model families. The proof-of-concept fine-tuning under a length penalty demonstrates that pressure for information density leads to divergence from human-like linguistic behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Psycholinguistics studies how humans process language, and features like object relative clauses, long syntactic dependencies, and low-frequency n-grams are known to increase processing difficulty. Large language models are trained to predict the next token, often on data and objectives that reward information density (e.g., code, math, reasoning), rather than on successful human communication. This study connects these two areas by measuring psycholinguistic features in AI-generated text.

## [Nuisance Orthogonality Predicts World Action Model Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29168) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29168) introduces 'nuisance orthogonality' as a measurable representational prior that predicts how well world action models (WAMs) generalize to visual variations after policy training. Across multiple image and video generative backbones, higher orthogonality of features under out-of-distribution visual changes correlates with stronger robustness in the trained policy. This work offers a geometric perspective that could let researchers prospectively screen generative backbones for embodied AI, potentially reducing costly policy training and improving robustness in robot manipulation. It bridges representation learning theory with practical world-model design, affecting robotics and embodied AI practitioners. The metric, nuisance orthogonality, measures the orthogonality of a feature under visual variation outside the empirical in-distribution manifold; the study validates this across various image and video backbones and further shows that robust WAMs exhibit nuisance suppression, i.e., relative suppression of feature displacement caused by such variations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: World action models (WAMs) are embodied predictive-action models that forecast future states to guide action, often built on large video or image generative backbones. Their generalization varies widely depending on the backbone, but the reasons are poorly understood. This paper investigates the representational geometry inherited from the backbone and how policy training reorganizes it, hypothesizing that a useful prior separates nuisance information from the in-distribution manifold before policy training.

## [TrainBridge uses GPU compilation evidence to optimize Ascend NPU training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TrainBridge is an agentic method that extracts optimization intents from mature GPU compilation stacks and uses them as references to rewrite and iteratively profile-tune Ascend NPU training programs. It achieves an average 1.7x speedup across four workloads and nearly 2.0x across 27 Modded-NanoGPT speedrun records. This work addresses the gap in compiler optimization maturity between NVIDIA GPUs and Ascend NPUs by transferring optimization knowledge, potentially lowering the barrier for migrating AI training workloads to non-NVIDIA accelerators. It also demonstrates how agentic AI can automate complex performance tuning tasks that traditionally require costly trial and error. The method relies on extracting optimization intents from GPU execution traces rather than directly porting code, and it iteratively refines rewritten NPU programs through profiling. Evaluation covers LLM pretraining, image classification, and vision-language training, but the approach is presented in a single arXiv preprint without community discussion or peer review yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Ascend NPUs are AI accelerators developed by Huawei as an alternative to NVIDIA GPUs, and they rely on frameworks like PyTorch through adapters such as torch_npu. Operator fusion is a key compiler optimization that combines multiple operations into a single kernel to reduce memory traffic and launch overhead, but it is only partially applied in NPU training programs, leaving fragmented computation. Agentic AI refers to systems that can autonomously plan and execute multi-step tasks, and here it is used to automate the optimization process.

<a id="item-121"></a>
## [Conditional Flow Models Convert Epistemic Uncertainty into Apparent Aleatoric Variance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29172) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29172) identifies a systematic phenomenon in Conditional Flow Models (CFMs): their generated samples often have larger variance than the true conditional distribution, even in nearly deterministic regression tasks. The authors show that this excess variance reflects epistemic uncertainty about the conditional distribution being converted into apparent aleatoric variance, and that it decreases with more training or data. This finding has important implications for uncertainty quantification and calibration when using CFMs as predictive models, since inflated variance can mislead downstream decision-making. It also suggests a practical use-case: designing high-coverage predictive sets that reliably cover the ground-truth regression target. The effect is most pronounced where the model has not resolved dependence on the conditioning variable, and comparisons with explicit smoothing estimators confirm that the induced variance tracks model underfitting of complex or hard-to-fit targets. The paper provides a theoretical account in a simplified setting and validates the phenomenon through controlled synthetic and real-data experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Conditional Flow Models (CFMs) are generative models that learn to transform a simple base distribution into a conditional distribution given inputs, often used for tasks like regression and uncertainty quantification. In uncertainty quantification, aleatoric uncertainty refers to inherent randomness in the data, while epistemic uncertainty refers to uncertainty due to lack of knowledge or model limitations. The paper examines how CFMs, which produce full conditional distributions, may conflate these two types of uncertainty.

<a id="item-122"></a>
## [AI Approval Monitors Can Fail Together, Paper Warns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29178) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29178) models correlated failures among AI approval monitors using fail-prone systems from distributed computing, showing that monitors sharing a base model or a poisoned evidence source can fail together. The authors evaluated this across twelve small open-weights models, three closed frontier models (Claude Opus 5.5, GPT-6 Astra, Gemini 4 Argon), and DeepSeek-V3.2, finding that every reliable fooling of a monitor into approving fraud came through an evidence source and affected only that source's readers. This work addresses a real and underexplored safety problem: aggregating multiple AI monitors does not guarantee safety if their failures are correlated, which is increasingly likely as organizations deploy monitors built on the same foundation models. The findings suggest that diversifying inputs and counting sources rather than votes can significantly reduce false blocks while maintaining safety, with implications for any agentic system that relies on multi-monitor approval. On new data, a rule that counts sources instead of votes blocked 0.9% of correct payments, compared to 7.4% for the best counting rule, at equal safety; however, the pre-registered worst-case test failed because one attack left approvers on a single source, meaning no rule over the twelve language-model monitors survives every measured attack. Adding a non-persuadable rule engine restored the condition for a safe and live rule on the measured attacks, and the authors recommend diversifying inputs, hardening monitors, and counting sources, not votes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: In distributed computing, a fail-prone system is a list of sets of nodes that may fail together, used to reason about quorum systems and Byzantine fault tolerance. This paper borrows that concept to model AI approval monitors, where monitors that share a base model or a poisoned input may fail in a correlated way. The work also touches on prompt injection, where an attacker plants instructions in data that a monitor reads, and on data poisoning, where an attacker corrupts the evidence source itself.

## [LTEM: LLM-Friendly Table-Enhanced Markdown for Table Understanding and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a table-structure taxonomy decomposing tables into six atomic features (F1-F6) and six complexity levels (L1-L6), and constructs STB, a controlled benchmark of 1,089 synthetic tables distributed across these levels. It then proposes LTEM, a formally specified minimal Markdown extension that explicitly encodes merged cells and hierarchical headers, and shows across five state-of-the-art LLMs that LTEM achieves accuracy comparable to HTML with a favorable accuracy-token trade-off and substantially fewer invalid responses than LaTeX. Real-world tables in scientific papers, financial reports, and statistical summaries frequently use merged cells and multi-level headers, yet the lightweight formats LLMs handle best (Markdown, CSV) cannot express these structures while expressive formats (HTML, LaTeX) impose high token cost and parsing complexity. LTEM offers practitioners a practical middle ground for structured-data pipelines, and the released taxonomy, benchmark, and format specification provide a foundation for systematic future research on LLM-table interaction. LTEM targets L3-L5 structures and remains linear to read and parsable in a single pass, with less verbosity than HTML/LaTeX. For table generation, LTEM is comparable to MultiMarkdown though HTML remains best, and the benchmark STB contains 1,089 tables systematically distributed across complexity levels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models process tabular data through serialized text formats, and the choice of format affects both token cost and the model's ability to recover structure. Markdown and CSV are token-efficient but cannot represent merged cells or multi-level headers, while HTML and LaTeX can represent them but are verbose and error-prone for models to generate. This paper formalizes that trade-off with a taxonomy and a controlled benchmark, then proposes a minimal Markdown extension to balance expressiveness and efficiency.

## [DiagSafe: Rubric-Based Diagnostic Evaluation for LLM Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29225) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced DiagSafe, a diagnostic evaluation framework that replaces binary safe/unsafe labels with prompt-level rubrics assessing safety and usefulness separately, backed by a bilingual English-Chinese benchmark built on an 84-label safety taxonomy. Experiments show DiagSafe achieves 95.34% agreement with human annotations, far above the 69%-87% of baselines, with high inter-judge consistency (Cohen's κ = 0.864). Binary safety labels offer little explanatory depth and automated judges often disagree, so DiagSafe's rubric-based approach could make LLM safety auditing more reliable and interpretable. Its findings that leading models differ markedly in unsafe rates and are especially vulnerable in Chinese and multi-turn contexts highlight concrete gaps for alignment work. The benchmark has two subsets: DiagBase covers single- and multi-turn prompts with direct harmful queries and template-based jailbreaks, while DiagAttack embeds harmful intents into complex instruction-following tasks to form challenging instruction attacks. Each rubric includes specific exemptions to suppress false positives, and the usefulness dimension is evaluated independently of safety.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: LLM safety benchmarks typically classify model responses as simply safe or unsafe, which gives little insight into why a response is problematic and leads to inconsistent judgments when automated LLMs act as judges. Rubric-based evaluation instead uses detailed criteria to score outputs along multiple dimensions, and jailbreaks are prompts designed to bypass a model's safety guardrails. DiagSafe builds on these ideas by grounding its rubrics in globally applicable AI safety policies and testing both English and Chinese prompts.

## [MOSAIC: Omni-Modal Benchmark Diagnoses Long-Term Memory Failures in Conversational Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29249) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOSAIC introduces a benchmark of 923 questions over persona-grounded multi-session histories (11–24 sessions) spanning text, image, video, and audio, with targeted spatial, temporal, and prosodic signals. It pairs these with a matched diagnostic protocol that freezes the question, reader, prompt, and budget while intervening on representation, retrieval, and delivery to localize where evidence is lost. This benchmark shifts omni-modal memory evaluation from a single leaderboard score to a diagnostic decomposition, revealing that representation loss is modality-dependent and residual reading dominates even with gold evidence. It will help researchers and developers of conversational agents identify and fix specific failure stages in long-term memory and cross-modal reasoning. The benchmark constructs targeted spatial, temporal, and prosodic signals rather than decorative media, with the strongest causal validation for spatial information and selector-level validation for annotated prosody. Matched decomposition shows that abstention remains weak: strong answerable scores coexist with failures to refuse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term memory benchmarks for conversational agents have traditionally focused on text or text-and-image inputs and report a single end-to-end score, making it hard to pinpoint where memory fails. Omni-modal assistants must also remember what users show and convey through video and audio, including prosodic cues. MOSAIC builds on this need by creating persona-grounded histories with controlled multimodal signals and a diagnostic protocol inspired by causal intervention.

## [Hierarchical Masked Diffusion Language Model Guides Token Generation Order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29255) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HMDLM introduces a two-pass hierarchical masked diffusion language model that assigns each token position a hierarchy level from training data, using a first pass to predict a hierarchy distribution over masked positions and a second pass to predict token distributions conditioned on it. Giving each hierarchy level its own noise schedule creates a soft preference for revealing higher-priority positions earlier while keeping decoding fully parallel. This approach improves performance on tasks with natural priority structures, reducing perplexity by over 1.5 points on LM1B and up to 7 points on WikiHow compared to strong diffusion baselines, and increasing Sudoku solution accuracy by up to 8.6 absolute points. It shows that incorporating hierarchical priors into masked diffusion can make parallel generation more effective for structured tasks. The hierarchy levels require zero or minimal additional annotations, and the model re-derives the forward and reverse processes and the NELBO for this factorization. An LLM judge on WikiHow preferred HMDLM generations more than twice as often as the baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models are an alternative to autoregressive models that generate text by iteratively denoising a fully masked sequence in parallel rather than predicting tokens left-to-right. Standard masked diffusion uses a shared noise schedule across all positions, without explicitly encoding which positions should be resolved first. Many tasks have a natural priority order, such as titles guiding body text or Sudoku clues determining easier cells before harder ones.

## [CRAB: Cuboid-Based Representation for As-Built BIM from Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29257) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CRAB introduces a cuboid-based framework that combines ProposalNet for coarse component cuboid prediction with AssemblyNet, a relation-aware Flow Matching model, to reconstruct editable, IFC-compatible BIM components from point clouds. The method encodes walls, doors, windows, beams, and columns as architectural cuboids with semantics and parameters, and achieves a mean interior wall thickness error of 2.80 cm, with 79.50% of matched walls within 3 cm. This addresses a real gap in scan-to-BIM pipelines, where existing methods often rely on handcrafted rules or fail to recover wall thickness, opening geometry, and component-level BIM structure. By producing editable, IFC-compatible components, CRAB could reduce manual correction effort and improve interoperability in as-built modeling workflows. To address limited cuboid-level supervision, the authors extend Structured3D with component-wise cuboid annotations and construct cuboid-based procedural scenes. Experiments show CRAB surpasses floor plan and plane-based alternatives in component completeness, geometric accuracy, and generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: As-built BIM reconstruction from point clouds aims to create semantic, volumetric, parametric, and editable building models from laser scans. IFC is a common open file format for BIM interoperability, and scan-to-BIM is a standard workflow in the architecture, engineering, and construction industry. Flow Matching is a recent generative modeling approach that learns a velocity field to transform a simple distribution into a complex one, offering an alternative to diffusion models.

## [Flat-RLVR uses sharpness-aware minimization to diversify LLM reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29281) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Flat-RLVR introduces sharpness-aware minimization into reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) to find flat reward optima, theoretically linking flat reward regions to reduced policy distribution sharpening. Empirically, it improves sample diversity while maintaining single-attempt accuracy when combined with GRPO variants. This addresses a known failure mode of RLVR/GRPO post-training where the policy becomes overly peaked and suppresses rare-but-correct reasoning paths, which can hurt exploration and generalization. The method offers a principled way to improve the trade-off between sampling efficiency and reasoning capacity in LLM post-training. Flat-RLVR performs sharpness-aware minimization during LLM post-training, which typically requires an extra forward-backward pass to estimate the sharpness-aware gradient, potentially increasing training cost. The paper provides both theoretical and empirical support, but details on scale, benchmarks, and computational overhead are not specified in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a post-training paradigm where rewards come from automatic rule-based checkers, and it has been central to improving LLM reasoning, especially with group-relative policy optimization (GRPO). Sharpness-aware minimization (SAM) is an optimization technique that seeks flat loss regions to improve generalization by minimizing both loss value and loss sharpness. Flat-RLVR combines these ideas to smooth the policy distribution in RLVR.

## [Online NashGap Certificate for General-Sum Extensive-Form Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29300) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper derives an online certificate for the NashGap of iterates from heuristic solvers in general-sum extensive-form games, using regret and terminal-distribution accumulators to bound the gap with linear cost per checkpoint. It shows that in two-player games the NashGap of a weighted average strategy equals the weighted external regret plus a signed value correction, and the certificate remains sound under alternating updates and for three or more players. This provides a practical way for heuristic solvers to certify their own solution quality without computing a best response, which is often expensive. It could improve trust in game-solving algorithms and enable more efficient portfolio methods for finding Nash equilibria in complex games. The certificate is read off the regret state with no best response against the average, and the mismatch between alternation and averages enters as a signed comparator term maximized in a single backward pass per player. On biased Leduc benchmarks, a pool of sixteen training-free solvers solves 89% of 64 instances at threshold 10^-5, matching the trajectory-minimum union, and the zero-best-response certificate certifies all 57 in-sample solves and 58 of 59 held-out ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Extensive-form games model sequential decision-making with imperfect information, and Nash equilibrium is a standard solution concept. Counterfactual regret minimization (CFR) is a popular algorithm that minimizes regret to approximate Nash equilibria, but in general-sum games finding Nash equilibria is PPAD-complete and heuristic solvers are often used without guarantees. The NashGap measures how far a strategy profile is from equilibrium, and certifying it typically requires computing a best response, which can be costly.

## [YingLan: Multimodal Ocean Foundation Model Unifies Heterogeneous Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29313) ⭐️ 7.0/10

YingLan is a new multimodal ocean foundation model that integrates physics-attribute-conditioned dynamic encoding, Spatiotemporal Increment Coupling (STIC), and ocean-state evolution-aware pretraining to learn transferable representations from satellite, gridded, and buoy observations. In a seven-day sea surface salinity forecasting task in Shenzhen coastal waters, it achieved an average RMSE of 0.687508 PSU, reducing relative error by 0.58%, 5.31%, and 12.42% compared with LangYa, XiHe, and GloNet2, respectively. This work addresses the open problem of learning transferable representations from heterogeneous ocean observations, moving beyond task-specific models toward a general foundation model for ocean modeling. Its implementation on MindSpore and Huawei Ascend 910B NPUs also demonstrates compatibility with domestic AI computing infrastructure, which could lower barriers for large-scale ocean AI research in China. The model uses dynamic encoding to generate adaptive input projections conditioned on physical semantics and observation characteristics, while STIC models multiscale spatiotemporal dependencies through cross-scale interactions of latent temporal increments. The pretraining combines masked observation reconstruction with temporal evolution objectives, but the authors note that broader cross-task and cross-regional evaluations are still needed to assess generalization.

**Background**: Ocean modeling traditionally relies on numerical models and task-specific machine learning, but the diversity of data sources—satellite imagery, gridded reanalysis fields, and in-situ buoys—poses challenges for unified representation learning. Foundation models, which are pretrained on large unlabeled datasets and then adapted to downstream tasks, have shown success in vision and language and are now being explored for geoscientific domains. <a id="item-131"></a>
## [OpenMAP-HIE: Unsupervised Diffusion Model Segments Neonatal HIE Lesions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29322) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OpenMAP-HIE introduces a pathology-informed score-based diffusion model that localizes neonatal hypoxic-ischemic encephalopathy (HIE) lesions on ADC images without voxel-wise supervision by comparing conditional and unconditioned noise predictions. On the BONBID-HIE dataset, it achieved a Dice score of 0.283±0.244, average precision of 0.505, and Hausdorff distance of 25.45±15.63 mm, with optional Z-map refinement boosting Dice to 0.602±0.269. This work addresses the scarcity of expert annotations in neonatal HIE imaging by offering an unsupervised segmentation approach that performs comparably to supervised ADC-only models, potentially enabling automated lesion detection in clinical settings where labeled data is limited. It also demonstrates a novel inference strategy for diffusion models in medical anomaly detection, which could inspire similar annotation-free methods for other pathologies. The method derives anomaly evidence from differences between conditional and unconditioned noise predictions at independently evaluated diffusion noise levels, avoiding a full reverse-diffusion chain, and retains only discrepancies consistent with the expected ADC reduction in acute HIE, aggregated via geometric mean and spatial mean filtering. Limitations include challenges in detecting small and low-contrast lesions, as reflected by the moderate Dice score without refinement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Neonatal hypoxic-ischemic encephalopathy (HIE) is a brain injury caused by oxygen deprivation around birth, and its lesions appear as reduced signal on apparent diffusion coefficient (ADC) maps derived from diffusion-weighted MRI. Diffusion models, such as score-based models, learn to denoise data and have been adapted for anomaly detection by modeling healthy distributions; ControlNet is a conditioning architecture that preserves spatial structure in diffusion models. Unsupervised segmentation aims to identify lesions without pixel-level labels, which is valuable when expert annotations are scarce.

## [Paper Proposes Memory-Centric Continual Learning for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29357) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Memory-Centric Continual Learning (MCCL), a framework that argues agent memory should be evaluated by whether it improves future actions rather than by retrieval accuracy alone. MCCL includes a three-axis taxonomy of memory, a two-loop lifecycle for updating memory and management policies, an evaluation protocol for retention, adaptation, and transfer, and a memory scaling framework. This reframing could shift agent memory research from passive retrieval caches toward mechanisms that enable genuine continual learning, potentially leading to self-improving autonomous agents that adapt over long deployments. It affects researchers and practitioners building LLM-based agents that must learn from experience without retraining fixed model weights. The taxonomy organizes memory by substrates (where state is stored), assets (what form it takes), and policies (how it is managed), while the two-loop lifecycle updates memory in an inner loop and refines management policies in an outer loop using task feedback. The paper is a preprint with no empirical results visible in the abstract, and its evaluation protocol aims to isolate behavioral gains from simple context expansion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning is a machine learning subfield focused on enabling models to learn from new data over time without forgetting prior knowledge. Foundation-model-based agents have fixed weights and limited context windows, so external memory systems are often used, but they typically act as passive retrieval caches evaluated on recall of facts or past interactions. MCCL argues that such memory should instead be judged by its contribution to continual learning and future task performance.

## [PLRM: Parallelizable Probabilistic Latent Reasoning for Diverse Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29385) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Probabilistic Latent Reasoning Models (PLRM), a parallelizable variational framework that uses a JEPA-style objective to infer latent distributions in parallel from observed reasoning trajectories, while a prior predicts subsequent latent distributions via teacher forcing. It also adds a discrete strategy variable and a mixture-based prior to preserve the Gaussian-mixture structure induced by multiple distinct reasoning trajectories. Current latent reasoning models are largely deterministic and rely on autoregressive latent prediction, which limits them to a single reasoning path and makes training prohibitively slow. PLRM addresses both bottlenecks, enabling efficient training and better modeling of multiple valid reasoning trajectories, which could improve reasoning diversity and performance on multi-trajectory datasets. PLRM achieves performance competitive with deterministic latent reasoning models on single-trajectory datasets while consistently outperforming competing stochastic baselines on multi-trajectory datasets. The approach avoids the unstable optimization and limited empirical performance that often plague stochastic latent reasoning methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting has language models generate intermediate reasoning steps token by token in natural language, but many tokens serve textual coherence rather than core reasoning. Latent reasoning instead moves reasoning into a compact continuous latent space, bypassing the language bottleneck. However, existing latent models are mostly deterministic, capturing only a single reasoning trajectory, and stochastic variants suffer from unstable training and slow autoregressive latent prediction.

## [LLMSeg: Training-Free Text-Prompted Segmentation via B-Splines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29409) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LLMSeg introduces a training-free pipeline that repurposes a vision-capable multimodal LLM (Gemini-3.7-Flash) as a text-prompted image segmenter through a detect-segment-refine loop using closed periodic B-splines. It achieves 71.5 cIoU on ReasonSeg val and is competitive with or surpasses the specialist SAM 3 model on identical items across ten referring and reasoning segmentation splits. This work suggests that general-purpose MLLMs can perform segmentation without dedicated architectures, mask decoders, or task-specific training, potentially lowering the barrier to building promptable segmentation systems. It also implies that backend model updates can improve accuracy without retraining the pipeline, which could reshape how segmentation tools are developed and maintained. The pipeline decomposes segmentation into three LLM-driven stages: detect (localizes instances and describes coarse morphology), segment (traces closed periodic B-splines confined to detected boxes, defining outer boundary and optional hole loops), and refine (iteratively proposes focus boxes around residual errors until the mask stabilizes or the pass budget is exhausted). The output is a binary mask with inspectable intermediate geometry, and the code is available at abdullahamdi.com/llmseg.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Promptable segmentation models like SAM 3 are unified foundation models that can detect, segment, and track objects using text or visual prompts, but they require dedicated architectures and large-scale training. B-splines are spline functions built from basis functions with minimal support, and closed periodic B-splines form seamless closed curves useful for representing object boundaries. LLMSeg asks whether a general-purpose multimodal LLM can segment images directly without an added segmentation head or mask decoder.

## [TraDE: New Framework Evaluates How AI Agents Handle Disclosure Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29413) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Trajectory-based Disclosure Evaluation (TraDE), a framework grounded in Social Penetration Theory that uses predefined disclosure trajectories to isolate how preceding disclosure history shapes conversational agent responses under matched current disclosure. Across multiple models and relational behavior benchmarks, they found that responses to disclosures matched on depth can differ depending on the preceding trajectory, particularly in whether models accept or challenge the user's framing. This work addresses a real gap in evaluating conversational agents over time, moving beyond isolated conversational states to capture history-dependent relational behavior. It has high value for researchers in HCI, NLP, and affective computing who aim to build agents that provide consistent social and emotional support. The differences in model behavior track accumulated exposure to deeper disclosure rather than any single moment of vulnerability or the order of disclosures, and they tend to persist for several turns after disclosure returns to shallow levels. Models also diverge most consistently from human supporters in the Emotional Support Conversation (ESConv) corpus not when users open up but when they step back in their disclosure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Social Penetration Theory, developed by Altman and Taylor in 1973, describes how interpersonal communication moves from shallow to deeper levels of self-disclosure as relationships develop, often compared to peeling an onion. Conversational agents are increasingly used for social and emotional support, where disclosure unfolds over time, but current evaluations typically examine isolated conversational states, confounding disclosure history with current disclosure.

## [RegoMender: Multi-agent framework for automated Rego policy repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29456) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced RegoBench, the first benchmark of 1,159 real-world Rego defects, and RegoMender, a feedback-guided multi-agent framework that repairs Rego policies via a shared blackboard and atomic toolkit. On RegoBench, RegoMender achieves 82.9% syntactic and 77.9% test-based semantic success rates, outperforming the strongest baseline by 13.4%. Policy-as-Code is foundational in cloud-native systems, yet repairing Rego policies has remained a manual, trial-and-error process because existing automated program repair methods target mainstream languages. RegoMender and RegoBench address this gap, potentially reducing maintenance burden and improving security for OPA-based authorization and compliance policies. RegoMender dynamically chooses to patch, rewrite, or roll back, and unlike other approaches it maintains effectiveness across single-step and chained repair tasks; it also generalizes to HCL and SQL and achieves an 86.4% repair rate on 132 real-world CVE vulnerabilities. The work is an arXiv preprint (2610.29456) with no community discussion yet.

Policy-as-Code expresses organizational rules as executable, version-controlled code, but Rego's strict syntax and declarative semantics make automated repair difficult. ## [Harness Scaling Trains Agentic RL Models Across Diverse Execution Configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29458) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Harness Scaling, a new scaling dimension for agentic reinforcement learning that trains models across a factorized space of execution harness configurations rather than a single fixed harness. It selects a compact training subset with balanced factorial coverage and organizes cross-harness training to combine global coverage with controlled variation for the same task. This work addresses the specialization and limited transfer of single-harness training by showing that harness diversity is a meaningful scaling dimension, consistently improving generalization to unseen white-box configurations and even transferring to external black-box harnesses. It could influence how practitioners design RL training pipelines for LLM agents that must operate across varied tool-use and context-management environments. The method constructs a factorized harness space and uses a compact training subset with balanced factorial coverage, and experiments span multiple benchmarks, model scales, and RL algorithms. Notably, models trained through structured white-box variation outperform those trained through a single complex black-box harness when transferred to unseen external black-box harnesses.

Agentic reinforcement learning trains a model end-to-end through such interactions, but existing approaches typically fix a single harness during training, which can cause the model to specialize to that harness and transfer poorly to others.

## [Nonlinear Schrödinger Bridges Enable Scalable Generative Modeling via Branching Diffusions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29476) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SBNL (Schrödinger Bridge NonLinear), a nonlinear extension of the classical Schrödinger bridge problem derived from coupled forward-backward PDEs and mean-field game theory. It also presents an embarrassingly parallel branching diffusion algorithm for computing the solution, validated on tasks including MNIST and FFHQ image synthesis. This work bridges generative modeling with PDE-based methods and mean-field games, potentially enabling scalable high-dimensional generative tasks with nonlinear interactions. It could impact both the generative modeling community and researchers working on PDE-based numerical methods. The branching diffusion algorithm is embarrassingly parallel and scalable to high dimensions, and the framework embeds score-based generative modeling, diffusion models, and tractable Gaussian Schrödinger bridges. Numerical experiments include recovery of the classical Schrödinger bridge, nonlinear extensions with convex penalties (Gross-Pitaevskii) and potential interaction terms.

**Background**: The Schrödinger bridge problem seeks the most likely path measure connecting two distributions with minimal relative entropy to a reference process, and is central to diffusion-based generative models. Mean-field games describe strategic interactions in large populations via coupled Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman and Fokker-Planck equations, while branching diffusions provide probabilistic representations for semilinear PDEs. This paper combines these ideas to define a nonlinear bridge and compute it efficiently.

## [MoE Routing Statistics Enable Process Supervision for Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29498) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes using Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routing statistics as an intrinsic confidence signal for process supervision, introducing two methods: RoCo-Rethink, which identifies the least confident reasoning step and regenerates the subsequent trajectory, and RoCo-PRM, which uses a strong MoE to automatically annotate reasoning trajectories with routing-derived step scores and distills them into a standalone process reward model (PRM). Process reward models typically require expensive human annotation or rollout-based estimation, so deriving supervision directly from MoE routing offers a cheaper, architecture-native alternative that could simplify training of reasoning models and improve their multi-step accuracy. The routing confidence combines overall routing mass, head routing mass, and within-step routing variation; experiments across multiple MoE architectures and diverse reasoning tasks show both methods outperform strong baselines, and RoCo-PRM transfers effectively across model architectures and tasks.

This work shows that routing statistics themselves carry useful confidence information, turning a dispatch mechanism into a supervision source.

A new arXiv paper introduces FairAMO, a controlled evaluation protocol with frozen candidate forecasts, and shows on GIFT-Eval that agentic model orchestration (AMO) reduces MASE by less than 6% relative to the best single time series foundation model (TSFM) across all tasks. The findings challenge the assumption that agentic orchestration significantly improves forecasting, suggesting that gains are bounded by candidate TSFMs and driven by complementarity rather than ensemble size, which has implications for how researchers and practitioners design and evaluate forecasting systems. ## [Rationale Contrastive Decoding Reduces Query Shortcuts in Chain-of-Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29518) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes Rationale Contrastive Decoding (RCD), which applies context-aware decoding to Chain-of-Thought reasoning by contrasting next-token distributions with and without the rationale. Experiments on five reasoning benchmarks show RCD improves answer accuracy over standard CoT and increases the behavioral dependence of final answers on rationale content. This work addresses a known weakness in Chain-of-Thought prompting—models often rely on query-specific shortcuts rather than their generated rationales—and offers a simple, training-free decoding method that could make LLM reasoning more faithful and reliable without additional fine-tuning. RCD is training-free and operates at inference time by amplifying tokens whose likelihood increases due to the rationale, with a theoretical interpretation via a KL-regularized reward-maximization objective. Intervention analyses using ground-truth and random rationales confirm that RCD increases the causal influence of rationales on final answers.
