Agent evaluation increasingly goes beyond checking final answers to examining the full trajectory of actions an agent takes, using so-called golden trajectories as reference. ## [Survey Finds Formal Specification Is the Bottleneck for Safe LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14590) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A PRISMA 2020 systematic review of 38 studies (2022–2026) on LLM agent specification, verification, and enforcement finds that formal specification is the key bottleneck, with only 24.8–34.2% of LLM translations from natural language into PDDL being semantically correct on one benchmark. It also reports that runtime monitoring is the most mature enforcement strategy, reducing unsafe actions by 65.37% in one household-agent study, but its guarantees cover only single steps or short horizons. As LLM agents increasingly take irreversible real-world actions, the lack of formally grounded, task-level safety guarantees is a pressing risk for researchers and practitioners deploying agents in high-stakes domains. The survey's finding that no approach simultaneously achieves soundness, completeness, scalability, semantic grounding, and utility preservation highlights a fundamental gap that must be addressed before agents can be trusted with consequential tasks. For seven of the nine principal claims, GRADE assigns the lowest certainty rating (Very Low), meaning further studies are likely to produce substantially different figures, and each claim rests on only one or two studies in narrow domains. In one τ-Bench study, blocking 94% of unsafe actions still left fewer than 5% of tasks completed both safely and correctly in most settings, a phenomenon the authors call the verifier tax.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: PDDL (Planning Domain Definition Language) is a standardized formal language for AI planning that separates domain descriptions from problem instances, enabling generic planning algorithms. PRISMA 2020 is a widely adopted reporting guideline for systematic reviews, and GRADE is a framework for rating the certainty of evidence across studies. LLM agents are AI systems that use large language models to plan and execute multi-step actions, often with real-world effects.

## [LLMs Misweight Group-Linked Proxy Fields, Audit Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22887) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.22887v2) introduces an evidence-referenced audit that compares LLM proxy reliance against two references—the known outcome rule and a Bayesian linear regression fitted to the same labeled examples—while separately measuring ranking accuracy. Testing four models on a simulated patient-ranking task with known field weights, the authors find that models rely on non-predictive fields at zero predictive value, under-rely on predictive proxies as their value grows, and are influenced by display format (ordered labels draw more reliance than arbitrary labels across all four models). This work offers a concrete methodology for detecting proxy discrimination in LLM judgments, moving beyond simple decision-change audits to quantify whether reliance is warranted by evidence. It has direct implications for AI fairness evaluation and auditing practices, especially as LLMs are increasingly used to judge people in high-stakes domains like healthcare and hiring. The audit is limited to a simulated patient-ranking task with known field weights, and the authors note that ranking accuracy did not consistently track changes in reliance, meaning decision changes and accuracy alone cannot show whether proxy reliance is warranted. They recommend that audits report reliance against an explicit predictive reference alongside accuracy, starting with ordered fields whose values move with group membership such as scores or income bands.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Proxy discrimination occurs when a model relies on inputs correlated with a protected attribute (like race) even when that attribute is never shown, potentially producing biased outcomes. Traditional audits often only check whether a decision changes when a protected attribute is altered, but this does not reveal whether the model's reliance on correlated fields is justified by their predictive value. Bayesian linear regression provides a principled reference for how much weight each field should receive given the labeled data, allowing auditors to compare model behavior against an evidence-based benchmark.

## [Interface Mismatch Silently Zeroes Tool-Use Benchmark Scores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03966) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.03966v3) identifies 'interface-induced trajectory censoring,' showing that on BFCL v4 subsets, changing only the serving adapter while holding model weights fixed moves official scores from 0.00 to 0.96 on simple_python and from 0.00 to 0.19 on multi_turn_base. A template-parser factorial experiment found that neither component replacement alone restored parsing, but joint replacement recovered 196/200 first-request parses. This reveals a systematic measurement flaw in tool-use benchmarks: evaluation scores can be attributed to the model when they actually depend on the model-interface configuration, affecting anyone comparing LLMs or training agents with tool use. The authors argue for layer-wise observability and conformance checks before drawing conclusions from benchmark or training outcomes. Across five Qwen2.5-Coder checkpoints, classifier-positive emissions increased with model size while the mismatched interface reported no calls, and a matched-interface Qwen3 ladder showed few silent emissions through 8B. In a preregistered 150-step 7B LoRA experiment, changing only the parser in verl raised tool executions from 10 to 16,844, yet neither programmatic-feedback nor native function-calling evaluation detected a held-out success gain.

**Background**: Tool-use benchmarks such as BFCL v4 (Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard) measure whether a model can emit valid function calls, but the evaluation typically passes model outputs through a serving stack that parses and executes those calls. ## [Transformer World Models Cope with Asynchronous Sensor Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07299) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper studies transformer-based world models when sensor observations arrive asynchronously, proposing to zero-order-hold the most recent reading of each stale channel and to feed the model two features, staleness and time-to-refresh, that encode the known sampling schedule. The approach is evaluated across open-loop rollouts in continuous-control locomotion, closed-loop model-predictive planning where the learned model serves as planner dynamics, and a linear latched-actuator system where refresh events apply a zero-order-held command to the plant. Learned world models typically assume synchronous observations inherited from simulators, but real robots sense at heterogeneous rates, so this work addresses a realistic gap that affects model-based reinforcement learning and robotics deployment. It also clarifies when sampling schedules carry useful predictive information, which matters for designing planners that rely on learned dynamics under real sensing constraints. The key finding is that the benefit of the time-to-refresh feature depends on the causal role of the sampling schedule: it helps when refresh events actually affect the system, not merely when they report its state. The study compares zero-order holding against interpolation, which fabricates unobserved measurements, and downsampling to the slowest sensor, which discards valid data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: World models are learned predictive models of an environment's dynamics, often built on transformers and used in model-based reinforcement learning to imagine rollouts or to serve as the dynamics inside a planner. Zero-order hold is a standard signal-processing operation that reconstructs a continuous signal by holding each discrete sample until the next one arrives. Model predictive control (MPC) is a control scheme that repeatedly optimizes actions over a finite horizon using a dynamics model, so the fidelity of that model under irregular sensing directly affects control quality.

## [Auditable conditional-strategy framework boosts lung cancer decision support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29381) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A physician-authored study evaluated MedGPT Clinical Explorer (MCE), an auditable conditional-strategy framework, with 250 physicians from 98 institutions who produced 2,250 strategies for 40 reference cases. MCE-assisted strategies scored higher on the Admissible Pathway Attainment Score (APAS) than unaided strategies (adjusted difference 12.87, 95% CI 11.18–14.55) and retrieval-reference strategies (5.22, 95% CI 3.52–6.93). The results suggest that structuring clinical alternatives, decision-changing unknowns, safety constraints, and fallback plans into an explicit, auditable representation can improve the applicability of AI-assisted clinical requirements in complex lung cancer decisions. This offers a practical direction for clinical decision support that goes beyond retrieval-augmented generation by making consequential omissions and pathway contingencies visible before action. The primary outcome, APAS (0–100), measures expression of applicable case-specific clinical requirements, and physicians' whole-strategy acceptability judgments correlated with APAS (Spearman's rho = 0.671). A separate nine-item completeness-and-constraint gate (CSPR) and a complementary relationship audit assessed whether candidates, conditions, and subsequent actions were coherently connected; the study is domain-specific and prospective workflow and patient-outcome evaluations remain future work.

## [EHRAdapt adapter maps EHR tuples into frozen LLM embeddings for rare clinical events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34007) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EHRAdapt introduces an adapter that maps (time, modality, code) EHR tuples directly into a frozen language model's embedding space, representing each event vector as the sum of a frozen ontology-trained semantic prior and a learned low-rank evidence residual. The authors perform continued pretraining on records from about 4 million patients with three frozen backbones (OLMo2 1B, Llama3.2 1B, and OLMo2 7B), training only 0.1–0.6% of all parameters, and the full adapter outperforms all ablations on held-out next-event prediction for every backbone. This work shows that structured EHR data can be fed into frozen LLMs without lossy text serialization, and that separating semantic priors from learned residuals materially improves modeling of rare clinical events, which are often the most clinically important yet least observed. It is relevant to clinical machine learning teams building predictive or classification models on coded health records. Modality and time gaps enter through a learned embedding and attention biases, while event codes get dedicated vectors; removing the semantic pathway hurts rare events over ten times more than the most frequent ones, whereas removing the residual hurts overall prediction but improves the rarest events. On reportable infectious-disease and syndromic classification, EHRAdapt outperforms text-based LLM and count-based baselines and the same LLMs adapted through next-event prediction, with both pathways improving rare-disease discrimination.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Electronic health records store clinical histories as coded tuples of time, modality, and code, while pretrained language models expect text tokens, so prior approaches serialize tuples into text, which inflates sequence length and repeatedly encodes the same structure. Clinical vocabularies are long-tailed, meaning rare events have few observations, which makes learning dedicated embeddings for them difficult. Biomedical ontologies provide structured clinical descriptions that can be embedded and used as semantic priors to supply meaning even when data are scarce.

## [AdaGuard: Adaptive Guard Models with User-Defined Safety Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34241) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce AdaGuard, a family of 0.6B, 4B, and 8B guard models that detect policy violations in LLM agent trajectories under user-defined policies supplied at inference time, trained with a new reinforcement learning algorithm called SafePO on the AdaptiveSafety dataset of 10,939 training and 1,000 test examples. The 4B model achieves 89.30% binary accuracy on AdaptiveSafety and 71.82% on DynaBench. Most existing guard models rely on fixed risk taxonomies, which cannot accommodate the varying requirements of different applications and tasks; AdaGuard's policy-conditioned approach allows the same agent action to be judged differently depending on the applicable rules, making it more practical for deploying LLM agents in diverse real-world settings. The AdaptiveSafety dataset covers policies with 1 to 100 rules and pairs each example with an explanation and the complete set of violated rules, using policy and behavioral counterfactuals plus structural augmentations for rule reordering and identifier remapping. SafePO combines structured rewards, group-relative advantages at the response level, and a separately trained value model that modulates token weights in explanation versus verdict regions, with separate normalization to balance their contributions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Guard models are classifiers or moderators used to check whether prompts, responses, or agent actions violate safety rules before deployment. Traditional guardrails such as Llama Guard treat moderation as instruction-following classification against a fixed taxonomy, so they cannot easily adapt when an organization has its own policy. Counterfactual data augmentation creates minimally altered examples that flip the label, helping models learn which features actually determine compliance rather than relying on superficial cues.

## [RocketAgent: A Long-Horizon AI Agent for Multidisciplinary Rocket Thrust Chamber Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06044) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RocketAgent is a long-horizon engineering agent that coordinates multidisciplinary preliminary design of liquid-rocket thrust chambers using a plan-owning Coding Agent, a provenance-aware knowledge graph, and a typed Design Intermediate Representation (Design IR). In a representative simulation-based design, it recovered from an infeasible cooling search through an engineer-authorized operating-point revision, then identified feasible subsystem designs and coordinated geometry generation and multiphysics assessment for final configuration selection. This work demonstrates how agentic AI can manage long-horizon, interdependent engineering workflows where downstream constraints force revisiting earlier decisions, a challenge common in aerospace and other complex systems design. It suggests a path toward AI assistants that maintain design consistency across heterogeneous tools and support human-authorized revisions, potentially reducing iteration time and errors in multidisciplinary optimization. The system uses revision-aware checks to invalidate affected results and block superseded inputs, with consequential changes requiring engineering approval. Controlled tests across two configurations and three scenarios verified expected dependency invalidations and superseded-input blocking before solver execution, while separate module tests assessed surrogate predictions and nozzle adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Liquid-rocket thrust-chamber design requires tightly coupled analyses (e.g., performance sizing, cooling, geometry, multiphysics) where a change in one area can invalidate results in another, making consistent information management difficult across heterogeneous tools. Knowledge graphs organize data with provenance (source and history), and intermediate representations (IRs) are abstract data structures used in compilers and engineering pipelines to separate high-level intent from low-level execution. Long-horizon agents are AI systems designed to plan and execute extended, multi-step tasks with tool use and state tracking.

## [Potential-Based Estimators Cut Variance in Autoregressive LM Expectations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11399) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces potential-based estimators that exploit next-token conditional probabilities to reduce variance when estimating expectations under autoregressive language models. The method constructs a family of estimators parameterized by potentials—real-valued functions of prefixes that induce an additive decomposition of the test functional—and derives conditions for variance reduction, achieving substantial improvements over Monte Carlo and importance sampling baselines in entropy estimation, watermark detection, and rare-event probability estimation. Many language model applications require precise expectation estimates, but obtaining them via naive Monte Carlo is computationally expensive. This technique offers a practical way to improve estimation precision at comparable cost, which could benefit tasks like entropy-based compression, watermark detection, and rare-event analysis in deployed language models. The estimators leverage next-token conditional probabilities that are already available as a by-product of sampling, so no additional model queries are needed. The paper provides theoretical conditions for when variance reduction is guaranteed, and demonstrates substantial variance reductions relative to Monte Carlo and importance sampling baselines at comparable computational cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive language models generate text by predicting the next token conditioned on previous tokens, and many tasks require estimating the expected value of some function under the model's distribution. Monte Carlo estimation draws samples and averages the function, but its variance can be high, requiring many samples for precise estimates. Importance sampling is a variance reduction technique that draws from a different distribution and reweights, but it can be ineffective for certain functionals. Potentials are functions of prefixes that help decompose the target functional additively, enabling more efficient estimation.

## [HiPPT aligns wearable PPG with text at multiple scopes for reusable representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HiPPT introduces a physiology-grounded pretraining framework that aligns PPG waveforms with text at multiple evidence scopes, using a newly constructed PPG-Text corpus of over 4 million wrist-worn recordings from 202K users. It combines record-level morphology alignment, cross-recording context alignment, and waveform-guided text modeling, achieving the best primary linear-probing results across six downstream tasks and enabling zero-shot profiling of age group, sex, and BMI category. This work shows that matching supervision to the appropriate waveform evidence scope yields transferable and semantically accessible PPG representations, which could improve health monitoring from consumer wearables across tasks like blood pressure and vascular stiffness estimation. It also demonstrates that large-scale multimodal alignment can enable zero-shot physiological profiling without task-specific labels. HiPPT is evaluated on six downstream tasks spanning blood pressure, vascular stiffness, physiological attributes, and cross-dataset heart-rate estimation, with zero-shot performance improving as more recordings are aggregated. The authors state that code and pretrained weights will be released, though no release date or licensing details are provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Photoplethysmography (PPG) is an inexpensive optical sensing method used in wrist-worn wearables to measure pulsatile blood flow, commonly for heart rate and other vital signs. Representation learning aims to pretrain models that produce reusable embeddings transferable to many downstream tasks, and multimodal alignment—often via contrastive learning with dual encoders—maps different modalities such as waveforms and text into a shared embedding space. HiPPT applies this paradigm to PPG by pairing recordings with waveform-derived morphology and metadata-derived context descriptions.

## [MADDIE: Multi-Agent LLM Engine for Autonomous Drug Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16975) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present MADDIE, a multi-agent drug discovery engine built on large language models, comprising a central Orchestrator and four specialized worker agents: HitIdentifier, LeadOptimizer, Synthesizer, and Evaluator. Across 50 protein targets, MADDIE generated molecules satisfying key property requirements along with their synthesis routes, outperforming existing agentic systems, generative models, and general-purpose LLMs by a wide margin. This work addresses a key limitation of current LLM-based scientific agents: single-agent systems scale poorly to long-horizon tasks, while multi-agent systems typically follow rigid predefined workflows that cannot capture the iterative, non-linear nature of real drug discovery. By enabling flexible, adaptive workflows, MADDIE could reduce the cost and expertise barrier for computational drug discovery and influence how AI agents are designed for other complex scientific pipelines. MADDIE's Orchestrator dynamically composes the four worker agents into flexible workflows, allowing different strategies per target while remaining efficient in time and token usage. The evaluation spans 50 protein targets, and the code and dataset are publicly available on GitHub, though the work is an arXiv preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

**Background**: Drug discovery is notoriously resource-intensive, often requiring billions of dollars and a decade of development to bring a drug to market. LLM-based autonomous agents have emerged as a way to automate these workflows, but existing designs either struggle with long multi-step tasks or hard-code the sequence of steps in advance.

## [AssetForge synthesizes business automation tasks with reusable assets and Rubrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AssetForge introduces an asset-augmented pipeline that pairs a reusable construction-asset library with task-specific Rubrics to synthesize executable business automation tasks. On AutomationBench v1.0.6 public-600, fine-tuning Qwen3.6-35B-A3B on 6,000 AssetForge examples achieves 41.17% strict pass@1, 4.4 times the 9.33% base score, and outperforms traditional Rubrics (30.33%) by 10.84 points under GLM-5.3 supervision. This work addresses the high cost of authoring executable tasks for training business automation agents, showing that reusable assets can substantially boost data efficiency and downstream agent performance. It could lower barriers for building agent training pipelines and influence how benchmarks like AutomationBench are used for fine-tuning. The asset library provides application interfaces, world-state components, policy/evidence carriers, and native assertion definitions, while Rubrics direct their instantiation; executable checks and model-based review validate candidates with bounded repair. Gains also appear on AppWorld Test-Normal and Test-Challenge, but the abstract is truncated and full limitations are unclear.

AutomationBench is a benchmark from Zapier that evaluates agents on realistic workflows across sales, marketing, operations, support, finance, and HR using 47 real tools. ## [TIR-EC: Tool-Use RL with Tree Teacher Boosts ED Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21449) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TIR-EC is a tool-integrated reinforcement learning framework that trains LLMs to call EHR feature-group tools and calibrate risk using rewards from a gradient-boosted tree teacher, plus a reverse curriculum that distills clinical skills into model weights. On three clinical prediction tasks, the 4B agent achieves the highest macro F1 among all compared models, beating conventional ML baselines and rivaling much larger frontier LLMs. This work shows how to close the persistent gap between LLMs and tree-based models on structured EHR prediction, which could make LLM agents viable for real-time emergency department triage and risk scoring. It also demonstrates a general recipe—tool-use RL with a strong non-LLM teacher—that may transfer to other tabular-plus-text domains. The framework exposes EHR feature groups as callable tools and uses a gradient-boosted tree teacher to supply both a reference set of informative tools and a graded risk target; a reverse curriculum gradually removes task-specific clinical skills from the prompt during RL so they are internalized into weights. The reported 4B agent selects more useful evidence with fewer tool calls, though this is an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Emergency department prediction often relies on structured measurements and free-text records, where gradient-boosted decision trees (GBDTs) remain strong baselines for tabular data. LLMs can reason over both modalities but tend to underperform trees because they fail to select informative features and misjudge numerical risk. Tool-integrated reinforcement learning lets an LLM learn when and how to call external tools via reward signals, while reverse curriculum learning trains from easier goal-near states and gradually increases difficulty.

## [LOADBench: Open Dataset and Benchmarks for ML in Analog IC Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21591) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LOADBench introduces an open-source dataset of over 100 operational amplifier topologies in the SkyWater130 PDK, each with structural and functional annotations, sizing constraints, Ngspice testbenches, and NSGA-II sizing runs yielding about 10^3 Pareto-optimal and 10^6 intermediate sizing-performance pairs per topology. It also defines six standardized benchmark tasks with precise protocols, including inverse sizing, topology selection, sub-circuit classification, structural circuit generation, Pareto-membership prediction, and multi-objective optimization. This addresses a critical gap in machine learning for analog IC design, where results are typically reported on bespoke, often closed datasets with ad-hoc metrics, making comparisons unreliable. By providing a large open dataset and standardized benchmarks, LOADBench enables reproducible and directly comparable evaluation across heterogeneous analog design automation methods, potentially accelerating ML applications in analog design. The dataset covers 1-, 2-, and 3-stage opamp topologies and includes topology-specific sizing constraints (symmetry and sizing rules), with all data, ground-truth annotations, and reference implementations of metric-defining routines released under an open license. The benchmarks are designed to evaluate diverse ML tasks, from classification and generation to multi-objective optimization, using the open-source SkyWater130 PDK and Ngspice simulator.

## [OwlSight: A Panoramic Monitor for Chain-of-Thought Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23670) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OwlSight introduces a factor-based Chain-of-Thought (CoT) monitoring strategy that allows users to customize monitoring requirements via prompts, enabling a single monitor to cover multiple tasks and dimensions such as capability, safety, and trustworthiness. The paper defines fine-grained and coarse-grained factors (e.g., monitoring a specific email address leak vs. privacy leakage) and reports that OwlSight outperforms general-purpose LLMs by 1.32–30.57 and 11.71–27.81 points at fine and coarse granularity, respectively, across 15,971 reasoning trajectories, achieving state-of-the-art results on OpenAI's CoT monitorability benchmark. This work addresses a key gap in AI oversight: existing CoT monitors are task-specific, but regulating reasoning models requires monitoring a broad range of behaviors across capability, safety, and trustworthiness. By enabling a single customizable monitor, OwlSight offers a more scalable and flexible paradigm for overseeing frontier AI systems, which could be valuable for AI safety researchers and regulators. OwlSight relies solely on factors specified in the prompt to monitor behaviors, and experiments show it generalizes to unseen models' CoTs and remains robust to factor rephrasings, CoT compression, and obfuscation. The approach supports both fine-grained and coarse-grained monitoring needs, though the paper does not detail computational costs or potential limitations in highly adversarial settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) monitoring is an AI safety technique that inspects a model's intermediate reasoning steps (its chain of thought) to detect intent to misbehave, such as deception or unsafe planning, before the final output is produced. Reasoning models like OpenAI's o1 and DeepSeek-R1 generate such step-by-step reasoning, making CoT a potential oversight surface. However, monitors are often task-specific, and recent work shows reasoning models can sometimes control their CoTs to reduce monitorability, highlighting the need for more general and robust monitoring approaches.

## [HybridResearcher: 4B agent bridges structured and unstructured evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24899) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HybridResearcher introduces a 4B LLM agent trained via synthesized cross-source bridge trajectories to reason across structured (e.g., databases) and unstructured (e.g., web) evidence. On the HybridDeepResearch benchmark, it improves over its base model Qwen3.5-4B by 30.0 points pass@8 and surpasses the much larger Qwen3.5-397B-A17B by 8.3 points. Real-world analytical questions often require combining database results with web evidence, yet even frontier agents struggle across this boundary. HybridResearcher shows that this cross-source reasoning can be taught directly to small models without catastrophic forgetting, potentially making capable deep-research agents far cheaper and more deployable. The training data is built by starting from validated single-source tasks (text-to-SQL or web search) and fabricating the missing source around a bridge entity from the seed's output, retaining samples only when strict gates confirm neither side alone suffices. Training uses supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning, and the model also improves on held-out single-source benchmarks BrowseComp and BrowseComp-Plus.

## [VLA-Rolling reuses action tails to cut denoising steps in flow-matching VLA models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25043) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VLA-Rolling introduces a lightweight inference framework that reuses discarded action tails from previous action chunks and adapts denoising steps per action via confidence estimation, while keeping the underlying VLA frozen. Across π0.5, SmolVLA, X-VLA, and MolmoAct2 on LIBERO, MetaWorld, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin 2.0, it reduces inference cost in all 16 model-benchmark pairs, achieving up to 1.48× speedup and improving success rate in 14 pairs by up to 15.08 percentage points. This work addresses a real inefficiency in receding-horizon control for flow-matching VLA models, where the unexecuted action tail is typically discarded and the next chunk is regenerated from fresh noise. By showing that action tails are reusable intermediate states, it offers a practical, model-agnostic way to make robotic inference both faster and more accurate without retraining the base policy. The framework operates in three stages—action-tail inheritance, per-action confidence estimation, and confidence-guided refinement through renoising and denoising budget allocation—and the optimal denoising budget varies substantially across samples. Results are reported on four VLA models and four benchmarks, with the base VLA kept frozen throughout.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Flow-matching VLA models generate an action chunk by iteratively denoising Gaussian noise, but under receding-horizon control they typically execute only a short prefix before replanning with updated observations. The unexecuted remainder, called the action tail, is discarded, and the next chunk is regenerated from fresh noise. VLA-Rolling shows that this tail retains transferable denoising progress and can be reused to reduce the number of denoising steps needed.

## [LoGIST uses decision diagrams to make LLM reasoning logic-invariant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25402) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.25402v2) shows that even strong LLMs fail to preserve predictions across logically equivalent inputs and proposes LoGIST, a framework that maps each input to a decision diagram and encodes it as a logical embedding to condition the LLM's reasoning. Across multiple training settings, LoGIST reduces inconsistency under logical equivalence and improves generalization to unseen logical forms. This work targets a known weakness in LLM reasoning—inconsistency across logically equivalent inputs—and suggests that logical invariance is a useful training target for building more robust and generalizable reasoning models. If validated, such structure-guided approaches could improve reliability in downstream tasks that depend on predictable logical behavior. LoGIST represents each input as a decision diagram, a directed acyclic graph that captures underlying logical structure, and encodes this graph into an embedding that conditions the LLM. The abstract is truncated and no community discussion is provided, so independent validation of the reported gains is not yet available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models often appear to reason well but may rely on shallow semantic cues rather than true logical structure. A key test of logical understanding is whether a model gives the same answer to logically equivalent inputs that differ in surface form. Decision diagrams, such as binary decision diagrams, are compact directed acyclic graphs that represent Boolean functions and can make logical structure explicit. LoGIST uses such diagrams to inject explicit logical structure into the LLM's reasoning process.

## [TokenWorth: Learning Which Memories Are Worth Reusing for Resource-Constrained Lifelong Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26453) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.26453v2) from Nanjing University proposes TokenWorth, a lightweight feedback-driven memory adaptation framework that learns query-dependent memory worth from sparse execution feedback without updating the backbone model. Across three LifelongAgentBench environments and both local and API-based deployments, TokenWorth achieves the highest task success rate while using the fewest output tokens among evaluated methods under specified resource budgets. The work reframes memory selection as a resource allocation problem, showing that under tight generation, interaction, and memory budgets, existing lifelong-memory methods provide limited benefit over simple retrieval or even no-memory agents. This suggests that effective self-improvement depends less on accumulating more experience and more on learning what is worth remembering, which could influence how future agents are designed for cost-sensitive deployments. TokenWorth combines an interpretable base scorer with a residual Recursive Feature Machine (RFM) and uses the same learned value to guide both experience retrieval and bounded memory management. The code is publicly available on GitHub, and the approach is evaluated under explicit resource budgets rather than unbounded settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving agents aim to continuously improve after deployment by learning from experience, but they operate under constraints on generation (output tokens), interaction, and memory capacity. Lifelong memory methods typically retrieve past experiences to inform decisions, yet retrieving an ineffective experience can waste scarce resources and hinder downstream execution. LifelongAgentBench is a benchmark for evaluating agents that must learn and adapt over long horizons.

## [TURTLE Evolves Self-Tuning Benchmarks for Long-Horizon Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TURTLE, an Unsupervised Environment Design (UED) approach that uses proposer, solver, and feedback generator LLM agents to evolve existing tool-use tasks into harder, more diverse benchmarks. Experiments on AppWorld, τ-Bench, and AutomationBench with Gemma 4 31B-it show a 165% increase in hard tasks on AppWorld and a broader range of failure modes. Existing tool-use benchmarks are limited and hard to scale because they rely on human supervision or are too domain-specific, which bottlenecks evaluation of AI assistants. TURTLE offers a scalable, self-tuning way to generate adaptive test suites, potentially improving how agents for apps like Gmail, Venmo, and Slack are evaluated and trained. The proposer is rewarded for generating difficult yet solvable tasks based on the solver's empirical performance, while the feedback generator summarizes execution traces for the proposer to exploit. TURTLE can adapt to different environment requirements, from user simulators and dense document retrieval to multi-app interactions, and elicits more reasoning and simultaneous tool interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Unsupervised Environment Design (UED) is a reinforcement learning paradigm that automatically generates challenging environments to expose and remedy agent weaknesses, often using an adversary that proposes environments and an agent that solves them. Long-horizon tool use refers to LLM agents performing multi-step tasks across large tool ecosystems, requiring discovery of relevant tools, inference of sub-goals, and adaptation over time. Existing benchmarks like AppWorld, τ-Bench, and AutomationBench evaluate such agents but are limited in scale and diversity.

## [Differentiable Graph Implicit Solver Handles PDE Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27888) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a fully differentiable graph-based implicit solver that incorporates PDE constraints, evaluated on four tasks: forward modeling of evolutionary and static problems, inverse estimation of equation coefficients, inverse estimation of the right-hand side, and learnable graph coarsening to accelerate forward modeling. This work enables efficient PDE-constrained optimization on unstructured grids, with potential to significantly reduce the complexity of forward modeling and improve inverse problem solving in geophysics and other engineering fields where model uncertainty reduction is critical. The solver's stability and differentiability are key to reducing forward modeling complexity, and the learnable graph coarsening component allows adaptive mesh reduction while preserving essential physics; however, the abstract does not specify the numerical methods used or the scale of problems tested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Partial differential equations (PDEs) are often solved numerically on unstructured grids, which can be represented as graphs where nodes are grid points and edges denote connections. Implicit methods solve large systems of equations and are stable for stiff problems, while automatic differentiation enables gradient-based optimization through such solvers. Graph coarsening reduces graph size by merging nodes into supernodes, accelerating computations while preserving properties.

## [Deep RL Policymaker Sets Prices to Curb Negative Externalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.06060) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a deep reinforcement learning policymaker agent that computes market prices and allocations to account for negative externalities, operating in an environment of other learning agents. It demonstrates that the policymaker can tune prices for objectives like sustainability, fairness, and welfare, and is significantly more successful at maintaining resource sustainability than the market equilibrium outcome in scarce resource environments. This work shows how AI can design market interventions to address externalities such as over-appropriation of common-pool resources, which traditional competitive markets fail to price. It has implications for mechanism design, sustainability policy, and multi-agent systems where self-interested learning agents interact. The policymaker is trained via deep reinforcement learning and can balance multiple objectives, including sustainability, resource wastefulness, fairness, and buyers' and sellers' welfare. The approach is evaluated in scarce resource environments where the sustainability challenge is most acute.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Negative externalities are costs imposed on third parties that are not reflected in market prices, such as pollution or over-extraction of a shared resource. Traditional competitive markets tend toward equilibrium where supply equals demand, but this equilibrium ignores such external costs. Deep reinforcement learning combines deep neural networks with reinforcement learning, enabling agents to learn complex policies from raw input data. This paper applies deep RL to the problem of designing market interventions that account for externalities.

## [Fair Allocation Mechanisms for Strategic Agents Achieve Pure Nash Equilibria and EF1/EFX Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.08644) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proves that the Round-Robin algorithm and the cut-and-choose algorithm of Plaut and Roughgarden have pure Nash equilibria for every instance, and that all equilibria induce allocations that are EF1 (for Round-Robin) or EFX and maximin share fair (for cut-and-choose) with respect to the agents' true valuations. This resolves an open question of whether fair division mechanisms can be robust to strategic behavior while still guaranteeing fairness, which is crucial for designing truthful mechanisms in multi-agent systems and has implications for resource allocation without money. For Round-Robin, all pure Nash equilibria yield EF1 allocations; for the cut-and-choose algorithm, equilibria yield EFX and also satisfy maximin share fairness, which is not true in the non-strategic setting. A weaker version of the latter result holds for any mechanism for two agents that always has pure Nash equilibria inducing EFX allocations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Fair division of indivisible goods studies how to allocate items among agents with preferences, where fairness notions like envy-freeness (EF) are often too strong. Relaxations such as EF1 (envy-freeness up to one good) and EFX (envy-freeness up to any good) are widely used, and the existence of EFX allocations is a central open problem. This paper adds a strategic dimension: agents may misreport their valuations, so mechanisms must be designed to have equilibria that are fair.

<a id="item-213"></a>
## [OptCS: Valid FDR Control After Data-Driven Conformal Score Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17983) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Optimized Conformal Selection (OptCS), a general framework that enables valid multiple testing and finite-sample false discovery rate (FDR) control even when the conformity score is constructed in a data-driven manner using the same calibration and test data. It provides three FDR-controlling procedures: selecting a powerful score from candidates, training the score on all labeled data without sample splitting, and combining full-sample training with model selection. This work addresses a key limitation of existing conformal selection methods, which require the score function to be independent of calibration and test data, thereby restricting the use of powerful data-driven scores and limiting performance when labeled data are scarce. By allowing flexible score construction while maintaining finite-sample FDR guarantees, OptCS can improve the power of selective inference in applications such as drug discovery and large language model evaluation. OptCS introduces general conditions under which valid conformal p-values can be constructed despite substantial data reuse, and it handles complex p-value dependencies to maintain finite-sample FDR control. The framework is instantiated in three procedures that optimize models differently, and its efficacy is demonstrated via simulation studies and real applications in drug discovery and large language models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a distribution-free uncertainty quantification method that uses a conformity score computed on labeled calibration data to produce prediction sets with guaranteed coverage. Conformal selection extends this idea to select interesting instances from unlabeled data by using conformal p-values and multiple testing to control the false discovery rate (FDR), which is the expected proportion of false discoveries among selected instances. Standard conformal selection requires the score function to be fixed independently of the calibration and test data to ensure validity, but this restricts the use of data-driven score construction and can waste valuable labeled data.

## [ML-Aided NOMA Joint Source-Channel Coding for Wireless Image Transmission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03690) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2504.03690v2) introduces a machine learning-aided wireless image transmission method that merges compression and channel coding using a multi-view autoencoder, enabling non-orthogonal multiple access (NOMA) where transmitters share all channel resources simultaneously. The proposed progressive fine-tuning algorithm doubles the number of users per iteration, supporting up to 64 users with only a 2.4% increase in trainable parameters compared to a single-user model. This approach exploits interference rather than avoiding it, potentially improving spectral efficiency and capacity in multi-user wireless systems under limited bandwidth and power constraints. It outperforms existing NOMA-based methods across various datasets, metrics, and channel conditions, paving the way for more efficient and robust multi-user communication systems. The model allows signals from different users to interfere and leverages gains from NOMA, while the receiver must recover all images and associate each with its transmitter. A progressive fine-tuning algorithm maintains initial performance with orthogonalized user-specific projections, then improves through fine-tuning steps, scaling to 64 users with potential for further scaling.

**Background**: Non-orthogonal multiple access (NOMA) is a scheme where multiple users share the same time and frequency resources, typically via power-domain multiplexing, improving spectral efficiency over orthogonal multiple access (OMA). Joint source-channel coding (JSCC) combines source compression and channel coding into a single code, often using autoencoders for end-to-end learning. Multi-view autoencoders learn joint representations from multiple data modalities or views, and here they are adapted to handle multiple users' signals.

## [Theory of Mind Reasoning Improves LLM Alignment in Ultimatum Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24255) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2505.24255v3) reports that equipping LLM agents with Theory of Mind (ToM) reasoning enhances their behavioral alignment with human norms, decision-making consistency, and negotiation outcomes in the ultimatum game. The study ran 2,700 simulations across multiple LLMs, including reasoning models like o3-mini and DeepSeek-R1 Distilled Qwen 32B, and found that ToM-enhanced LLMs outperformed standard reasoning LLMs. This work offers empirical evidence that ToM reasoning can steer LLMs toward more human-aligned behavior in strategic social interactions, which is valuable for AI alignment and the design of multi-agent systems. It also highlights that different game roles may benefit from different levels of ToM reasoning, informing the development of more socially intelligent agents. The experiments initialized agents with three prosocial beliefs (Greedy, Fair, Selfless) and compared chain-of-thought with ToM reasoning of varying orders; agents almost always followed their assigned beliefs, but Greedy and Selfless agents still fell short of human norms in outcomes. Human verification found that Llama 3.3 70B produced reasoning most consistent with its actions and beliefs, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: The ultimatum game is a classic experimental economics game where a proposer suggests how to split a sum of money and a responder can accept or reject, with rejection leaving both with nothing. Theory of Mind (ToM) is the cognitive ability to attribute mental states—beliefs, intents, desires—to oneself and others, and it is considered crucial for complex social interactions. Recent research has explored whether large language models exhibit ToM capabilities, often using false-belief tasks, and this paper extends that inquiry to strategic negotiation scenarios.

## [QDRT: Quality-Diversity Framework Automates Diverse LLM Red-Teaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07121) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT), a framework that uses behavior-conditioned training and a behavioral replay buffer to automatically generate high-quality, diverse adversarial prompts. QDRT trains multiple specialized attacker models and outperforms prior methods in both diversity and attack effectiveness against open-source models like GPT-2, Llama-3, Gemma-2, Qwen2.5, and commercial models GPT-4.1 and GPT-5-Chat. This work addresses a key limitation in automated red-teaming: previous approaches often measure diversity simplistically and rely on a single attacker model, which restricts coverage of attack styles and risk categories. By generating more diverse and effective attacks, QDRT can help developers better identify vulnerabilities and support safer deployment of LLMs. QDRT achieves goal-driven diversity through behavior-conditioned training and an open-ended behavioral replay buffer, and it trains multiple specialized attackers to cover different styles and risk categories. The code is available on GitHub, and evaluations span both open-source and commercial LLMs, though the paper is an arXiv preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Red-teaming is a systematic process of probing AI models with adversarial prompts to elicit harmful responses, helping assess safety before deployment. Quality-diversity (QD) algorithms are evolutionary methods that aim to produce a diverse collection of high-quality solutions rather than a single best one, using explicit behavioral descriptors to characterize variation. QDRT applies QD principles to red-teaming by treating attack strategies as behaviors to be explored and optimized.

## [TRON neural operator reconstructs global radiation fields from sparse sensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12045) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce TRON, a spatiotemporal neural operator that reconstructs global radiation fields from sparse sensor histories, achieving 0.096% mean relative L2 error against EXPACS reference fields and ~1 ms inference versus 57.6 s for EXPACS. This work establishes proxy-to-field reconstruction as a general and efficient route to recovering spatially distributed physical information beyond the direct reach of sparse sensor networks, with potential applications in geosciences, environmental monitoring, and beyond. TRON reconstructs sea-level effective dose at 65,341 locations from histories of 12 neutron monitors, and also reconstructs temperature at held-out weather stations from thermometer or barometer histories, outperforming interpolation, calendar-based, and hypsometric baselines.

**Background**: Neural operators are deep learning architectures that learn mappings between infinite-dimensional function spaces, enabling fast surrogate models for partial differential equations. EXPACS is a program for calculating atmospheric cosmic-ray spectra and doses, while neutron monitors are ground-based detectors that measure cosmic-ray flux. TRON leverages these concepts to infer unobserved fields from sparse, indirect sensor data.

The paper introduces AgriVLN, a vision-and-language navigation method for agricultural robots, along with the A2A benchmark comprising 1,560 episodes across 6 classes of agricultural scenes. It also proposes an STL module that decomposes long instructions into subtasks, improving success rate from 0.33 to 0.47 on the A2A benchmark when integrated with the NavGPT backbone. It could enable more autonomous and flexible agricultural robots, benefiting precision agriculture and reducing labor dependency. The A2A benchmark uses a step-by-step instruction format and a continuous environment for actions, and evaluations show existing state-of-the-art VLN methods perform well on short instructions but poorly on long ones. The STL module mitigates this by decomposing complex instructions into simple subtasks, though it slightly increases navigation error from 2.76 m to 2.91 m.

A new study (arXiv:2508.14936v4) replicated statistical analyses from six epidemiological publications using synthetic data generated by adversarial random forests (ARF), finding that results consistently aligned with original findings while preserving privacy. ## [Hyperdimensional Probe Unifies LLM Interpretability via Vector Symbolic Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25045) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Hyperdimensional Probe, a hybrid supervised probe that combines symbolic representations with neural probing using Vector Symbolic Architectures (VSAs) and hypervector algebra. It unifies top-down interpretability of supervised probes, SAE's sparsity-driven proxy space, and output-oriented logit investigation, enabling deeper input-focused feature extraction while supporting output-oriented analysis. This work addresses a key limitation in LLM interpretability by integrating input-oriented and output-oriented perspectives, potentially advancing mechanistic interpretability and providing a more holistic understanding of LLM representations. It could impact researchers and practitioners seeking to decode internal model semantics across different LLMs and configurations. The approach overcomes limitations of logit-based analyses constrained by token vocabulary and mitigates noisier interpretability outcomes from SAEs in bounded conceptual feature spaces. Experiments demonstrate consistent extraction of meaningful semantic information across different LLMs, embedding sizes, and configurations in input-completion and QA-focused text generation scenarios.

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are opaque, and understanding their internal representations is a key challenge. Current interpretability methods include supervised probes that extract features from hidden states, Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) that decompose activations into interpretable components, and logit-based analysis that inspects output distributions. ## [AFFORD2ACT: Sparse Keypoints for Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01433) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AFFORD2ACT introduces a method that uses only 19 2D keypoints (38 coordinates) as visual input for robot manipulation policies, guided by an off-the-shelf language-conditioned affordance model. Across six real-robot tasks, this compact representation outperforms RGB, RGB-D, and dense-affordance behavior cloning baselines, achieving 82% average success in held-out settings. This work challenges the assumption that dense visual features are necessary for effective manipulation policies, suggesting that compact keypoint-based representations can be more efficient and robust. It could influence future policy learning by reducing reliance on high-resolution images and enabling lightweight, real-time robotic control. The policy uses up to 15 object keypoints selected from a task-relevant region identified by the affordance model, plus 4 end-effector keypoints, with a lightweight gating mechanism deciding which keypoints to attend to. Replacing learned gating with uniform weights drops success from 92% to 52%, and the method remains vulnerable to incorrect masks, occlusion, tracking drift, and distractors.

Affordance models identify task-relevant regions in an image, and sparse 2D keypoints provide a compact representation that gives spatial information while removing appearance, texture, and background. AFFORD2ACT investigates how much manipulation capability can be retained under such a representation constraint.

<a id="item-222"></a>
## [LLM rewriting and N-gram indexing defend de-identified documents from search-based linkage attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2510.06383v3) proposes a two-step method that combines an inverted N-gram index with an LLM-based rewriter to prevent search-based linkage attacks on de-identified documents. The approach iteratively reformulates spans containing rare N-grams until they no longer appear in fewer than k documents, and experiments on court cases and Wikipedia biographies show it effectively blocks linkages while preserving semantic fidelity. De-identification alone does not prevent an adversary from mapping a de-identified text back to its source via search, so this work addresses a critical gap in privacy-preserving text publishing. It offers a practical defense for organizations releasing sensitive documents such as legal records or biographies, though the authors caution that more advanced semantics-oriented attacks can still succeed. The method builds an inverted index of N-grams to efficiently identify which N-grams occur in fewer than k documents, either alone or in combination, and then queries an LLM rewriter iteratively on those spans. While the rewriting prevents search-based linkages on two datasets, the paper explicitly notes that linkages remain feasible with more advanced, semantics-oriented approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: De-identification models aim to hide the identities of individuals mentioned in a document, but they do not address linkage risks—the possibility of mapping the de-identified text back to its original source. A simple linkage attack extracts phrases from the de-identified document and checks whether they appear in the original dataset. An inverted index is a data structure that maps terms (here, N-grams) to the documents containing them, enabling fast search. LLM-based rewriting uses a large language model to paraphrase text, and prior work has explored such rewriting for privacy-preserving text transformation.

## [WAE-MMD with Programmable Priors Achieves State-of-the-Art Disentanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11953) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper provides quantitative and visual evidence that VAEs and adversarial training fail to enforce factorized aggregate posteriors, introduces the Latent Predictability Score (LPS) to measure entanglement, and proposes using Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) to sculpt the latent space with programmable priors, achieving a 70% reduction in entanglement compared to leading baselines. This work challenges the dominance of VAEs and adversarial methods in disentangled representation learning and offers a non-parametric alternative that achieves state-of-the-art unsupervised statistical independence, potentially impacting generative modeling and representation learning research. The method optionally injects semi-supervised information for explicit semantic alignment and enables precise shaping of the aggregate posterior distribution, validated through quantization-aware training, without sacrificing reconstruction quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Disentangled representation learning aims to capture independent semantic factors in a latent space, typically using Variational Autoencoders (VAEs) or adversarial training. Wasserstein Autoencoders (WAEs) with Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) offer an alternative regularization that directly matches the encoded distribution to a prior using a kernel-based distance, avoiding adversarial training.

## [Operon: Incremental Construction of Ragged Data via Named Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16080) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Operon introduces a statically verifiable workflow engine that treats ragged data as flat functions over named dimensions, resolving shape ambiguity and synchronization issues. The system, implemented in Rust, uses a DSL where users annotate data flow with dimension names, and a static checker verifies task iterations regardless of runtime lengths. This approach could significantly improve data processing systems by enabling precise dependency tracking and fault recovery without programmer intervention, impacting domains like scientific computing where ragged data is common. The formal foundation may influence future workflow engine designs. Operon models in-progress arrays as partial functions over downward-closed coordinate domains, providing exact per-task indegree before all upstream coordinates are enumerated. The static checker ensures no faulty iterations occur, and the runtime schedules tasks dynamically based on data states.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Ragged data refers to multidimensional collections whose inner lengths vary and are only known at runtime, common in scientific computing. Traditional workflow engines treat such data as nested containers, leading to ambiguous shape semantics and coarse synchronization barriers. Operon proposes a formalism of named dimensions with explicit dependencies to address these issues.

## [Orthogonal Supervision Disentanglement Boosts AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06746) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Orthogonal Supervision Disentanglement, a method that separates semantic and artifact supervision signals, and instantiates it in AlignGemini, a two-branch detector where a VLM receives pure semantic supervision and a vision expert receives pure artifact supervision. Trained on images from only two generators, AlignGemini achieves a 9.5% improvement across five in-the-wild benchmarks without complex training strategies. This work shifts focus from model architecture to supervision structure, showing that disentangled orthogonal supervision is key to cross-generator generalization in AI-generated image detection. It could lead to more robust detectors that work across unseen generators, benefiting platforms and security applications combating synthetic media. The authors also introduce AIGI-Now, a diagnostic benchmark covering eight contemporary generators including five closed-source commercial models, with disentangled semantic and pixel evaluation subsets. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

Vision-language models (VLMs) can provide semantic understanding, while artifact-based experts focus on low-level pixel cues; combining them with properly structured supervision is the novel angle here.

## [Evolutionary BP+OSD Decoder Achieves Low-Latency Quantum Error Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2512.18273v3) proposes an evolutionary belief propagation (EBP) decoder optimized via differential evolution (DE) to improve the BP+OSD decoding pipeline for quantum error correction. The method introduces trainable weights into BP and a multi-objective selection rule to suppress frequent OSD activation, yielding lower complexity and better performance on surface codes and QLDPC codes. Quantum error correction is essential for fault-tolerant quantum computing, and decoding must balance performance, complexity, and latency. This work addresses the excessive iterations of BP and high complexity of OSD, potentially enabling more practical real-time decoding for surface codes and emerging QLDPC codes. The DE algorithm is gradient-free, allowing end-to-end optimization of the EBP+OSD structure, and the multi-objective selection rule significantly reduces OSD overhead. Simulations show that EBP+OSD simultaneously achieves superior decoding performance and substantially lower complexity than conventional BP+OSD, especially in stringent low-latency regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum error correction protects fragile quantum information by encoding logical qubits into many physical qubits, and decoding is the classical process of inferring errors from syndrome measurements. Belief propagation (BP) combined with ordered statistics decoding (OSD) is a standard decoder for quantum low-density parity-check (QLDPC) codes, but BP can require many iterations and OSD is computationally expensive. Differential evolution is a gradient-free heuristic optimizer that iteratively improves a population of candidate solutions, making it suitable for tuning decoder parameters.

## [RubRIX: Clinician-Validated Rubric Cuts Caregiving AI Risks by 45-98%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13235) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced RubRIX (Rubric-based Risk Index), a theory-driven, clinician-validated framework that identifies five risk dimensions in LLM responses to caregiver queries: Inattention, Bias & Stigma, Information Inaccuracy, Uncritical Affirmation, and Epistemic Arrogance. Evaluating six state-of-the-art LLMs on over 20,000 caregiver queries from Reddit and ALZConnected, they found that rubric-guided refinement reduced risk components by 45-98% after a single iteration. Existing AI safety evaluations focus on general harms like toxicity and hallucination, but caregiving contexts involve vulnerable users with complex emotional and informational needs that require domain-sensitive risk assessment. This work provides a validated methodology and benchmark datasets that could inform safer deployment of LLMs in healthcare support settings. The framework is grounded in the Ethics of Care theory and operationalizes risk dimensions derived empirically from caregiver queries. The evaluation spans six LLMs and over 20,000 queries, and the authors release benchmark datasets to enable future research on contextual risk evaluation.

Epistemic arrogance refers to unwarranted confidence in the completeness or accuracy of AI outputs, a risk that can be particularly harmful when caregivers rely on AI for sensitive information. ALZConnected is an online community for people affected by Alzheimer's and their caregivers, providing real-world queries for evaluation.

## [Halo Architecture Proposes Exact Rational Arithmetic for Stable LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18702) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2601.18702v5) introduces the Halo Architecture, which replaces floating-point arithmetic in LLMs with exact rational arithmetic powered by a custom Exact Inference Unit (EIU). To manage the exponential bit-width growth inherent in rational arithmetic, Halo employs a Dual-Ring Topology combining Diophantine Approximation for memory bounding and periodic state collapse for logical consistency. This work challenges the fundamental floating-point substrate of modern LLMs, suggesting that eliminating numerical instability could allow for the removal of heuristic scaffolding like Pre-RMSNorm and gradient clipping, potentially reducing total time-to-convergence by orders of magnitude. If feasible, it could reshape LLM architecture design and training efficiency. The architecture claims an 'Efficiency Paradox' where eliminating gradient noise (sigma -> 0) enables Macro-Learning Rates, but practical feasibility remains unproven as the preprint lacks empirical results and the EIU hardware is not yet realized. The Dual-Ring Topology's Micro-Ring uses Diophantine Approximation to bound memory, while the Macro-Ring enforces symbolic alignment via periodic state collapse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Modern LLMs rely on floating-point arithmetic, which introduces numerical instability mitigated by heuristics like Pre-RMSNorm, residual connections, and gradient clipping. Rational arithmetic offers exact computation but suffers from exponential bit-width growth, making it impractical for large-scale models. Diophantine Approximation, a number theory concept, deals with approximating real numbers by rationals and is used here to control memory complexity.

## [LOFT: One-Shot Machine Unlearning in Low-Dimensional Feature Subspace](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22456) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose LOFT, a machine unlearning method that operates in a low-dimensional feature subspace by optimizing a small projection matrix to separate remaining and forgetting data. The method requires only a one-shot feature fetch from the pretrained model and avoids repeated raw-data access or updates to the entire model. This approach could significantly reduce the computational cost of machine unlearning, making it more practical for privacy compliance and model editing at scale. It offers a simple, one-shot alternative to existing methods that require full model retraining or iterative updates. LOFT optimizes a projection matrix that maximally captures remaining data information while diminishing forgetting data information, and this matrix can be flexibly inserted into the pretrained model. Experiments show lower computational overhead and superior unlearning performance across diverse models, datasets, and tasks, with code publicly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training data from a pretrained model without retraining from scratch, which is important for privacy regulations like the right to be forgotten. Low-dimensional feature subspaces are representations that capture the most important variations in data, and operating in such subspaces can make computations more efficient. LOFT leverages these concepts to perform unlearning by adjusting a small projection rather than the full model.

## [CORE Method Manipulates LLM Search Rankings via Content Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03608) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CORE, a method that appends strategically designed optimization content (string-based, reasoning-based, or review-based) to retrieved content to control output rankings in LLM-based search engines. Experiments on GPT-4o, Gemini-2.5, Claude-4, and Grok-3 show an average Promotion Success Rate of 91.4% at Top-5, 86.6% at Top-3, and 80.3% at Top-1 across 15 product categories, using a new benchmark called ProductBench. This work highlights a vulnerability in LLM-based search: recommendations are strongly biased by initial retrieval order, disadvantaging small businesses and independent creators. CORE demonstrates that this bias can be exploited to manipulate rankings, raising concerns about fairness, transparency, and the need for defenses in generative engines. CORE treats the LLM as a black box and optimizes only the retrieved content, preserving fluency. The ProductBench benchmark includes 15 product categories with 200 products each, and top-10 recommendations collected from Amazon's search interface, enabling realistic evaluation.

**Background**: Generative engine optimization (GEO) is the practice of improving visibility in responses generated by AI systems like ChatGPT, Gemini, and Perplexity. LLM-based search, or generative engines, provides direct product recommendations instead of traditional search results, but the initial retrieval order heavily influences the final output. CORE is a GEO technique that manipulates this process by modifying retrieved content.

## [RuleSmith: Multi-Agent LLMs Automate Game Balancing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06232) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RuleSmith is a framework that combines multi-agent LLM self-play with surrogate-based optimization to automatically tune game parameters. Demonstrated on CivMini, a simplified civilization-style game with 12 tunable parameters, it consistently finds near-balanced configurations in independent runs. This approach could significantly reduce manual playtesting and expert intuition required for game balancing, potentially speeding up game development and enabling dynamic difficulty adjustment. It also showcases LLMs as effective agents in complex optimization loops. The primary implementation uses Gaussian-process Bayesian optimization with Expected-Improvement-based adaptive sampling, and experiments with NTBEA and TPE show the self-play interface supports different search components. Cross-model evaluations and small human studies provide preliminary evidence of transfer across player populations.

**Background**: Game balancing involves tuning game rules to ensure fair and engaging play, traditionally done through extensive playtesting and expert intuition. Bayesian optimization is a technique for optimizing expensive-to-evaluate functions by building a surrogate model, often a Gaussian process, and using it to guide sampling. Expected Improvement is an acquisition function that balances exploration and exploitation by quantifying the expected gain from sampling a point.

## [Information Theory Reveals Limits of Chain-of-Thought Monitorability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18297) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.18297v2) uses information-theoretic analysis to show that non-zero mutual information between chain-of-thought (CoT) and output is necessary but not sufficient for effective CoT monitoring. The authors identify two approximation errors—information gap and elicitation error—and demonstrate that CoT obfuscation can occur even without optimization pressure against CoT, while proposing a label-free training objective to mitigate this fragility. This work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for understanding when and why CoT monitoring fails, which is critical for AI safety strategies that rely on monitoring reasoning traces to detect misbehavior such as test-hacking or reward hacking. It also offers a practical, label-free method to improve monitorability, potentially making CoT oversight more robust in real-world deployments. The paper distinguishes information gap (the monitor's inability to extract all available information from the CoT) from elicitation error (the monitor's approximation of the optimal monitoring function), and shows that a label-free objective based on mutual information between outputs and CoTs can prevent CoT obfuscation even when training against a monitor, while also helping mitigate reward hacking under imperfect task rewards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring is an AI safety technique where a separate LLM-based monitor analyzes a model's step-by-step reasoning to detect undesirable behaviors, such as test-hacking during code generation. Mutual information, a concept from information theory, quantifies the statistical dependence between two variables—here, the CoT and the final output. Previous work has shown that CoT monitorability is fragile and can degrade under certain training procedures, but this paper provides a formal information-theoretic framework to analyze the sources of this fragility.

## [MICA: Critic-Free RL with Multi-Granularity Credit Assignment for Emotional Support Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06194) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose MICA, a critic-free reinforcement learning framework that assigns both immediate and delayed credit to each turn in multi-turn emotional support dialogues by deriving signals from a shared potential function over the user's structured support state. On EMPA, EQ-Bench, and EmoBench with Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Qwen3-8B/14B/32B, MICA consistently outperforms GRPO and REINFORCE++, achieving up to +42.5 on EMPA without adding rollout cost. Extending RL from single-turn to multi-turn interaction is a key bottleneck for building interactive LLMs, and MICA shows that turn-aware credit assignment can make multi-turn RL both effective and practical without a learned critic or matched-state comparisons. This could benefit dialogue systems research, especially emotional support agents where responses shape future user states. MICA introduces an Incremental Distance Reward measuring per-turn decrease in residual distance to the target state, whose Monte Carlo return captures delayed effects; after scope-specific normalization, the two signals form a mixed advantage for stable per-turn optimization. The method requires no rollout trees, no learned critic, and remains robust to reward judges, though evaluation is limited to emotional support benchmarks and Qwen model families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for LLMs has worked well in single-turn tasks, but multi-turn settings suffer from sparse rewards and the credit assignment problem: when a reward arrives at the end of a long interaction, it is hard to determine which earlier turns deserve credit. Temporal credit assignment is a long-standing challenge in deep RL, and Monte Carlo returns estimate delayed effects by averaging complete episode returns. Emotional support dialogue is especially hard because each response changes the user's future state, so there is no matched-state step-wise comparison available.

## [MCLR fine-tuning objective improves guidance-free conditional diffusion generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.22364) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce MCLR, a fine-tuning objective that maximizes inter-class likelihood-ratios to strengthen class-specific structures in diffusion models, substantially improving guidance-free conditional generation and narrowing the gap to classifier-free guidance (CFG). They also prove that the CFG-guided score is the unique minimizer of a sample-adaptively weighted, ELBO-approximated MCLR objective, interpreting CFG as an implicit inference-time contrastive alignment procedure. This work addresses a known limitation of CFG—its necessity as an inference-time intervention—by providing a principled training-time alternative that could reduce reliance on guidance tuning and improve sample quality in conditional diffusion models. It also unifies CFG with alignment objectives like DPO, potentially bridging fragmented literature and influencing future generative modeling research. MCLR is a fine-tuning objective that explicitly maximizes inter-class likelihood-ratios, and the theoretical analysis shows CFG corresponds to an implicit contrastive alignment procedure; the paper also compares MCLR with DPO, CCA, and CFM, clarifying connections and differences. The approach is evaluated across diverse models and datasets, though specific benchmarks and quantitative results are not detailed in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by learning to reverse a noise-adding process, typically trained with denoising score matching (DSM) to estimate the score function (gradient of log-density). Classifier-free guidance (CFG) is a widely used inference-time technique that modifies the sampling trajectory to improve sample quality and condition adherence, but it requires tuning and can distort distributions. MCLR argues that standard DSM yields insufficient inter-class separation, motivating a training-time fix.
