## [LaVGuard: Selective Decoding to Curb Contextual Value Drift in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29135) ⭐️ 6.0/10

LaVGuard introduces a selective decoding method that uses an authorized ten-dimensional value profile and a profile-relative density gate to decide when to intervene in LLM generation, applying a logit tilt constrained by changed-position and cumulative-KL budgets. It reports raising boundary TPR at 5% FPR from 0.58 to 0.72 and achieving a risk-safety score of 0.88 versus 0.72 for position-matched edits at the same 0.12 mean intervention ratio. This work reframes value alignment as a bounded, task-relative decoding problem, making it possible to steer model outputs away from value drift while explicitly limiting how much the response distribution changes. Such guarantees could matter for safety-critical deployments where content-safe but value-conflicting responses are unacceptable. The method separates a frozen estimate of values expressed in the visible prefix from the authorized profile, and its logit tilt is constrained by both changed-position and cumulative-KL budgets, with formal results on local surrogate improvement, pathwise budget feasibility, and a bound on response-distribution change. The evaluation focuses on boundary detection and risk-safety scores rather than broad downstream benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Decoding-time interventions modify token probabilities during generation to steer LLM behavior without retraining, and they are often categorized as contrastive, guided, or parallel decoding. Logit tilting adjusts the logits (pre-softmax scores) that determine next-token probabilities, while KL divergence measures how much one probability distribution differs from another and is commonly used as a budget to bound distribution shift. Contextual value drift refers to responses that are content-safe but still promote priorities conflicting with the task, such as an assistant demanding compliance when asked to preserve autonomy.

## [Decision Models Misallocate Probability When Multiple Choices Are Correct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29217) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates three decision models (Jev, Liquid d1, and GPT-6 Luna Decisions) on three datasets (MetaTool, HotpotQA, and ECtHR) and finds that in 28% to 50% of lists where all correct choices outrank distractors, at least one correct choice receives a probability below the uniform 1/n. When given five identical copies of a correct choice, the models assign highly unequal probabilities, with mean gaps of 17 to 79 percentage points. This reveals a calibration failure that affects downstream applications relying on probability thresholds to select multiple correct answers, such as tool routing or multi-label classification. Practitioners using decision models cannot assume that a probability threshold reliably identifies the set of correct choices, even when ranking is perfect. The study shows that decision-model probabilities can rank correct choices above distractors, but a probability threshold does not identify the full set of correct choices. The disparity persists across three diverse datasets and three models, indicating a systemic issue rather than a model-specific quirk.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Decision models like Jev and Liquid d1 are designed to map unstructured input to normalized probability distributions over a finite set of choices, enabling downstream tasks such as tool selection and request routing. Providers often emphasize the reliability of these probabilities, but calibration is typically validated on tasks with a single correct answer. This paper examines whether those probabilities remain reliable when multiple choices are correct, a scenario common in multi-label settings.

## [Exact MILP Decoding Outperforms Local Repair for Safe Medication Sets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29228) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper compares local repair heuristics with an exact global decoder using mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) for safety-constrained medication recommendation, holding FLAME's frozen classifier scores, output size, and drug-drug interaction (DDI) constraints fixed on MIMIC-III. Across validation and test splits, global decoding consistently achieves higher Jaccard similarity, reaching +0.0064 over the strongest local baseline at the strict DDI operating point. The results show that medication recommendation is not only a representation-learning problem: even with a frozen clinical model, how scores are converted into a constrained medication set can materially affect final predictions. This suggests that post-hoc constrained decoding deserves more attention as a complementary lever for improving safety and accuracy in healthcare AI systems. The advantage of global decoding is small under loose DDI constraints but grows as constraints tighten; at the strict setting it retains more true-positive medications while removing more false positives. The finding holds across a fine accuracy–DDI sweep at matched realized DDI exposure and under three score representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Medication recommendation systems often use generative models to propose drug sets, but drug-drug interaction (DDI) constraints apply to the set as a whole, not individual drugs. A common post-hoc approach is to predict medications first and then locally repair interacting pairs by removing one drug at a time. Mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) is an optimization technique that can jointly select the highest-scoring feasible set under such constraints, providing an exact global solution rather than a heuristic repair.

## [LLM as Exogenous World Model Boosts Dynamic Scheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29308) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29308) proposes using a pretrained LLM as the exogenous component of a structured world model to forecast uncertain future job arrivals in dynamic scheduling, via an afterstate decomposition and Afterstate World-Model Planning (AWMP). This approach reduces weighted tardiness by 6-21% on dynamic job-shop and 22-46% on flexible job-shop benchmarks compared to the best of eight classical priority-dispatching rules, showing that pretrained LLMs can effectively handle uncertainty in scheduling and potentially other sequential decision-making domains. The afterstate decomposition separates known deterministic shop transitions from uncertain exogenous job arrivals, allowing the LLM to forecast only the latter via a compact belief representation; a surprise-triggered refresh queries the LLM only when observed arrivals deviate sufficiently, and online residual self-calibration conditions the LLM on recent forecast errors to handle nonstationary arrivals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Dynamic scheduling involves dispatching jobs as they arrive while anticipating future arrivals, and poor decisions can compound downstream costs under nonlinear tardiness objectives. World models are learned representations of an environment's dynamics that enable planning by simulating future outcomes. Afterstate decomposition is a technique from reinforcement learning that separates the deterministic effects of an action from subsequent stochastic events, simplifying value estimation. This paper combines these ideas by using an LLM to model only the stochastic exogenous arrivals, leveraging its pretrained knowledge to forecast uncertain events.

## [AgentMonopoly Tests LLM Agent Behavior and Persona Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29330) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces AgentMonopoly, a Monopoly-based environment for comparing LLM agents from four model families at two sizes, finding that larger models do not consistently win more games and that persona instructions only partially shape agent actions. The findings suggest that scaling model size alone may not improve strategic performance in multi-agent settings, and that persona following should be evaluated through actions and outcomes rather than just language, which is important for designing reliable agent systems. The study used five seeds per configuration and tested identical models, different sizes of the same family, and different families; the authors note that results are conditional on the tested checkpoints and prompts, and they have open-sourced the code.

AgentMonopoly provides a controlled game environment to study these questions.

## [MERP: Latent-Bottleneck Perceiver for Robust Multimodal Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29430) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MERP introduces a latent-bottleneck Perceiver framework that maps joint multimodal sequences into a fixed-size latent bank, preserving emotion-specific evidence from any available subset of text, visual, and acoustic streams. Under 20% test-time random modality drop, it achieves micro-F1 of 0.563 on CMU-MOSEI and 0.780 on M3ED, and retains 0.473 micro-F1 in the severe audio-only CMU-MOSEI setting versus 0.022 for LDDU. This work addresses the practical train-test modality gap in multimodal emotion recognition, where models trained on complete streams degrade sharply when some modalities are missing at deployment. The approach enables robust affect-aware systems without requiring missing-modality examples during training, which is important for real-world applications with unreliable sensors or privacy constraints. MERP extracts label-specific evidence before and after latent reasoning and aligns the two levels through reconstruction, and further uses positive/negative class prototypes, a noise-gated feature enhancer with frequency-shaped perturbations during training, and confidence-aware class-wise fusion. The fixed-size latent bank keeps repeated reasoning independent of raw input length, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: The Perceiver architecture uses a small set of latent units as an attention bottleneck through which inputs must pass, eliminating the quadratic scaling problem of early transformers by running a normal transformer stack in latent space. Multimodal emotion recognition combines text, visual, and acoustic signals to detect multiple emotions, but real-world deployments often lack some modalities, creating a train-test gap. Class prototypes are representative feature vectors for each emotion label that help improve separability in multi-label settings.

## [Paired Instruction Dependence Diagnostic Tested for VLA Corpus Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29646) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29646) evaluates whether a diagnostic called 'paired instruction dependence'—the rate at which a demonstrator changes its first decision when only the goal changes—can rank robot demonstration corpora by downstream policy quality. Across 136 independently seeded quadruped-navigation corpora, predictions from 56 corpora were tested prospectively on 80 held-out corpora, showing the diagnostic is reliable and associated with correctness but not establishing an incremental selection gain over a correctness-aware baseline. This work addresses data curation for vision-language-action (VLA) models, where selecting high-quality demonstration corpora is critical for training effective robot policies. Its rigorous prospective evaluation and honest reporting of a null result highlight the gap between predictive diagnostics and actual decision value, which could guide more careful validation of data-selection methods in robot learning. The primary proper-log-score increment was +0.059 nats with a 95% corpus-bootstrap confidence interval of [-0.027, 0.133], and the prespecified top-20% comparison did not establish an incremental selection gain. An exploratory decomposition suggests that separating both-correct and both-wrong responses yields larger held-out gains than the unsigned switch statistic, indicating that compressing counterfactual response structure into a single unsigned number may discard outcome-relevant information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take visual input and language instructions and directly output low-level robot actions. Training these models requires large corpora of robot demonstrations, and choosing among candidate corpora is a ranking problem: an upstream diagnostic should preserve which corpora yield better downstream policies, not merely correlate with average performance. Proper log score (measured in nats) is a standard metric for evaluating probabilistic predictions, where higher values indicate better predictive density.

## [COMET: Multi-layer runtime monitoring for human-supervised AI safety systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29816) ⭐️ 6.0/10

COMET is a coordinated multi-layer runtime monitoring framework that combines signal integrity, physics consistency, and AI competence-envelope monitors, and maps their outputs to a traffic-light display for human supervisors. Evaluated on a compressed-air leak dataset, COMET reduced the trusted-wrong-answer rate to zero on covariate-shifted no-leak data and on three of four sensor faults at a nominal false-alarm rate of about 1%. The framework addresses the problem of silent and confident failures in AI-enabled safety systems by converting anonymous detection flags from single monitors into actionable diagnoses for human supervisors. This is significant for safety-critical domains such as industrial monitoring and autonomous systems, where human oversight is essential but often lacks interpretable information. All monitors are frozen after development and tested zero-shot, and COMET is compared against no-monitoring and single-monitor setups. While single monitors come close in detection, they cannot attribute the fault mode; residual silent misses under semantic shift define the boundary of passive monitoring.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime monitoring is a technique for continuously checking a system's behavior during operation to detect faults or safety violations. In AI-enabled safety systems, monitors can watch different aspects such as sensor signal quality, consistency with physical laws, and whether the AI is operating within its competence envelope—the range of inputs it has been validated on. Covariate shift and semantic shift are types of distribution change between training and deployment data that can degrade AI performance, and sensor faults are hardware failures that produce incorrect readings.

## [TIDE: Tree-Based Diagnostic Evaluation for Fine-Grained LLM Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29985) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TIDE introduces a behavior-driven decision-tree induction method that recursively builds a hierarchical capability tree from benchmark samples and model scores, using an LLM judge to propose splits and behavior-based information gain to select them. Applied to five models across four benchmarks, it revealed fine-grained contrasts hidden by aggregate scores, such as Claude Opus 4.6 scoring 100% on microeconomic market reasoning versus GPT-5.4's 83.3%, while trailing on contract law reasoning at 77.8% versus 88.9%. Aggregate benchmark scores provide limited insight into a model's underlying capabilities, and TIDE offers a systematic way to diagnose strengths and weaknesses at a finer granularity, which could inform model development and evaluation. This approach may help researchers and practitioners better understand trade-offs between models that appear similar on headline metrics. TIDE relies on an LLM judge to propose candidate splits and classify samples into groups, with behavior-based information gain selecting among them, but its effectiveness depends on the judge's quality and may introduce bias. The study covers five models and four benchmarks, so generalizability to other domains and benchmarks remains to be validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Decision tree induction is a supervised learning approach that recursively splits data based on criteria such as information gain, which measures the reduction in entropy after a split. LLM-as-a-judge is a common evaluation technique where a large language model scores or assesses outputs, often guided by rubrics. TIDE combines these ideas to build a diagnostic tree from benchmark results, moving beyond single aggregate scores like accuracy or pass rate.

## [GuideEvolve: Self-Evolving Agent Framework with Expert Guidance and 3Ts Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30017) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GuideEvolve introduces a self-evolving agent framework that combines a task-specific expert-evaluation-driven evolutionary pipeline with three autonomous regulatory mechanisms called 3Ts: Think Fast and Slow, Throwback, and Termination by choice. On MGSM and MMLU benchmarks, it achieves 89.8% and 89.1% accuracy respectively, comparable to Gödel Agent's 90.6% and 87.9%, while reducing variance by 25-59% and cutting optimization iterations by about 50%. This work addresses key limitations of existing self-evolving agents by adding expert guidance and regulatory mechanisms that improve stability and reduce variance, which could make such agents more reliable for production deployment. The reduced iteration count and higher evolution success rate (100% vs. 82%) also suggest gains in efficiency and robustness. The framework achieves a 100% evolution success rate compared to the baseline's 82%, and reduces required optimization iterations from 30 to 15.1 and 13.4 across tasks. However, the abstract is truncated and the performance gains over state-of-the-art are modest, with MGSM accuracy slightly lower than Gödel Agent's.

## [Controlled Tests Reveal How Decision Models Use Rules and States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30045) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces controlled tests using executable arithmetic rules to separately measure rule transfer, joint reliability, and directed information use in decision models. It finds that equivalent rewrites of rules can have opposing task-level effects, and that a decision service changes answers more across equivalent wordings than across identical-text repeats. This work shows that aggregate accuracy alone is insufficient to understand which rule and information changes drive model answers, offering a methodology to diagnose reliability and information use. It could influence how decision models are evaluated in high-stakes settings where rule presentation and state associations matter. The method uses semantic rule separation and crossed rule and state tests, calibrates joint correctness through marginal-preserving reassociation, and alters tables to define alternative answer targets. Adaptation gains increase with nearest-source answer agreement, but fully conflicting quartets yield small pooled gains and different gain signs across source splits.

## [CIDOC CRM Periodic Table: A Single-Page Browser for 131 Classes and 480 Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28650) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the CIDOC CRM Periodic Table, a single-page browser that renders 131 classes and 480 property entries from four CIDOC CRM ontologies (CIDOC CRM 7.1.3, CRMsci 3.2, CRMdig 5.0, and CRMinf 1.2.1) as one navigable surface. It includes two inference facilities: a one-step triple builder that only allows class-property combinations licensed by declared domains, ranges, and subclass closure, and a multi-step path finder that enumerates property chains between two classes. This tool lowers the documentation barrier to adopting CIDOC CRM ontologies for humanities research data, making it easier for researchers to navigate and validate class-property combinations across multiple normative documents. It is a practical advance that could improve data interoperability in digital humanities and cultural heritage projects. The browser merges all loaded models into one code-keyed dictionary before computing hierarchy closures, enabling both inference facilities to resolve chains that cross model boundaries. The paper also documents the addition of CRMsci and three cross-model tests it enabled, and reports preliminary uptake signals from 56 months of analytics.

**Background**: CIDOC CRM is an ISO standard ontology (ISO 21127:2023) for cultural heritage and museum documentation, providing a formal structure for exchanging information. Its extensions, such as CRMsci for scientific observation, CRMdig for digital provenance, and CRMinf for inference and belief, formalize how research knowledge is produced and justified. However, adopting these ontologies is challenging due to four separate normative documents, four code conventions, and 89 typed links crossing document boundaries.

## [FutureLens Compares NMF and LDA for Longitudinal Topic Analysis of ACL Papers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28656) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FutureLens analyzes 5,655 ACL papers from 2012–2022, including 4,005 with future-work statements, to compare NMF and LDA on topic coherence, keyword diversity, and assignment stability. NMF achieves higher mean NPMI (0.1387 vs. 0.1280) and greater pairwise assignment stability, while LDA shows higher keyword diversity (0.8900 vs. 0.7750). This study highlights measurement challenges in using future-work statements to track field evolution, showing that model choice affects coherence and stability, which matters for scientometric analyses and research trend detection. It provides a methodological framework for validating longitudinal topic models in NLP. The paper reports mean input/reference topic-set overlap of 0.5191 for NMF and 0.4924 for LDA, and notes that temporal rank agreement varies widely and does not imply agreement in prevalence levels. Limitations include missing references, incomplete runs, and unverified reference provenance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Topic modeling is an NLP technique for discovering latent themes in large text collections. Latent Dirichlet Allocation (LDA) is a generative probabilistic model, while non-negative matrix factorization (NMF) is a linear-algebraic approach; both are widely used for exploratory analysis. Normalized pointwise mutual information (NPMI) is a coherence metric that measures how often topic words co-occur, normalized between -1 and +1.

## [LLM-built scholarly knowledge graphs need authorship and comparability provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28663) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.28663) argues that knowledge graphs built from LLM-extracted scholarly claims need stable addresses that record authorship and comparability decisions, not just source attribution. Using Sinitic dialectology as a case study, it introduces the PaRAGraph Casebook with four re-runnable cases showing where conflicting classifications and differing tone counts undermine naive verification. This work highlights a subtle but critical gap in LLM-powered knowledge graph construction: verifying a quotation or searching for conflicting values does not detect differences in what is being counted or classified. It matters for anyone building scholarly knowledge graphs or GraphRAG systems where evidence provenance must remain trustworthy as new sources arrive. The paper distinguishes three things: the working address that gathers candidate evidence, the category the source defines, and the decision that admits a passage to a particular comparison. The platform now stores these decisions as objects for one comparability dimension—the address of a record—including a locality's placement in each source's scheme, whether a name denotes a unit or a place, and the origin of every address.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs represent facts as entities and relationships, and provenance records where each fact came from so it can be audited. LLMs are increasingly used to extract claims from scholarly literature to build such graphs, but scholarly fields often contain competing classification schemes. Sinitic dialectology is a stark example: the same localities appear in schemes with different unit boundaries and ranks, and a unit's affiliation can change when diagnostic evidence changes.

## [Set Transformer predicts X-ray transmission in polymer composites in milliseconds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28709) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers developed STAR-XPC, a Set Transformer model that uses ChemBERTa embeddings to predict the X-ray transmission ratio I(t)/I0 of polymer composite slabs directly from their molecular constituents, mass fractions, and four bulk descriptors. Trained on 4,697 Monte Carlo simulations, the model achieves R² = 0.9988 and median absolute percentage error of 1.15%, while reducing inference time from over 5 hours to 1–3 milliseconds per query. This work demonstrates that permutation-invariant neural networks combined with chemistry-pretrained language models can replace computationally expensive Monte Carlo transport simulations for radiation shielding studies, enabling rapid design-space exploration and optimization of polymer composites. It could significantly accelerate research in radiation protection, medical imaging, and materials screening where fast, accurate transmission estimates are needed. The model handles mixtures of up to 11 constituents within a fixed-size padded set and is permutation invariant by construction. Training data were generated with a PENELOPE/penEasy pipeline at 30, 80, 100, and 120 kV tube voltages over polyethylene-based slabs of 0.15–2.5 mm thickness, and evaluation used leakage-free, jointly stratified 10-fold cross-validation; however, the approach is currently limited to this specific material system and voltage range.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo simulation is the gold standard for modeling X-ray photon transport through matter, but it is extremely computationally expensive because it tracks individual photon histories—often billions—to achieve statistical accuracy. The Set Transformer is a neural network architecture designed to process sets of elements in a permutation-invariant way, making it suitable for unordered inputs like molecular mixtures. ChemBERTa is a transformer language model pretrained on chemical structures, providing informative embeddings for molecules.

## [Dissertation Advances Offline Apprenticeship Learning for Human-Centric AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28716) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A dissertation presents a progression of offline apprenticeship learning frameworks—EM-EDM, THEMES, HALIDE, and CCHAL—that address reward modeling and demonstration quality in human-centric environments such as intelligent tutoring systems and clinical healthcare. The work includes a real-world classroom evaluation of StuEDM, which showed significant learning gains over an expert baseline while overriding students less than half as often. This research establishes offline apprenticeship learning as a practical, data-efficient, and ethically viable paradigm for high-stakes domains where online exploration is impossible or unethical. The demonstrated success in a real classroom suggests these methods could enable safer, more adaptive AI in education and healthcare without requiring risky environment interaction. THEMES partitions trajectories into temporally coherent sub-trajectories guided by a hierarchically learned reward regulator and outperforms flat and hierarchical baselines across one educational and three clinical datasets. CCHAL attributes quality to individual decisions via Bayesian credit assignment and a Bradley-Terry contrastive IRL objective, outperforming HALIDE on three clinical EHR datasets and an ITS dataset.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Apprenticeship learning (AL) is a technique where an agent learns a policy by observing expert demonstrations and inferring the underlying reward function, rather than through trial-and-error interaction with the environment. Offline reinforcement learning extends this by learning solely from fixed datasets, which is crucial in domains like healthcare and education where online experimentation is costly or unethical. Intelligent tutoring systems (ITSs) are computer-based educational platforms that adapt to student needs, and clinical healthcare decision-making requires policies that are both effective and safe.

## [SDI: Unifying Exploration and Planning in World Models via Sibling Disagreement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28779) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces SDI (Sibling Disagreement in Imagination), a method that uses repeated rollouts of an actor's own imagined action sequence as a shared reference to jointly scale exploration signals and plan-acceptance thresholds in world-model-based agents. SDI achieves a higher observed task-average score than every compared baseline across Meta-World, Adroit, and DM-Control, and matches or exceeds the strongest baseline on 15 of 18 tasks. This work offers a unified mechanism for exploration and planning in world-model-based reinforcement learning, potentially simplifying agent design by eliminating the need for separate disagreement ensembles. It could benefit RL researchers and practitioners working on sample-efficient learning in complex control tasks. SDI reuses the base agent's world model, actor, and critic without an additional ensemble, and the agent executes a planned action only when its predicted advantage exceeds the reference variability by a fixed factor. Ablations and paired evaluations show the gate improves recovery from actor failures and preserves successful behavior while reducing return degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: World models are learned simulators that allow agents to predict the outcomes of potential actions and plan in imagination, reducing real-world trial and error. In actor-critic methods like Dreamer, an actor proposes actions and a critic evaluates them, but the agent must still decide when to trust a planned sequence over its learned policy and how to explore effectively. SDI addresses both by comparing imagined alternatives against the actor's own repeated rollouts as a baseline variability.

## [ArcFlow trains one flow policy for multiple solver budgets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28788) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ArcFlow is a deployment-aware reinforcement learning algorithm that trains a single flow-matching policy to perform well across a range of solver budgets, rather than at one fixed budget. It combines action-aligned supervision, budget exposure, and worst-budget checkpoint selection, and achieves the highest mean one-step return among five flow-policy baselines on 25 of 30 tasks across DM Control, manipulation, and locomotion. Single-budget RL can hide performance degradation at cheaper inference budgets and even erase useful one-step behavior from a pretrained policy, so ArcFlow's deployment-budget-aware training offers a practical route to strong low-step control with one policy serving multiple inference costs. This matters for deploying flow-matching policies in latency- or compute-constrained settings such as robotics and real-time control. For reconstruction objectives, ArcFlow's bridge aggregates temporal probes into one surrogate ratio and one PPO-style clip per action, with a boundary residual exactly equal to the deployed one-step reconstruction error; for native velocity objectives, multi-budget endpoint supervision applies the same principle without replacing the native update. Both realizations preserve the architecture and deployment-time cost, and component studies confirm complementary gains from boundary supervision, the fixed-grid bridge, budget exposure, and deployment selection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Flow-matching policies are generative models that produce actions by integrating a learned velocity field over a number of solver steps, so fewer steps mean cheaper inference but potentially lower quality. Reinforcement learning is commonly used to fine-tune such policies, but training at a single fixed solver budget can overfit to that budget and degrade performance when deployed at other step counts. ArcFlow addresses this by explicitly training across a specified range of budgets and optimizing for the worst-case (cheapest) budget.

## [PhD Thesis Proposes Causal Approaches for Robust Decision-Making and Dynamical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28803) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new PhD thesis (arXiv:2610.28803) argues for a causal perspective on decision-making and dynamical systems, proposing an alternative framework for algorithmic recourse with realistic cost functions and temporal policy evaluation, and extending causal interventions to stochastic processes to link equilibria with Structural Causal Models (SCMs). The work addresses a key limitation of statistical learning—poor generalization under distribution shift—by leveraging causality to reason about interventions and hypothetical scenarios, which could improve the robustness and trustworthiness of AI decision-making systems in high-stakes applications like credit or hiring. The thesis is divided into two parts: the first reformulates algorithmic recourse in causal terms with a realistic cost function and temporal dimension, while the second extends causal interventions to discrete and continuous stochastic processes, showing that equilibria changes after intervention can be represented by SCMs as compressed temporal dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Statistical machine learning often fails when deployed data differs from training data, a problem known as distribution shift. Causal models, which represent underlying mechanisms rather than surface associations, enable reasoning about interventions and can generalize better to new environments. Algorithmic recourse refers to providing individuals with actionable changes to reverse an unfavorable automated decision, and structural causal models (SCMs) are mathematical frameworks for representing causal relationships.

## [RL Coordinator Trained with PPO Adapts Task Allocation in Uncertain Systems-of-Systems Missions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28818) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes an intelligent mission coordination methodology that integrates high-fidelity digital mission models with reinforcement learning, formulating mission tactics management as a Markov Decision Process and training a coordinator with Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) to map mission states to tactical actions. An aerial firefighting mission with three scenarios demonstrates that all trained coordinators significantly outperform random and heuristic baselines, and a coordinator trained on both scenarios concurrently matches scenario-specific coordinators while significantly outperforming every other policy. This work shows that digital-engineering-enabled mission simulations combined with reinforcement learning offer a reusable methodology for improving mission engineering practice, which can be extended to fleet design and selection from a mission-first perspective. It addresses the brittleness of static architectures in uncertain, dynamic mission environments, relevant to systems engineering as it shifts from monolithic systems to Systems of Systems. Single-scenario coordinators can be brittle: the coordinator trained on the more forgiving scenario degrades to random tactics performance in more demanding scenarios, whereas the demanding-scenario coordinator retains most of its advantage when transferred. Sensitivity analyses on ignition location, weather, scenario, and training curricula show that the variability represented during training shapes post-deployment performance.

Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) is a policy gradient reinforcement learning algorithm introduced by OpenAI in 2017 that improves an agent's policy incrementally while preventing drastic updates. ## [Parallel Pretraining Alone Falls Short, Two-Stage Approach Works](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28838) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.28838) compares parallel pretraining (PPT), which concatenates each document with its translation in the same training sequence, against standard pretraining (SPT) at matched token budgets. Surprisingly, PPT alone does not consistently improve language understanding, but a two-stage approach (PPT followed by SPT) consistently outperforms SPT alone in both high- and low-resource settings. This negative result challenges the assumption that leveraging parallel structure during pretraining automatically improves multilingual models, and the proposed two-stage recipe offers a practical way to build stronger cross-lingual foundations without sacrificing general language understanding. It is relevant to researchers and practitioners working on multilingual LLMs, especially those using machine-translated corpora. The analysis suggests that PPT builds a strong cross-lingual foundation, as shown by better use of shared information across languages, but the mismatch between parallel training contexts and single-language task inputs limits its gains in general language understanding. The two-stage approach first uses PPT to build a cross-lingual foundation, then continues with SPT to adapt to standard language modeling, consistently improving both general language understanding and cross-lingual task performance at matched training budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multilingual pretraining trains a single language model on text from many languages to enable cross-lingual transfer, where knowledge learned in one language helps performance in another. Parallel corpora—collections of documents and their translations—are known to aid cross-lingual tasks but are often too small for pretraining; machine-translated web corpora have made parallel data available at scale. Standard multilingual pretraining typically samples source documents and their translations independently, ignoring their parallel structure, and this paper investigates whether explicitly using that structure helps.

## [Quantum Layers in LLaVA-OneVision: Accuracy Gains and Parameter Savings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28964) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.28964) evaluates classically simulated quantum layers inserted into or replacing components of the LLaVA-OneVision vision-language model, testing 4- and 8-qubit basic-ring and strongly entangling circuits at four insertion and four replacement locations. On the Visual Spatial Reasoning dataset, inserting a quantum layer after the visual projector achieved 80.7% development accuracy, 2.28 percentage points above classical full fine-tuning, while replacing the final decoder feed-forward sublayer retained 79.22% accuracy with 13.07 million fewer trainable parameters. This study provides an early empirical map of where quantum layers help or hurt within large multimodal models, suggesting that quantum insertion can improve accuracy and selective replacement can reduce parameters. It offers a starting point for hybrid quantum-classical VLM design, though whether these gains generalize remains an open question. The benefits depend strongly on placement: earlier replacements lower accuracy, and fewer parameters do not reliably shorten training or reduce computation. The authors also evaluated robustness under six attack settings to assess potential security benefits, and the code is available at the TITANS-Lab-Artifacts/QuantumVLM GitHub repository.

**Background**: Quantum computing is moving from the noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) era, where devices are limited by noise and error rates, toward early fault-tolerant quantum computing (EFTQC). Quantum-enhanced AI research often uses classically simulated quantum circuits, such as basic-ring and strongly entangling layers, to prototype hybrid models before real quantum hardware is practical. Vision-language models like LLaVA-OneVision combine visual and textual inputs to perform multimodal tasks, and researchers are exploring whether quantum components can extend their capabilities.

## [Quantum Models Tested Under Differential Privacy on Health Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28968) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.28968) empirically compares parameter-efficient quantum models against classical MLPs and logistic regression under matched differential privacy budgets across three clinical cohorts and one wearable cohort. The quantum model leads on three clinical cohorts by up to 0.104 ROC AUC averaged across budgets, but trails on the wearable cohort, and no difference survives Holm correction across 24 tests. This work directly probes whether quantum machine learning's low parameter count can mitigate the utility cost of differential privacy in small health cohorts, a question relevant to privacy-preserving clinical AI. The negative or inconclusive results help set realistic expectations for near-term quantum advantages in sensitive health data settings. The study uses five privacy budgets and five seeds, and an analytic gradient bound shows that lower clipping thresholds can improve utility by reducing noise despite clipping bias. An experiment on IBM's 156-qubit ibm_fez processor with eight fixed test cases yields mean absolute probability errors of 0.046 (private) and 0.063 (non-private) relative to simulation, supporting NISQ inference feasibility; code is publicly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Differential privacy (DP) provides a formal guarantee that adding or removing one record barely changes an algorithm's output, with the privacy budget epsilon controlling the noise-utility tradeoff: smaller epsilon means stronger privacy but more noise. Variational quantum circuits are parameterized quantum models trained by classical optimizers, and their low parameter count is hypothesized to reduce the noise needed under DP. Clinical registries and wearable sensors enable early health-risk assessment but pose leakage risks when models are released.

## [HumanCentric-Scenes-AI: A Curated Multimodal Dataset of AI-Generated People](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29045) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces HumanCentric-Scenes-AI, a human-curated dataset of 296 AI-generated stimuli depicting people in four contexts: surveillance (85 CCTV-style images), occupations (128 portraits covering 64 occupations with balanced gender pairs), AI-edited images (27 real photos with regions altered by generative models), and short videos (56 clips). Each stimulus includes the file, generation prompt where recorded, and per-stimulus metadata, and the authors report gender distributions compared to U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics occupational shares, edit-region size effects on zero-shot detection difficulty, image-quality distributions for surveillance, and subject-category coverage for videos. This dataset provides a human-curated resource for studying how people perceive and detect AI-generated human-centric media, which is increasingly prevalent in surveillance, professional portraits, and short-form video. It can support bias analysis of generative models and calibration of AI-detection classifiers, areas of growing importance as synthetic media becomes more photorealistic. The dataset is relatively small (296 stimuli) and manually curated over multiple rounds for photorealism, with limitations including the lack of specified gender in 134 initial generations and the focus on U.S. occupational statistics. The authors report edit-region size and its association with zero-shot detection difficulty, but the dataset is intended primarily for human-perception research rather than as a large-scale training resource.

**Background**: AI-generated media, including deepfakes and synthetic images/videos, are created by generative models such as GANs and diffusion models, and can be highly photorealistic. ## [AnoMix Distills Multiple Anomaly Detectors into One Lightweight Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29063) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AnoMix introduces a method to distill heterogeneous frozen time-series anomaly detectors into a single lightweight student model using weak pairwise supervision and a novel multi-teacher ranking-distillation objective called CoRank. Evaluated on seven benchmark datasets, the student model with under 80k parameters outperforms individual detectors and static score ensembles, and a zero-shot variant transfers to unseen datasets, even surpassing large pretrained time-series foundation models. This work addresses the high inference and maintenance costs of deploying ensembles of anomaly detectors by compressing complementary scoring principles into a single compact model, making unsupervised anomaly detection more practical for resource-constrained environments. The zero-shot transfer capability suggests potential for general-purpose anomaly detection models that reduce the need for dataset-specific training. The method extracts weak pairwise supervision from the training-set anomaly scores of frozen teachers, normalizes each teacher's score sequence independently, and uses teacher consensus to convert score gaps into pairwise supervision for training the student. The compact student has under 80k parameters on benchmark settings, and the zero-shot variant demonstrates that the distilled ranking signal transfers to unseen datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Time series anomaly detection aims to identify unusual patterns in sequential data, and unsupervised methods are common because labeled anomalies are scarce. Knowledge distillation compresses a large model (teacher) into a smaller one (student), and multi-teacher distillation combines knowledge from several teachers. Ranking distillation transfers the ranking order of items from teacher to student, which is useful when absolute scores are less important than relative ordering.

## [Value-Rectified Distillation Tackles Mode Averaging in Flow-Based Offline RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29075) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Value-Rectified Distillation (VRD), a value-guided framework that reformulates offline policy distillation as a generative trajectory alignment problem to prevent mode averaging when distilling flow-matching behavior policies. VRD is instantiated as two algorithms: VRD-Q, which uses value-quantile behavior distillation to filter out low-value behaviors, and VRD-R, which reorganizes the latent space to geometrically isolate high-value trajectories. This work addresses a critical failure mode in offline RL where standard distillation of multi-modal flow policies collapses actions into invalid out-of-distribution regions, potentially improving policy robustness on diverse continuous control and planning tasks. It could benefit practitioners using generative models for decision-making in robotics and autonomous systems where offline data is multi-modal and non-expert. VRD employs a dynamic coupling mechanism to rectify flow dynamics and decouple behavioral modes, and empirical evaluations show improvements over existing generative baselines on D4RL, OGBench, and Minari benchmarks, especially on multi-modal, non-expert datasets. The approach is specifically designed for flow-matching based policies and may not directly apply to other generative policy classes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning aims to learn policies from fixed datasets without environment interaction, but multi-modal action distributions can cause mode averaging, where the policy outputs invalid interpolations between modes. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that learns continuous normalizing flows by regressing a vector field, and distilling such policies into simpler forms is challenging when data is multi-modal. Policy distillation transfers knowledge from a complex teacher policy to a simpler student, and in offline RL, value guidance helps isolate high-value actions.

## [UNIVERS: Universal Voxel-wise Embeddings via Randomized Synthesis for One-Shot Landmark Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29098) ⭐️ 6.0/10

UNIVERS is an end-to-end trainable framework that learns universal voxel-wise embeddings by synthesizing diverse pseudo-data from organ shape templates with randomization in semantic layout, visual appearance, field of view, and spatial pose. Coupled with cross-domain discriminative voxel-wise contrastive learning, it enables one-shot landmark localization in multi-modality volume scans with arbitrary fields of view, and experiments on three adrenal gland datasets show it consistently outperforms state-of-the-art one-shot methods, especially in cross-modality scenarios. This work addresses a key limitation of existing self-supervised landmark localization methods—poor generalization across imaging modalities—by eliminating the need for paired multi-modality training data and multi-stage iterative training. If validated beyond adrenal glands, it could reduce annotation burden and improve robustness in clinical workflows where only a single annotated scan is available and modalities vary. The method is demonstrated on localizing the adrenal glands, which are paired, small, and irregular endocrine organs, using three challenging datasets; the source code is publicly available on GitHub. The approach relies on domain randomization over synthetic pseudo-data rather than real paired multi-modality images, which may raise questions about the realism gap between synthetic and real scans.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: One-shot landmark localization aims to detect anatomical landmarks in medical images using only a single annotated example, greatly reducing the cost of expert annotation. Self-supervised learning with contrastive learning typically creates augmented views of the same image, which limits generalization when test images come from different imaging modalities (e.g., CT vs. MRI). Voxel-wise contrastive learning extends contrastive objectives to 3D volumetric data, and randomized synthesis of pseudo-data is a form of synthetic data generation used to improve robustness in medical imaging.

## [Dual-Channel Contrastive Encoder Models Sperm Whale Coda Rhythm and Spectrum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29101) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the Dual-Channel Contrastive Encoder (DCCE), which uses separate encoders for rhythm (inter-click intervals) and spectral features of sperm whale codas, then fuses them for three classification tasks. Evaluated on 1,383 codas with three random splits and three recording-date-disjoint folds, DCCE achieves mean macro-F1 scores of 0.901, 0.865, and 0.597 for social unit, coda type, and individual identification, respectively. This work demonstrates that contrastive learning can effectively separate and fuse timing and spectral information in bioacoustic signals, offering a template for analyzing animal communication with task-specific feature combinations. It could benefit marine biologists and machine learning researchers studying cetacean vocalizations, though its impact remains within the niche of bioacoustics. Raw inter-click intervals (ICI) remain the strongest single feature for coda type (macro-F1 0.987), and ICI plus temporal mel statistics is a strong identity reference (0.724). An auxiliary identity objective improves spectral-branch identity prediction but does not improve the fused-identity score, and date-grouped evaluation and year holdouts expose generalization limits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Sperm whales communicate using stereotyped sequences of clicks called codas, which vary in rhythmic pattern (inter-click intervals) and spectral composition. Contrastive learning is a self-supervised technique that trains models to distinguish similar (positive) from dissimilar (negative) pairs, and has been applied to audio processing. Recent AI analyses suggest codas form a complex communication system with at least 150 repeatable patterns, motivating computational approaches to decode them.

## [Indian Engineering Students Navigate AI-Free Exams and AI-First Workplaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29140) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29140) presents an interview-based study of 23 Indian engineering students and recent graduates, examining how they use generative AI across coursework, AI-free examinations, and campus placements, and how they judge their readiness for AI-mediated work. Using reflexive thematic analysis, the authors identify five themes, including AI-first workplaces versus AI-free placement screens, preparation strategies, coursework AI use, faculty authority and enforcement, and concerns about work readiness. This study fills a geographic gap in HCI research on generative AI in education, which has largely focused on Western and East Asian contexts, and it raises timely questions about what grades and placement test scores actually certify when AI can help produce them. Its findings have implications for employers, institutions, and AI tool designers in India's massive engineering education system, which has an approved intake of nearly 2.9 million seats for 2026-2027. The study is qualitative and based on only 23 interviews, so its findings are not statistically generalizable; it uses signaling theory to discuss what grades and screen scores certify once AI can assist in producing them. The authors offer recommendations for employers, institutions, and AI tool designers, but the paper reports incremental rather than paradigm-shifting findings.

## [Generated Chest X-rays: Quality Checks Can't Verify Authenticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29152) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.29152) evaluates whether generated chest radiographs can be distinguished from real ones at dataset intake by separating quality checks, origin classification, and record verification. Using Qwen to select up to 20 images from pools of 1,000 Cheff-identified or SynthCheX candidates, the study finds that quality-based selection raises quality pass rates but does not improve combined detection, and that an origin classifier on frozen DINOv3 features flags 4,677 of 5,000 SynthCheX images while misclassifying 110 of 2,297 real NIH images and 568 of 957 genuine images from another collection as fake. This work highlights a critical gap in medical dataset integrity: automated quality and origin checks can filter submissions but cannot verify that an image actually corresponds to a real examination. As synthetic medical images become more prevalent, the findings advocate for separately recording and checking source and intended use, which could impact how medical imaging datasets are curated and trusted. The origin classifier achieves an AUROC of 0.987 on unprocessed SynthCheX versus NIH images, but JPEG compression reduces this to 0.841, and even without processing, 152 SynthCheX images pass both pixel checks. The study uses MedGemma for quality evaluation and a classifier on frozen DINOv3 features for origin prediction, with selection experiments showing no selected Cheff-identified image passes both checks.

**Background**: CheXpert is a large public dataset of chest radiographs from Stanford Health Care, often used as a source of real medical images. DINOv3 is a vision foundation model from Meta whose frozen features are widely used for image classification tasks. The paper addresses the growing concern that synthetic images, while useful for augmentation, could be misrepresented as real evidence in medical datasets.

## [OrbitWeaver Checkpoints VLM Inference Across Satellite Interruptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29183) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OrbitWeaver is a framework that preserves vision-language model (VLM) inference progress across onboard interruptions on low-Earth orbit satellites using phase-aware checkpointing and dependency-aware dynamic recovery. Experiments on satellite payload hardware show up to a 4.07-fold end-to-end speedup and a 3.56-fold reduction in SSD write volume, and a 72-hour deployment on two satellites demonstrated progress retention across repeated bus-enforced shutdowns. This work addresses a practical obstacle to running VLMs directly on satellites: power, thermal, and fault-induced reboots can interrupt long inference requests, and without protection all volatile activations and KV caches are lost. By making inference resumable, OrbitWeaver could enable more flexible onboard processing of observation data without downlinking, benefiting embedded and ML-systems researchers working on fault-tolerant edge AI. The phase-aware checkpointing selects recovery boundaries based on state lifetimes and computational characteristics of vision encoding, language-model prefill, and decoding, balancing storage traffic against recomputation. The dependency-aware recovery planner schedules restoration from partially completed states across layer and token-chunk dimensions and computes an optimal execution order within 100 ms, overlapping checkpoint and weight reads with recomputation to reduce restart latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) process images and text together and are increasingly considered for onboard satellite use to analyze observation data without downlinking it. However, commercial off-the-shelf satellite payloads face power and thermal constraints, fault-triggered reboots, and competing payload demands, so a single inference request may span multiple execution windows. Checkpointing saves intermediate state to durable storage so computation can resume after an interruption, but traditional checkpointing is not optimized for the multi-phase structure of VLM inference or the limited storage and compute of satellite hardware.

## [ChronoSynth: Constraint-Aware Question Generation from Temporal Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29339) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ChronoSynth introduces a memory-augmented, constraint-aware framework for generating natural-language questions from temporal knowledge graphs, explicitly preserving answer, time, event, and ordering constraints. It constructs a Temporal Intent State, retrieves typed question patterns from an indexed memory, and validates generated questions with feedback-driven revisions, achieving state-of-the-art results on CRONQUESTIONS and MultiTQ. This work addresses temporal semantic drift, a key failure mode in knowledge graph question generation where fluent questions inadvertently alter time conditions or event references. It could improve the reliability of question generation for temporal reasoning benchmarks and downstream applications like conversational QA over evolving knowledge. The framework relies on an indexed pattern memory of typed question patterns and a validation loop that detects supported inconsistencies to guide revisions. Experiments include ablations confirming the contribution of each component, though the approach is evaluated only on two temporal KG datasets.

Constraint satisfaction in natural language generation ensures that generated text adheres to specified logical conditions.

The paper introduces a framework that derives BI-RADS descriptors from breast MRI segmentation masks using a radiomics-based decision tree with eight quantitative measurements, then employs a constrained large language model to generate natural-language clinical rationales. Applied to 1,506 cases from the MAMA-MIA dataset, it produced 3,012 reproducible rationales with 99.98% faithfulness and 99.3% counterfactual accuracy. This work addresses a key explainability gap in deep learning-based medical image segmentation by linking masks to clinically standard BI-RADS descriptors and generating faithful rationales, which could aid radiologist review and clinical integration. The decision tree uses eight radiomics measurements plus lesion size and component count to determine descriptors at eight nodes, and the LLM is constrained to express only the derived records. Agreement between descriptors from expert and automatic masks drops from 0.832 to 0.549 as mask quality falls from Good to Missed, and the rationales are intended as draft representations for radiologist review, not autonomous reports.

## [JEPA objectives underperform masked language modeling on longitudinal EHRs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29528) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper empirically tests three Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) formulations adapted to longitudinal EHR sequence representation on the MIMIC-IV dataset. Despite training stably without representational collapse, all JEPA variants consistently underperform standard masked language modeling (MLM) on downstream tasks. This negative result is valuable for the medical machine learning community, showing that stable latent prediction alone is insufficient for competitive EHR representation learning. It cautions researchers against assuming that JEPA's success in vision and other domains will transfer directly to healthcare data. The study finds that target size, not target heterogeneity, increases prediction error, yet reducing target size does not recover downstream performance. The scope is limited to a single dataset (MIMIC-IV), and the authors highlight the challenge of defining clinically meaningful latent targets for EHRs.

## [Block Diffusion Matches Autoregressive Models for EHR Prediction with Faster Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29533) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates block-based masked diffusion (BD) as an alternative to autoregressive (AR) models for zero-shot prediction from longitudinal electronic health records on the MIMIC-IV dataset. Under matched tokenisation, context length, model capacity, and evaluation conditions, a representative block size of 8 achieves approximately 1.43× faster evaluation while retaining much of AR's predictive performance. This work suggests that block diffusion can offer a computationally attractive alternative to strictly sequential autoregressive models for clinical prediction tasks, potentially enabling faster inference in resource-constrained healthcare settings. The near-identical linear probe performance indicates that BD learns comparably informative representations, which could encourage broader adoption of diffusion-based approaches in clinical machine learning. The study finds a clear quality-efficiency trade-off across block sizes: small blocks retain predictive performance with faster inference, but generative rollout remains below AR in most settings. Increasing the denoising budget yields diminishing returns, and alternative decoding strategies provide limited gains, suggesting that further improvements in iterative decoding are needed to close the generative performance gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive models generate sequences token by token in a fixed order, which can be inefficient for clinical trajectories where events are temporally clustered and interdependent. Block-based masked diffusion instead generates tokens in blocks using iterative denoising, allowing bidirectional context within each block. MIMIC-IV is a large, de-identified dataset of intensive care unit admissions widely used for clinical machine learning research. Zero-shot prediction refers to making predictions without task-specific fine-tuning, relying on the model's learned representations.

## [CHIP 2026 Shared Task 1 Overview: Patient-Specific Phenotype Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29606) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents an overview of CHIP 2026 Shared Task 1, which focuses on patient-specific phenotype concept recognition in full-text biomedical literature and consists of two subtasks: full-text phenotype concept recognition and patient-specific phenotype association. The evaluation used 200 annotated full-text articles from the PatientPheX dataset, containing 506 patients, 15,193 phenotype mentions, and 5,656 patient-phenotype associations, with 455 registered teams and the best system achieving an overall score of 0.7389, outperforming the baseline by 8.37 percentage points. This shared task advances methods for extracting phenotype information from full-text biomedical literature, which is crucial for rare disease diagnosis, genotype-phenotype analysis, and precision medicine. The large annotated dataset and competitive results provide a benchmark for future research in biomedical information extraction and highlight the potential of combining biomedical language models with large language models. The best-performing system achieved an overall score of 0.7389 on the final test set, with top teams exploring diverse approaches combining biomedical language models, large language models (LLMs), and ensemble strategies. The evaluation results highlight remaining challenges in patient attribution and phenotype normalization from full-text biomedical literature, and the post-evaluation phase remains open on Tianchi.

**Background**: Phenotype concept recognition involves identifying phenotypic abnormalities (e.g., symptoms, clinical signs) in text and mapping them to standardized vocabularies such as the Human Phenotype Ontology (HPO). Patient-specific phenotype association goes further by linking each phenotype mention to the correct patient, which is challenging in full-text articles describing multiple patients. The PatientPheX dataset was created for this task, comprising 200 full-text articles (mostly case reports and case series) with detailed annotations. CHIP (China Conference on Health Information Processing) is a leading forum for biomedical NLP in China, and its shared tasks benchmark progress in clinical and biomedical text mining.

## [FogTTA: Online Test-Time Adaptation for Transformer Detectors in Fog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29618) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FogTTA introduces an online teacher-student test-time adaptation framework that improves the robustness of Transformer-based object detectors, specifically RF-DETR, in foggy weather without requiring source data or retraining. The teacher generates pseudo-labels from weakly augmented target inputs, which are refined via non-maximum suppression and confidence filtering, while the student learns from strongly augmented samples using Varifocal loss, and the teacher is updated via exponential moving averaging. This work addresses a practical problem for autonomous driving: object detectors trained on clear-weather images suffer significant performance drops in fog due to domain shift. By enabling real-time adaptation without source data, FogTTA offers a deployable solution for maintaining detection accuracy in adverse weather conditions. The framework uses RF-DETR as the underlying detector and maintains real-time performance while outperforming prior baselines in detection accuracy and stability. It relies on pseudo-labeling with noise mitigation via Varifocal loss and exponential moving averaging for stable teacher updates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time adaptation (TTA) is a machine learning paradigm where a deployed model adapts to distribution shifts at inference time using only unlabeled test samples, without access to the original training data or labels. RF-DETR is a real-time Transformer-based object detector developed by Roboflow, achieving state-of-the-art results on benchmarks like COCO and RF100-VL. Teacher-student pseudo-labeling is a common approach in domain adaptation, where a teacher model generates pseudo-labels on the target domain to guide a student model's learning.

## [AI-Assisted Lean Formalization Corrects 2018 Planning Thesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29637) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An arXiv paper presents an AI-assisted Lean formalization addendum to Amar's 2018 planning thesis, reconstructing and correcting its Nash-to-core proposition by making outsider opportunities explicit and verifying two-person implications under fixed outsiders. It also proves a finite negotiation procedure terminates with participant approval, joint-cost non-worsening, and local optimality, and provides a resource-game counterexample showing monotonicity alone yields no uniform price-of-anarchy bound. This work demonstrates how AI-assisted formalization can turn informal engineering intuitions from legacy research into precise, machine-checked mathematical claims with explicit assumptions and reproducible computations, which is valuable for researchers in formal methods and game theory seeking traceable reconstructions. The formalization includes a verified negotiation procedure in Lean, supporting formalizations of bargaining geometry and regret-based efficiency, and reproducible computations comparing a heuristic reconstruction with the verified procedure on 44 source-based instances and 96 starting cases from four synthetic network families. The contribution is a traceable reconstruction linking source statements, explicit assumptions, checked proofs, executable code, and retained counterinstances, rather than new mathematical ingredients.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Lean is an interactive theorem prover that allows mathematicians to write proofs in a formal language and have them checked by a small trusted kernel. In cooperative game theory, the core is the set of feasible allocations where no coalition can benefit by breaking away, and the Nash equilibrium is a solution concept for non-cooperative games. A TU (transferable utility) core requires that payoffs can be transferred among players, and the price of anarchy measures the efficiency loss due to selfish behavior.

## [LineageEvo Adds Lineage-Level Control to LLM Evolutionary Factor Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29663) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29663) proposes LineageEvo, which introduces two coupled lineage-level control mechanisms for LLM-based evolutionary alpha factor mining: lineage-conditioned LLM variation that distills lineage-scoped search experience into operator priors, and lineage-stratified search allocation that distributes search budget across lineages. Evaluated on CSI 500 and S&P 500, LineageEvo achieves the strongest predictive performance on both markets while producing a more balanced lineage distribution and a structurally broader factor pool. Current LLM-based evolutionary factor mining organizes search mainly at the individual and population levels, leaving the search weakly directed and prone to concentrating around structurally similar factors. By showing that lineage is a useful intermediate unit for search control, this work offers a concrete way to improve both the predictiveness and diversity of automatically mined alpha factors, which matters for quantitative finance researchers and practitioners using LLM-driven factor discovery. The method couples lineage-conditioned LLM variation—fusing lineage-scoped priors with global operator priors—with lineage-stratified search allocation that balances factor quality against lineage concentration. Experiments measure factor predictiveness, portfolio performance, search efficiency, and both lineage- and expression-level diversity, though the evaluation is limited to two equity markets and the approach remains specialized to formulaic alpha mining.

**Background**: Formulaic alpha factor mining searches for symbolic expressions that transform market data into predictive signals for ranking stocks or building portfolios. Recent methods use large language models as semantic variation operators within evolutionary algorithms (LLM-EA), where candidate factors are iteratively mutated and selected by fitness. In this paradigm, a lineage is the chain of successive factor variants connected through evolutionary descent, a unit of organization that prior methods have largely left unused.

## [BiasGuard-D: Typed Adversarial Contracts for Auditable LLM Output Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29669) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces BiasGuard-D, a framework where two adversarial LLM agents from distinct model families exchange typed claim documents, a three-model quorum adjudicates by majority vote, and a combined referential-plus-semantic invariant validates the verdict. It demonstrates that the deliberation layer transfers from stereotype to hallucination remediation on HaluEval QA, raising right-answer recovery by 49 percentage points with only role-framing changes. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM output quality enforcement by providing a deterministic, auditable check that can catch conflicting defects (e.g., a grounded fact adjacent to a hallucination) that classical data cleaning and single LLM judges cannot resolve. It offers a provenance trail auditable under emerging AI regulations, potentially impacting how production information systems manage LLM-generated records. The semantic component of the invariant fires on verdicts that referential checks miss, and removing the iterative loop sharply drops invariant satisfaction. The triage gate required a cross-family second auditor to maintain recall on objective hallucination, and every claim, vote, and instruction is stored as a typed record for provenance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-generated records are increasingly treated as data assets in production systems, but they can simultaneously satisfy and violate quality rules, creating conflicts that classical data cleaning cannot resolve. Existing interventions like prompt engineering, chain-of-thought, and RLHF reduce surface error rates but lack audit trails and cannot distinguish genuine remediation from blanket suppression. Typed contracts between AI agents provide a formal boundary for inter-agent messaging, and deterministic guardrails aim to make LLM outputs safe and auditable.

## [Structure-Aware GNNs for Petri Nets Improve Process Discovery Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29725) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces PNRWPE and DA-PNGNN, two structure-aware message-passing techniques that expose directed cycles and incidence-matrix invariants to graph neural networks for learning Petri net representations. On the 96 Process Discovery Contest 2023 models, PNRWPE raises macro balanced accuracy from 0.751 to 0.796, nearly doubling loop accuracy, while DA-PNGNN achieves 0.830 and also improves net-to-tree retrieval top-1 accuracy on ProcRosetta by 4.1 to 5.9 points without retuning. This work addresses a key limitation of message-passing GNNs on Petri nets by explicitly encoding structural features like loops and incidence invariants, which are critical for process mining tasks such as process discovery and model retrieval. The measurable gains, especially on loop-heavy models, could lead to more accurate and robust process mining tools that better handle complex business processes. PNRWPE uses a positional encoding built from return probabilities of directed place-transition walks, while DA-PNGNN employs same-type messages gated by diffusion kernels of the incidence matrix. The improvements are demonstrated on the PDC 2023 dataset and the self-supervised ProcRosetta benchmark, with the latter showing gains without retuning, indicating generalizability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Petri nets are bipartite directed graphs used to model concurrent processes, with places and transitions as nodes. The incidence matrix captures the net's topology by recording connections between places and transitions, and its invariants (like siphons and traps) provide structural insights. Message-passing graph neural networks learn node representations by aggregating information from neighbors, but their local inductive bias limits their ability to capture global structures like cycles. Positional encodings are often added to inject such structural information.

A new arXiv paper (2610.29843) reproduces and extends prior concept-augmentation research beyond nouns to verbs and adjectives, testing whether the part of speech carrying the concept signal affects perplexity and downstream performance. The study finds that concept augmentation improves perplexity most consistently on news data regardless of POS, while verbs yield the most reliable downstream gains. This work suggests that where conceptual information is introduced during training matters, which could inform more targeted data augmentation strategies for LLM post-training. It is a useful incremental contribution to training methodology rather than a paradigm shift, and will mainly interest researchers working on data augmentation and representation learning. Representational analyses using CKA and linear probing reveal that the conditions producing the largest downstream gains are not necessarily those that most alter the model's internal representations. This dissociation between behavioral and representational effects suggests that the effectiveness of concept augmentation depends on both where and how conceptual information is introduced.

## [Gemini 3.8 Flash Screens Falls Zero-Shot on Thermal Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29913) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers evaluated the production multimodal LLM Gemini 3.8 Flash at temperature 0 as the decision stage of an event-triggered fall screener, using one request per short thermal clip. On 438 staged falls and 563 non-fall windows from a public 32x24 thermal dataset, the deployed prompt achieved 69% recall on held-out subjects and zero false alarms on 499 non-fall windows, while 76 hours of home monitoring at 160x120 produced no false calls and caught 4 of 9 visible staged falls. This study shows that a general-purpose multimodal LLM can serve as a training-free fall screener on privacy-preserving thermal video, achieving zero false alarms in a real home where two conventional detectors trained on laboratory data raised 14.2 and 23.9 false alarms per day. It suggests a practical path for low-cost, privacy-conscious monitoring of older adults living alone, though the modest sensitivity and prompt sensitivity indicate careful tuning is still required. A single cautionary prompt sentence ("a person who goes out of view ... is not a fall") caused many misses: removing it raised held-out sensitivity from 69% to 83% on 3.8 Flash and from 80% to 92% on Gemini 3.7 Flash, and in the home from 4 to 9 of 9 visible falls, but at the cost of calling deliberate lie-downs a fall. Sensitivity collapsed to 34% at 8x6 pixels, identical requests disagreed on 4% of windows, and when the model called a fall its explanation landed within one second of the annotated landing 99% of the time.
