## [ThreadRAG: Semantic Topology Graph Preserves Context in RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29933) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ThreadRAG introduces a Semantic Topology Mapper (STM) that organizes retrieved documents into context-intact Semantic Nodes and builds a Semantic Topology Graph (STG) with narrative, semantic, and lexical edges, plus Energy-Driven Pathfinding (EDP) to linearize query-relevant subgraphs into coherent textual evidence paths. Experiments on three multi-hop QA benchmarks show a 14.4% EM improvement over text-centric methods on HotpotQA and a 5.8% EM gain over the best baseline on 2WikiMultihop, while reducing token cost to 23.3% of the strongest baseline. This work addresses two key bottlenecks in knowledge-graph-based RAG—semantic loss from distilling text into discrete symbols and modality mismatch between fragmented graphs and continuous text—by proposing a context-preserving, modality-native approach. If validated, it could significantly improve multi-hop question answering accuracy and efficiency, impacting RAG system design for complex reasoning tasks. The Semantic Topology Graph uses three types of edges (narrative, semantic, lexical) to capture different aspects of document relationships, and Energy-Driven Pathfinding performs energy diffusion to identify relevant subgraphs before deterministic linearization. The approach is evaluated on HotpotQA and 2WikiMultihop, but as an arXiv preprint it lacks peer review and community validation.

## [FaultGene: LLM-Assisted Fault-Augmented Test Generation for HIL Validation of Autonomous Driving Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29969) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FaultGene is an LLM-assisted framework that automatically generates executable fault-augmented test scenarios for hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) fault-resilience validation of autonomous driving systems. Given a baseline Scenic/CARLA scenario and a structured fault specification, it produces a fault-augmented test case, using offline refinement with semantic-consistency checks and execution-level feedback to correct candidates and fine-tune a generator that then works directly at deployment time. Preparing fault-augmented scenarios for HIL testing is largely manual, limiting test coverage, scalability, and efficiency; automating this step could make fault-resilience validation of autonomous driving cyber-physical systems more scalable and reproducible. The approach also shows how LLMs can be grounded with execution feedback to generate domain-specific test artifacts rather than free-form code. FaultGene relies on a baseline Scenic/CARLA scenario plus a structured fault specification, and its offline refinement uses semantic-consistency checks and execution-level feedback to ensure candidates preserve the intended scenario context and produce the desired fault-induced degradation. The resulting generator is claimed to produce fault-augmented tests at deployment time without manually engineered reference executions or repeated refinement loops.

**Background**: Hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) validation runs the autonomous driving stack on real embedded hardware while the vehicle and environment are simulated, bridging the gap between costly, hard-to-reproduce physical road tests and simulation that cannot fully capture hardware-dependent behavior and fault propagation. Scenic is a scenario description language used with the CARLA simulator to define diverse driving scenarios, and fault injection deliberately introduces faults into sensing or computation to assess system resilience. Researchers introduced BatchBench, a leaderboard built on the JUMP-CP dataset that evaluates AI systems' robustness to batch effects and domain shift in biomedical images from unseen experimental batches and data sources. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in biomedical AI, where models often fail under distributional shifts caused by different experimental conditions, and provides a standardized way to measure progress in domain adaptation for the field. BatchBench leverages JUMP-CP, one of the largest public biomedical imaging datasets generated with the Cell Painting assay, which exhibits pronounced batch effects and domain shifts, making it a challenging testbed for robustness evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Batch effects are systematic technical variations that arise when data is collected in different batches, such as across plates, days, or laboratories, and they can confound biological signals. Domain shift refers to the change in data distribution between training and test environments, which often degrades AI performance. The JUMP-CP dataset, created by the JUMP-Cell Painting Consortium, contains images of cells stained with fluorescent dyes to reveal cellular structures, and it is widely used for image-based profiling in drug discovery.

## [LoopRec: Item-Level Adaptive Recursion for Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30128) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes LoopRec, an item-level adaptive recursion method for sequential recommendation that uses a weight-shared block with a lightweight gate at each recursion boundary to decide whether each item exits early or continues to deeper computation. Experiments on public benchmarks with four backbone families show that LoopRec consistently outperforms matched fixed-depth counterparts. This work challenges the common practice of allocating a fixed computational budget to every item in a user's history, showing that non-uniform depth assignment can improve both efficiency and recommendation quality. It could influence the design of more efficient and effective sequential recommender systems. The method converts fixed global depth into a learned per-item property without auxiliary depth labels, and analysis shows that lower-frequency items and more recent interactions naturally receive deeper computation. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Sequential recommendation models user interaction histories to predict the next item, and existing models typically apply the same number of layers or computation to every item. Adaptive computation aims to allocate more resources to harder examples, and weight sharing (reusing the same block recursively) is a common technique to increase depth without adding parameters.

## [Systematic Survey Maps Generative AI's Role in IoT Computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07635) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2504.07635v2) presents a systematic literature review, following PRISMA methodology, of Generative AI integration within IoT computing. It introduces a comparison framework and well-defined research questions to explore past, present, and future directions of GenAI-IoT convergence. Existing research on GenAI for IoT is fragmented into narrow applications, so this holistic survey helps both experts and newcomers understand the broader opportunities, challenges, and implications across sectors like healthcare, manufacturing, and smart cities. The survey uses the PRISMA (Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses) methodology and explicitly aims to distinguish itself through a broader scope and more rigorous cross-domain investigation than prior fragmented studies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI refers to models that can create new content such as text, images, or code, while the Internet of Things (IoT) is a network of connected devices that collect and exchange data. Their combination, often called AIoT (Artificial Intelligence of Things), aims to enable systems that can sense, learn, and act with less human intervention. Edge computing, which processes data near its source, is often linked to IoT to reduce latency. This survey addresses the need for a comprehensive overview as GenAI and IoT both evolve rapidly.

## [PRISM: Training-Free LLM Framework Boosts Financial Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14130) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PRISM introduces a training-free framework for financial document retrieval that combines prompt engineering, in-context learning, and candidate refinement, raising Recall@100 on FiQA-2018 from 0.7783 to 0.8326 and cutting per-query cost by about 35% on FinAgentBench while placing third on its private leaderboard. This work shows that LLMs can deliver competitive financial retrieval without expensive fine-tuning, offering a practical path for cost-sensitive deployments and highlighting when added complexity (like multi-agent coordination) does not pay off. The framework's k-reciprocal encoding stops helping once upstream recall is high, and multi-agent coordination loses 0.0702 and 0.0681 combined NDCG@5 to single-path controls, with the gap concentrated at the chunk stage; the authors also release an expert-annotated FinAgentBench evaluation set and a label-robustness sweep over 16 alternative ground-truth constructions.

FiQA-2018 is a benchmark from the WWW'18 challenge for financial opinion mining and question answering, while FinAgentBench is a newer benchmark for agentic retrieval with multi-step reasoning in finance. k-reciprocal encoding is a re-ranking technique originally from person re-identification that encodes nearest neighbors to refine distances.

## [Synthesis and Verification of Register Automata over Data Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19100) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents a local-search synthesis procedure and an exact robustness checker for Deterministic Register Automata (DRAs) over ordered data sequences, reducing robustness to coverability and then to a shortest-path problem. For a fixed number of registers, the checker runs in polynomial time, and the framework is implemented in a tool called RAExc. This work bridges formal methods and machine learning by using learned DRAs as interpretable surrogates for neural sequence classifiers, with PAC guarantees on fidelity and stability. It enables verification of robustness for sequence classifiers, which is crucial for safety-critical applications. The checker's complexity is polynomial in the number of registers but exponential in the register count, and it produces variable-length witnesses without a priori length bounds, supporting alignment costs like DTW. The approach is evaluated on benchmark languages and sequentialized MNIST, demonstrating high accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Data sequences such as time series and sensor streams are words over large or infinite data domains, and their properties often depend on comparisons between values at different positions, which finite automata cannot express directly. Deterministic Register Automata (DRAs) extend finite automata with registers that store data values, enabling compact symbolic descriptions of quantitative sequence languages. The coverability problem asks whether a certain state can be reached or exceeded, and it is a fundamental problem in formal verification, often solved via Petri net techniques.

## [Li-Net uses sparse Top-K attention for efficient multi-channel forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18712) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2603.18712v3) introduces Li-Net, a linear-network architecture for multi-channel time series forecasting that combines a sparse Top-K Softmax attention mechanism with a multi-scale projection framework. The model dynamically compresses representations across sequence and channel dimensions, processes them through a configurable non-linear module, and fuses multi-modal embeddings to guide attention toward the most informative time steps and channels. Traditional forecasting methods often neglect interactions between channels, so Li-Net's explicit modeling of linear and non-linear cross-channel dependencies addresses an under-explored area. Its reported lower memory usage and faster inference could make it attractive for real-world applications in finance, supply chain, and energy planning where both accuracy and efficiency matter. The sparse Top-K Softmax attention restricts each query to only the top-K most relevant key-value pairs, reducing computation compared to full attention. Experiments on multiple real-world benchmarks show competitive accuracy against state-of-the-art baselines, with ablation studies and parameter sensitivity analyses validating each component, though the work is a preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-channel (multivariate) time series forecasting involves predicting future values for multiple interrelated signals, such as sales across products or energy demand across regions. Capturing dependencies within each channel and between channels is challenging, and many classic methods focus on temporal patterns while paying less attention to cross-channel interactions. Sparse attention, like Top-K Softmax, is a technique from transformer models that reduces computational cost by having each element attend to only a subset of others, and it is increasingly applied to time series for efficiency.

## [PAC-2026 Protocol Introduces Publication Authority for Falsifiable AI Claims](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17631) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes Publication Authority, an exact-state, non-transferable, single-use publication capability within the PAC-2026 (Publication-Accountability Calculus) protocol, evaluated under specification version SF-4. SF-4 defines six non-compensating obligations—evidence, runs and artifacts, measurement disclosure, authorization, surface correspondence, and lifecycle continuity—each yielding a target-bound witness, localized counterexample, or localized unverifiability, with only a fresh, complete all-pass record deriving a permit consumed by one atomic publication transition. This work addresses a core problem in AI accountability: AI-assisted claims can appear authoritative even when their evidence, analysis, authorization, and correction history describe different states. By making publication records falsifiable and independently challengeable, the protocol could provide a rigorous foundation for auditing AI-generated content in journalism, research, and other high-stakes domains. The evaluation explored 110,764 distinct safe reachable states across ten models, with 76 unsafe configurations yielding the expected violation or observer countermodel. An in-house, instance-blind study (G7-B) matched 183 scored expectations after shared integration, and three author-led native comparisons identified gaps in a constructed in-toto/OPA profile regarding measurement-state admission, proof-expiry evaluation, and required-run identity, which were repaired to restore parity on tested cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: PAC-2026 (Publication-Accountability Calculus) is a proposed machine-readable protocol for authorizing and auditing the publication of AI-assisted claims, developed as an AIJIM Protocol candidate. It builds on formal verification and accountability concepts, using state-space exploration to ensure that only claims meeting all obligations can be published. The protocol aims to separate evidence horizon from verification time and reject authentic but causally invalid authorization, drawing on ideas from supply-chain security frameworks like in-toto and policy engines like OPA.

## [SkillNEO: Non-Elitist Evolution for Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20926) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SkillNEO introduces a non-elitist evolutionary optimization method for LLM agent skills that separately selects a skill version to use and versions to keep improving, allowing lower-scoring intermediate versions to receive further edits. On 86 SkillsBench tasks across eight domains, it raises GPT-5.4 Mini's average reward from 49.1% to 66.4%, and the resulting skills also improve stronger models without further editing. This work addresses a real limitation of reward-greedy skill evolution: useful partial repairs can be discarded before their benefits emerge. By turning a weaker model's partial progress into reusable procedural knowledge that also helps stronger models, it suggests a practical path for accumulating agent expertise across model generations. The method compares how related skill versions perform on the same task to identify working steps to preserve and remaining failures to fix, and it can revisit earlier versions using later evidence. Gains span all eight domains, and on the 41 tasks with modified skills, reuse improves GPT-5.5 Luna by 10.5 percentage points and places GPT-5.5 10.6 points above its published original-skill reference, though Mini still fails some task requirements.

## [REFLEX: Flexibility-Calibrated Stochastic Bridge Generates RNA Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26261) ⭐️ 6.0/10

REFLEX introduces a sequence-conditioned stochastic bridge on SE(3) that uses experimental B-factors to calibrate residue-wise bridge widths during training, enabling generation of diverse RNA backbone ensembles from sequence alone without MSA subsampling at inference. This approach addresses the key trade-off between ensemble diversity and structural fidelity in RNA conformer generation, potentially improving our ability to model RNA's multiple functional states for drug discovery and structural biology. On a multi-conformer RNA benchmark, REFLEX achieves the best precision-recall trade-off for ensemble coverage across evaluation thresholds, and recovery-stress ablations show gains in target-frame recovery under stronger perturbations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: RNA molecules often function through multiple conformational states, but generative models can collapse to averaged structures or drift into unsupported regions. Stochastic bridges are generative models that learn to transport a simple distribution to a complex data distribution, and SE(3) is the special Euclidean group representing rigid-body motions in 3D space. B-factors from experimental structures measure atomic flexibility, with higher values indicating more mobile regions.

## [AnnoCaseLaw: Legal Annotation Benchmark of Civil Negligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00128) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AnnoCaseLaw introduces a densely annotated benchmark of 471 U.S. civil-negligence court opinions with over 12,000 expert highlights across five annotation categories, and evaluates proprietary frontier, open-weight, legal-specialised, and decision models on their ability to reproduce these annotations. This benchmark addresses the high-stakes challenge of automating expert legal annotation, where models must distinguish judicial reasoning from other legally relevant discussion, and it highlights the need to evaluate annotation coverage and cost alongside aggregate scores for feasible deployment. Models perform better on Facts and Outcome than on Application of Law to Facts, Procedural History, and Relevant Precedents; on Application of Law to Facts, proprietary frontier models achieve the strongest balance of reasoning coverage and precision, while the open-weight Gemma 4 achieves comparable Macro-F1 at a fraction of the estimated inference cost.

## [CausaLM Quantifies Causal Effect of Symptoms on Clinical NLP Diagnoses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19394) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2503.19394v2) uses the CausaLM framework to generate counterfactual text representations where target symptoms like chest pain are 'forgotten,' and employs Textual Representation-based Average Treatment Effect (TReATE) to quantify the causal effect of symptom presence on a model's predicted disease distribution. The results are contrasted with correlation-based baselines such as CONEXP. This work moves clinical NLP beyond correlation-based diagnosis, offering a principled way to estimate how specific symptoms causally influence model predictions. Such causal grounding could lead to more trustworthy and interpretable decision support tools, reducing misdiagnoses when symptoms are ambiguous or shared across conditions. The approach uses counterfactual text representations to isolate the causal effect of a symptom, quantified via TReATE, and compares it against correlation-based baselines like CONEXP. However, the study is an incremental exploration focused on 'chest pain' and may require validation across broader symptom sets and clinical datasets.

## [Energy-Based OOD Detection Enables Selective Teacher Guidance in RL Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.16590) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2506.16590v2) proposes an energy-based transfer learning method that uses out-of-distribution (OOD) detection to selectively issue guidance from a pretrained teacher policy only in states within its training distribution. The authors theoretically link energy scores to state-visitation patterns under on-policy reinforcement learning and empirically show improved sample efficiency and performance in both single-task and multi-task settings. This work addresses a key challenge in transfer reinforcement learning: negative transfer from a teacher when the new task differs significantly from the teacher's training task. By preventing sub-optimal guidance, the method could make continual learning and multi-task RL more reliable and sample-efficient, benefiting applications where agents must adapt to related tasks over time. The method uses energy scores as an OOD detector to decide when the teacher intervenes, and the authors provide a theoretical connection between these scores and state-visitation under on-policy RL. Empirical validation covers both single-task and multi-task settings, though the paper is an incremental improvement in a niche area and has not yet generated community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) agents often struggle with sample efficiency, especially when they must adapt to new but related tasks over time. Transfer learning can help by reusing knowledge from a pretrained teacher policy, but if the new task is too different, the teacher's guidance may bias exploration toward low-reward behaviors—a problem known as negative transfer. Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection identifies inputs that fall outside the training distribution, and energy-based models provide a scalar energy score that can serve as an OOD detector.

## [UniPrompt-CL: Shared Prompt Pool for Efficient Continual Medical Image Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10954) ⭐️ 6.0/10

UniPrompt-CL is a rehearsal-free domain-incremental learning framework for medical image classification that uses a single backbone and a single inference, sharing a unified prompt pool across transformer layers and expanding it with only a few prompts per new domain. On diabetic retinopathy, it improves average accuracy by 2.0 percentage points and substantially reduces forgetting compared to the strongest backbone-matched baseline, while on skin cancer it achieves competitive accuracy at the lowest training cost among DINOv2-B configurations. Medical AI systems must adapt to shifting data distributions across hospitals and devices while privacy constraints prevent storing past patient data, so a rehearsal-free method that reduces cross-layer redundancy and enables efficient retraining under limited on-site compute is practically valuable. The controlled comparison showing benefits arise from retention rather than learning new domains helps clarify when such prompt-sharing approaches are appropriate. A controlled comparison varying only pool organization shows the benefit is largest on medical benchmarks, smaller on OfficeHome, and reverses on a heterogeneous six-stage DomainNet stream, indicating the approach helps only under certain domain shifts. The method uses a single ViT inference rather than multiple backbones or independent layer-wise prompt pools, reducing cross-layer redundancy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Domain-incremental learning (DIL) enables vision models to adapt to changing data distributions while retaining knowledge from previous domains, and rehearsal-free DIL is more practical when privacy concerns or training time prevent revisiting old data. Visual prompt tuning (VPT) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning approach that adds learnable tokens to steer frozen Vision Transformers (ViTs), and DINOv2 is a family of self-supervised vision foundation models that provide strong general-purpose features for downstream tasks.

## [OracleAD: Interpretable Multivariate Time Series Anomaly Detection via Causal Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16511) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OracleAD is a new unsupervised framework that encodes each variable's past sequence into a causal embedding to jointly predict the current time point and reconstruct the input window, then uses self-attention to capture spatial relationships and aligns embeddings to a Stable Latent Structure (SLS) representing normal behavior. Anomalies are detected via a dual scoring mechanism based on prediction error and deviation from the SLS, achieving state-of-the-art results on multiple real-world datasets while providing interpretability at the variable level. This work addresses the growing complexity of anomaly detection models by offering a simpler, interpretable alternative that can pinpoint root-cause variables, which is crucial for practical deployment in domains like finance, healthcare, and industrial monitoring. It also introduces a novel stable latent structure concept that could inspire future research on interpretable time series analysis. OracleAD uses a dual scoring mechanism combining prediction error and deviation from the SLS, enabling fine-grained anomaly diagnosis at each time point and across individual variables. The SLS is a reference structure that summarizes stable inter-variable relationships observed during normal behavior, and any noticeable deviation originates from embeddings that violate learned temporal causality, directly identifying root-cause variables.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multivariate time series anomaly detection involves identifying unusual patterns across multiple correlated variables over time, and is challenging because anomalies are rare and often unlabeled. Traditional methods rely on increasingly complex architectures tuned to benchmarks, which may detect only fragments of anomalous segments and overstate performance. OracleAD simplifies this by using causal embeddings and self-attention to model temporal and spatial relationships, and introduces a Stable Latent Structure to represent normal relationships for interpretable detection.

## [GPT-4.1 Generates Clinically Coherent Mentalization Profiles, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.08945) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv study (2512.08945v2) assessed whether GPT-4.1 can generate narrative mentalization profiles structurally coherent with the dimensions of mentalization-based treatment (MBT). Five MBT-trained psychiatrists rated 50 human-model dialogue profiles on 4 canonical MBT axes and 2 higher-order dimensions, yielding mean scores of 3.63–3.98 and interrater reliability (ICC) of 0.60–0.84 in the evaluative domain. This work introduces the concept of "algorithmic reflectivity," suggesting that LLMs can reproduce the formal linguistic structure of reflective discourse without experiential or affective processes. It opens avenues for using LLMs as research and training tools in psychiatry, particularly for studying the linguistic aspects of mentalization, though clinical application remains distant. The study found strong associations between evaluative and argumentative coherence (Pearson r 0.826–0.967) and a negative control with mismatched dialogue-profile pairs yielded minimal ratings, supporting validity. However, clinicians noted limited affective variability and contextual integration in the outputs, indicating that while structural coherence is achieved, experiential depth is lacking.

Mentalization-based treatment (MBT) is a psychotherapy approach that targets improvements in mentalization and emotional regulation, drawing from psychodynamic and attachment theories. ## [Centralized Q-learning underperforms independent Q-learning in tabular predator-prey study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17454) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper compares independent Q-learning (IQL) and centralized Q-learning (CQL) in a tabular predator-prey gridworld across all four predator-prey pairings, with speed and stamina constraints, on 5x5 and 8x8 grids trained for 200,000 episodes each. The study finds that CQL costs its agents in both roles on both grids and its predators coordinate no more than IQL's, with one benefit: acting on the best joint action cut capture time by 42% on the small grid with faster prey and unlimited stamina, but helped nowhere on the larger grid. This study challenges the common assumption that centralized learning improves coordination in multi-agent reinforcement learning, showing that in a simple tabular setting, CQL can be detrimental. It highlights the importance of checking state-space coverage and implementing methods alike when comparing multi-agent learners, which could influence how researchers design and evaluate MARL algorithms. The paper measures coordination directly with pre-registered predictions and finds that on the larger grid, a tie-breaking default decided which method won even though agents passed a learning check, suggesting that tabular comparisons are only trustworthy if they also check state-space coverage and implement methods alike. The authors propose simple checks for both.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) often uses centralized training with decentralized execution, where a centralized critic learns joint actions to improve coordination. Tabular studies are used to compare independent and centralized learners because they avoid function approximation, but earlier work has only partially tested coordination, often fixing agent bodies or measuring only team performance. This paper addresses those gaps by varying body constraints and grid sizes and measuring coordination directly.

## [Heisenberg Antiferromagnet Hamiltonian Mapped to Weighted Graph Laplacian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04943) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper shows that in a fixed-magnetization basis, the Heisenberg Hamiltonian equals a constant minus the weighted Laplacian of a 'Hilbert graph' whose vertices are spin configurations and edges connect configurations related by exchanging antiparallel spins. This maps the ground-state energy to the largest eigenvalue of the Laplacian (a spectral Max-Cut relaxation) and the phase structure to an antiferromagnetic XY model on the same graph. This graph-theoretic reformulation provides a unified framework linking wave-function sign structure, Hamiltonian stoquasticity, non-convexity of phase optimization in variational Monte Carlo, and the average sign in quantum Monte Carlo, potentially impacting quantum many-body physics and optimization algorithms. The Hilbert graph is isomorphic to the token graph of the underlying lattice. A diagonal sign transformation removes all positive off-diagonal matrix elements if and only if the Hilbert graph is bipartite, reproducing the Marshall sign rule on bipartite lattices via graph two-coloring; otherwise, the minimal remaining positive weight equals the sum of edge weights left uncut by the Max-Cut.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: The Heisenberg antiferromagnet is a fundamental model in quantum magnetism where neighboring spins interact with a tendency to anti-align. The Marshall sign rule provides a known sign structure for the ground-state wave function on bipartite lattices, which is crucial for quantum Monte Carlo simulations. Graph Laplacians and spectral graph theory are widely used to study cuts and clustering, and Max-Cut is a well-known NP-hard optimization problem.

## [HealthMamba: Uncertainty-Aware Spatiotemporal Graph State Space Model for Healthcare Visit Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05286) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HealthMamba is a new framework that combines a Unified Spatiotemporal Context Encoder, a Graph State Space Model called GraphMamba, and an uncertainty quantification module to predict healthcare facility visits. Evaluated on four large-scale real-world datasets from California, New York, Texas, and Florida, it achieves around 6.0% improvement in prediction accuracy and 3.5% improvement in uncertainty quantification over state-of-the-art baselines. Accurate and reliable visit prediction is crucial for healthcare resource allocation and public health policy, especially during emergencies. HealthMamba's explicit modeling of spatial dependencies between facilities and its uncertainty awareness could make predictions more trustworthy for decision-makers. The model integrates three uncertainty quantification mechanisms and uses a hierarchical spatiotemporal modeling approach via GraphMamba. The reported improvements are averaged over four state-level datasets, but the abstract does not specify the types of abnormal conditions tested or the computational cost of the uncertainty module.

## [Data-Free Finite-Volume CNN Simulates Steady Euler Flow Around Cylinder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01001) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a data-free finite-volume convolutional neural network (FV-CNN) that solves steady compressible Euler flow around a circular cylinder at eight Mach numbers (2–15) without training on CFD or experimental field data. The method uses shared-face fluxes for conservative spatial balances and automatic differentiation for gradients, achieving density and pressure relative L2 discrepancies of 5.43–18.69% and 6.25–19.70% compared to finite-resolution CFD. This work demonstrates that physics-constrained neural networks can approximate compressible flow solutions without any training data, potentially reducing reliance on costly CFD simulations for preliminary design and analysis. It also provides a systematic comparison with the earlier AD-CNN method, highlighting trade-offs in accuracy and robustness. The FV-CNN shows smaller closed-band defects than AD-CNN in most comparisons (32/32 self-centered and 22–31/32 CFD-centered over seven half-widths), but AD-CNN retains smaller enthalpy RMS discrepancies in 7/8 cases and better shock-position RMS at Mach 11 and 15. Initialization robustness is not established: ten new Mach-15 initializations yield density discrepancies of 12.04–43.70% (mean 24.34%), with dispersion exceeding the historical gap, and reference-independent selection of coefficients, seeds, and checkpoints is not yet established.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: The Euler equations describe inviscid compressible fluid flow and are fundamental in aerodynamics. The finite volume method is a numerical technique that solves partial differential equations by tracking fluxes across cell faces, ensuring conservation. Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) embed physical laws into training, but typically require data or residual minimization; this work extends that idea by using a finite-volume discretization directly within a CNN architecture, eliminating the need for field data.

## [StructLens uses maximum spanning trees to reveal layer-wise structure in LM representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03328) ⭐️ 6.0/10

StructLens introduces a framework that constructs maximum spanning trees from semantic representations in residual streams to analyze how tokens are organized in representation space. The analysis finds that middle layers exhibit the strongest local-span organization, and that smaller local units become detectable earlier in pre-training while larger units emerge later. This work fills a gap in interpretability research by offering a holistic structural view of representations, complementing mechanistic approaches like attention analysis and Sparse Autoencoders. It could help researchers better understand how language models organize information across layers and training time, with implications for model analysis and development. The method is inspired by tree representations in dependency parsing and uses maximum spanning trees computed on semantic similarities between token representations. The code is publicly available on GitHub, and the analysis includes pre-training checkpoints to track the emergence of local units over training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Interpretability research often examines how models compute representations via attention patterns or Sparse Autoencoders, but the organization of the resulting representations is less studied. A maximum spanning tree is a tree that connects all nodes in a weighted graph with the maximum possible total edge weight, and it can be computed using algorithms like Prim's or Kruskal's. The residual stream is the central pathway in transformer models where representations accumulate across layers, and analyzing its structure can reveal how information is organized.

SYNAPSE is a training-free framework that extracts per-layer [CLS] representations, trains lightweight linear probes to rank neurons globally and per class, and applies forward-hook interventions during inference to analyze and stress-test Transformer models. The paper reports that task-relevant information is encoded in broad, overlapping neuron subsets, providing functional stability, while small structured manipulations in weight or logit space can redirect predictions. This work offers a systematic, repeatable method to probe internal representations and measure robustness without retraining, which is valuable for interpretability and AI safety research in sensitive domains like healthcare and cybersecurity. It could help developers identify label-specific vulnerabilities and design more robust Transformer models. The framework requires no retraining of the base model and uses forward hooks to intervene on internal representations during inference, enabling controlled experiments across tasks and architectures. The abstract is truncated in the provided content, so full experimental results and limitations are not fully assessable.

## [Path-Coupled Bellman Flows for Distributional Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08253) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Path-Coupled Bellman Flows (PCBF), a continuous-time distributional reinforcement learning method that uses flow matching with source-consistent Bellman-coupled paths to learn return distributions. It couples current and successor return flows through shared base noise and employs a λ-parameterized control-variate target to trade bias for variance reduction. PCBF addresses boundary mismatch and high-variance bootstrapping in flow-based distributional RL, potentially improving distributional fidelity and training stability. This could benefit risk-sensitive decision-making and offline RL applications where accurate return distributions are crucial. The method maintains a pathwise affine relation to the successor flow at intermediate times without requiring time-t marginals to satisfy a distributional Bellman fixed point for all t. The λ parameter controls the bias-variance trade-off: λ=0 gives an unbiased sample Bellman target, while λ>0 reduces variance at the cost of controlled bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Distributional reinforcement learning (DRL) aims to learn the full return distribution rather than just its expectation, enabling risk-aware agents. Flow matching is a simulation-free approach for training continuous normalizing flows by regressing vector fields of fixed conditional probability paths. The Bellman equation provides the recursive relationship for value functions in RL, and in DRL it extends to distributions.

## [CAVE: Structured Credit Assignment for Fragmented Visual Reasoning in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16416) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces CAVE, a structured process-reward method built on GRPO that evaluates intermediate reasoning steps at the action level using three signals: belief update, evidence acquisition, and adaptive focus control. It also presents TRACER-Bench, a new benchmark covering four nonlocal, semantically confusable reasoning dimensions with key intermediate evidence for supervising reasoning paths. This work addresses Fragmented Visual Reasoning, a known weakness of VLMs in integrating nonlocal visual information, and offers a process-reward approach that could improve robustness in complex multimodal tasks. The new benchmark may also facilitate standardized evaluation of such reasoning capabilities. CAVE operates at the action level within interleaved visual reasoning, using three complementary signals to guide optimization of each reasoning action. Experiments show substantial gains on tasks requiring fragmented visual evidence integration, including public benchmarks and TRACER-Bench, while maintaining competitive performance on general multimodal evaluations.

## [DualView: Adaptive Local-Global Fusion for Multi-Hop Document Reranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18767) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DualView, a lightweight cascaded reranking framework that combines a Local Scorer with stacked cross-attention for fine-grained query-document relevance and a Global Scorer using Transformer-based context aggregation for inter-document dependencies, fused via an Adaptive Gate conditioned on query semantics. On MuSiQue, it achieves 99.4% Top-4 Recall and 97.8% Full Hit accuracy at 4.0 ms latency (249 QPS), outperforming 600M-parameter cross-encoders like BGE-Large (92.0% Recall) and Jina-v3 (90.1% Recall) with 5-6x lower latency. This work shows that a lightweight dual-view reranker can substantially outperform much larger cross-encoders in both accuracy and latency for multi-hop document reranking, which is critical for knowledge-intensive applications like retrieval-augmented generation and multi-hop question answering. It suggests that explicitly modeling inter-document context and adaptive fusion is more effective than simply scaling up cross-encoder parameters. The framework operates as a post-retrieval stage over E5-base-v2 candidates with offline cached embeddings, and ablation studies confirm that both Local and Global views contribute substantially to multi-hop performance. However, results are reported under a fixed candidate set reranking setting, so performance may vary with different retrievers or in end-to-end pipelines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-hop question answering requires aggregating information from multiple documents, making it challenging to identify the minimal relevant document set from retrieved candidates while maintaining high recall. Reranking is a common post-retrieval step that reorders candidate documents to improve relevance, and cross-encoders are often used for this but are computationally expensive. Dual encoders are efficient but can lack precise relevance quantification, motivating hybrid approaches like DualView that combine local cross-attention scoring with global context aggregation.

The paper introduces the Task-Aware Attentive Neural Process (TA-ANP), a unified probabilistic framework that fuses floating car data (FCD) with sparse fixed-detector measurements to infer metropolis-scale traffic states. It also constructs the Metropolitan Multi-Source Traffic Dataset (MMTD), covering 26,166 road segments with 2,371 fixed-sensor-equipped segments for quantitative evaluation. This work addresses the underdetermined nature of network-wide traffic state inference by providing trustworthy uncertainty quantification, which can enable more efficient fixed-sensor placement with fewer deployments. It impacts intelligent transportation systems by improving real-time estimation and forecasting at both observed and unobserved locations. TA-ANP casts global traffic state inference as a stochastic process, leveraging meta-learning properties of neural processes to adapt to sensing configuration changes without retraining. A task-aware multi-query attention module with distinct spatiotemporal inductive biases handles three subtasks jointly, while Monte Carlo Dropout captures both aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty.

## [Mixture-of-Control: Sparse MoE for Cross-Layer State-Based Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31397) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced Mixture-of-Control (MoC), a state-based fine-tuning method that treats block-wise control states as experts in a sparse mixture-of-experts process, enabling efficient cross-layer communication between transformer blocks. Experiments on 33 datasets and 10 pretrained models show MoC consistently outperforms existing state-based fine-tuning methods while maintaining comparable memory and compute efficiency. This work addresses a key limitation of state-based fine-tuning—the lack of inter-block information exchange—without incurring the heavy computational overhead of prior cross-block communication mechanisms, potentially making parameter-efficient adaptation of large transformers both more effective and practical. MoC adaptively integrates local and global control signals by routing block-wise control states through a sparse mixture-of-experts mechanism, and the authors report that it maintains efficiency comparable to existing state-based methods in both memory and compute across diverse transformer-based benchmarks.

State-based fine-tuning is a newer alternative that updates lightweight control states rather than weights, offering substantial memory savings, but most existing methods apply controls only within each block, limiting cross-block information flow. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) is a technique that sparsely activates different subnetworks (experts) for different inputs, improving capacity and efficiency.

## [DrugReason combines knowledge graph and LLM reasoning for drug repurposing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06779) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose DrugReason, a multi-view reasoning framework that adaptively routes query-specific reasoning paths to specialized experts, combining grounded knowledge graph (KG) multi-hop reasoning with LLM-generated mechanistic rationales. Experiments on PharmaDB, DDInter, and DrugBank show it improves average performance over strong single-view baselines and is competitive with or superior to graph-based alternatives while offering interpretable routing-based predictions. Drug repurposing offers a faster, cheaper route to clinical translation than de novo discovery, but the candidate drug-disease space is enormous and relationships often depend on complex multi-hop mechanisms. By addressing the complementary weaknesses of KGs (limited to observed graph structure) and LLMs (lack of factual grounding and hallucination risk), DrugReason points toward more reliable and interpretable computational repurposing pipelines. The framework uses a cross-expert distillation objective so experts can share knowledge without losing specialization, and its routing decisions make predictions interpretable. Evaluation is limited to three biomedical datasets (PharmaDB, DDInter, DrugBank), and the abstract does not report absolute performance numbers or clinical validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Drug repurposing seeks new therapeutic uses for existing compounds, and computational approaches typically screen large drug libraries using similarity, network, or structure-based methods. Knowledge graphs organize curated biomedical evidence into relational networks that support multi-hop reasoning, while large language models can generate flexible mechanistic explanations from pretrained knowledge but may hallucinate unsupported facts. Combining these two views is an active research direction for improving factual grounding and interpretability.

## [LRQS: Low-Rank Quantile Surfaces for Bivariate Causal Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16931) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Low-Rank Quantile Surfaces (LRQS), a bivariate causal model in which, in the causal direction, an unknown monotone transformation of the conditional quantile surface admits a low-rank functional decomposition. The authors prove generic identifiability of LRQS and provide a nonparametric causal score with an alternating fitting procedure combining rank-constrained approximation and isotonic estimation of the monotone transformation. LRQS extends identifiable causal discovery beyond location-scale and post-nonlinear heteroscedastic noise models by capturing higher-rank changes in conditional distributional shape, which could improve causal direction inference in settings with strong nonlinear distortions. This matters for researchers in causal inference and machine learning who need robust bivariate causal discovery methods. LRQS subsumes location-scale noise models and post-nonlinear heteroscedastic noise models, while allowing multiple quantile bases to represent changes beyond location-scale effects. Identifiability is generic: reverse representability under the same constraints occurs only for exceptional, fine-tuned cause marginals, and experiments show LRQS is especially effective when distributional shape or observation distortion goes beyond existing assumptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Causal discovery aims to infer the direction of cause and effect from observational data, often by exploiting asymmetries between the cause and effect distributions. Quantile regression models the conditional quantiles of a response variable, and isotonic regression fits a non-decreasing function to data, which is useful for estimating unknown monotone transformations. Low-rank models assume that a complex function can be decomposed into a small number of components, simplifying estimation and identifiability arguments.

## [Survey Formalizes Agentic Video Understanding with Challenge-to-Design Taxonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31713) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv survey (2609.31713v2) reviews video understanding agents—systems that use video as the primary information source and solve tasks through adaptive state construction and action selection—and formalizes an agent loop for video understanding. It organizes the literature via a challenge-to-design taxonomy linking context bottlenecks to hierarchical evidence memory, evidence sparsity to active evidence acquisition, temporal causality to state and process tracking, and multimodal ambiguity to role-specialized coordination. As LLMs handle longer temporal contexts and more modalities, the field is shifting from fixed video-language inference to agents that decide what to inspect, retain, verify, and act upon, so this taxonomy gives researchers a shared vocabulary and design map. It could guide work on long-video QA, streaming and embodied video applications, and benchmark design. The survey also reviews state space paradigms, learning paradigms, supervision signals, benchmarks, and evaluation protocols, and identifies open directions toward agentic-native temporal modeling and video-native agents. As a survey it synthesizes existing work rather than presenting a new model or empirical breakthrough, and a companion project page collects related papers on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional video-language models encode a video once (often at a fixed frame rate) and then perform all reasoning in text, which is costly for long videos and can miss key evidence. Agentic video understanding instead lets an LLM-based agent actively choose which segments or frames to inspect, at what granularity, and through which modality, maintaining memory and verifying evidence as it goes. Related lines of work include hierarchical memory frameworks such as EventMemAgent and PyraVid, and interactive video reasoning systems that interleave inference with active evidence acquisition.

## [Concept Bottleneck Models' Robustness Depends on Perturbation Type, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38625) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.38625v3) introduces a generator-based evaluation framework that separates continuous geometric perturbations in latent space from discrete semantic interventions in concept space to compare standard classifiers and Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs). Using both empirical sensitivity metrics and certified robustness via randomized smoothing, the authors reconcile previously contradictory findings about whether CBMs improve robustness. The work clarifies that interpretability and robustness are distinct objectives, showing that concept bottlenecks do not uniformly improve robustness but instead redistribute sensitivity across perturbation spaces and model families. This has direct implications for practitioners who adopt CBMs assuming interpretable intermediate representations automatically yield safer models. The framework evaluates robustness empirically through prediction- and concept-level sensitivity metrics and certifiably through randomized smoothing in both latent and concept spaces, while also varying task conditions such as class semantic similarity and concept vocabulary size. The authors conclude that interpretability does not inherently confer robustness and that the trade-off depends critically on the perturbation regime and task structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs), introduced by Koh et al. in 2020, force a model to predict human-understandable concepts before making a final task prediction, making intermediate reasoning interpretable. Randomized smoothing is a certifiable defense that converts any base classifier into a robust majority-vote classifier by injecting controlled random noise, yielding provable robustness guarantees. Prior studies on whether CBMs are more or less robust than standard classifiers reported mixed results, which this paper attributes to conflating different robustness notions and perturbation regimes.

## [Hessian Curvature Signals Fail to Rank Models Under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39099) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.39099v2) empirically tests whether Hessian-based curvature proxies can rank models for out-of-distribution deployment in computational biology. Across molecular property, protein fitness, and drug-response tasks, the authors find that a novel forward-only Hessian-norm proxy and common Hessian measures correlate with generalization gap but do not reliably identify the deployment-best model. This negative result challenges the assumption that geometry-based signals can guide model selection when validation data no longer reflects deployment conditions, a common scenario in computational biology and beyond. It cautions practitioners against using curvature proxies as a substitute for proper out-of-distribution validation and highlights the gap between predicting generalization gap and ranking models. The study introduces a forward-only proxy motivated by the Hessian norm and compares it with Hessian trace and top-eigenvalue measures; notably, the proxy still correlates with generalization gap on most tasks even when trace and top-eigenvalue relationships are weak or reversed. The authors also find that augmenting validation helps some distribution shifts but significantly harms others, and that low geometric scores can favor collapsed predictors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Model selection typically uses validation data from the training distribution, but deployment often involves distribution shift, where validation may not preserve the best model. Hessian-based curvature measures describe the sharpness of the loss landscape and have been proposed as proxies for generalization. Spearman rank correlation is used to measure how well such proxies rank models relative to true performance.

## [MWRL: Reinforcement Learning Recovers Multiple Minimal Sufficient Witnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07226) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper formalizes the problem of minimal-witness identification and introduces Minimal-Witness Reinforcement Learning (MWRL), which uses a coverage-based credit assignment derived from a single verifier bit to recover multiple minimal sufficient witnesses. It also derives a value iteration planner for small-scale exact recovery and a policy gradient method that scales to large language models. This work expands reinforcement learning beyond single-solution optimization by making entire families of minimal witnesses learnable from verifier feedback, which could improve explainability and mechanism discovery in computation and science. It is particularly relevant for settings where multiple distinct explanations or causes must be identified. MWRL credits each proposal by the coverage the group union would lose without it, unifying minimality and recovery of alternatives from a single black-box verifier bit. Experiments show it recovers most minimal witnesses, while other methods return redundant supersets or a single witness; code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: A minimal sufficient witness is an irreducible set of conditions that is sufficient to produce an outcome—essentially what we mean by an explanation, mechanism, or reason. Standard reinforcement learning methods typically converge to a single solution or produce redundant ones, so recovering multiple distinct minimal witnesses from only a binary verifier signal is challenging. Credit assignment—measuring how much each action or proposal contributes to the final reward—is a core problem in reinforcement learning, and value iteration is a classic planning algorithm that iteratively updates state values to find optimal policies.

## [9B Clef-Flash decision model underperforms TF-IDF baselines on symptom classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15029) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper fine-tunes Cloudflare's 9-billion-parameter Clef-Flash decision model on the 24-class Symptom2Disease benchmark using QLoRA, reaching 95.42% accuracy and a macro F1 of 0.9551, up from 65.00% for the unadapted weights. However, TF-IDF linear SVM and logistic regression baselines trained on the same split achieve 98.75% accuracy with sub-millisecond CPU inference, outperforming the large model. This negative-result study cautions against assuming that large decision foundation models automatically outperform simple, well-tuned linear classifiers on short, lexically distinctive text classification tasks. It suggests that practitioners should benchmark against strong TF-IDF baselines before investing in large models, especially for medical NLP tasks with limited data. The fine-tuning used 4-bit quantized low-rank adaptation (QLoRA) on a stratified split of 960 training and 240 test cases, and a Leave-One-Symptom-Out clause-ablation analysis showed that predictions can hinge on a single distinguishing clause—removing a psoriasis-related clause dropped predicted probability from above 99.99% to 0.12% and shifted the prediction to impetigo. The authors note that short, lexically distinctive benchmarks like Symptom2Disease cannot demonstrate an advantage over simple baselines, and validation on longer real clinical text with clinician review is needed.

**Background**: Clef-Flash is a 9-billion-parameter decision foundation model from Cloudflare, built on a Qwen3.5 backbone, that scores a closed set of candidate labels in a single forward pass without generating text. QLoRA is an efficient fine-tuning method that loads the base model in 4-bit quantized form and trains small low-rank adapters, drastically reducing memory usage. TF-IDF is a classic text representation that weights words by frequency and inverse document frequency, often paired with linear classifiers like SVM or logistic regression for text classification.

## [PLATO: Pessimistic Latent Perturbation for Robust Offline Meta-RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15428) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PLATO introduces a method that perturbs inferred task latents outward from the training centroid to synthesize counterfactual tasks, training the policy on off-support latents with a pessimism-weighted decoder ensemble. It provides theoretical bounds on the out-of-support generalization gap and improves out-of-support returns on eight MuJoCo benchmarks over strong COMRL baselines. This work addresses a critical but overlooked gap in context-based offline meta-reinforcement learning: policies trained on clean task representations still fail on out-of-support tasks where the latent lies outside the training distribution. By explicitly training for such scenarios, PLATO could make offline meta-RL agents more reliable in real-world deployment where task parameters may extrapolate beyond the offline data. PLATO assumes a manifold hypothesis that task latents lie on a low-dimensional manifold and that out-of-support tasks are farther from the training centroid; it proves the perturbation leaves the training support by a controllable margin and that the generalization gap is bounded by the perturbation reach. The method relies on a learned decoder ensemble to roll out short trajectories at perturbed latents and down-weights steps with high ensemble disagreement, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Context-based offline meta-reinforcement learning (COMRL) learns task representations from pre-collected offline datasets so that a policy can quickly adapt to new tasks. The implicit assumption is that a clean representation separating task identity from behavior-policy artifacts will transfer to out-of-support tasks, but the encoder may produce latents outside the training support where the policy has never been trained. The manifold hypothesis suggests that high-dimensional data lie on a lower-dimensional manifold, which PLATO exploits by perturbing latents along directions away from the training centroid.

## [WeGR unifies generative retrieval with behavior-aware tokenization and preference alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16979) ⭐️ 6.0/10

WeGR introduces a unified generative retrieval framework with two components: CoCo-VAE, a collaborative and collapse-free tokenizer that injects query-to-item and item-to-item signals before quantization, and DHPO, a dynamic horizon policy optimization method that expands reward horizons to alleviate sparse rewards. A six-week online A/B test on a large e-commerce search platform showed relative gains of 2.539% in orders, 1.508% in CTR, and 1.381% in CVR, while reducing CPU and GPU usage by 25.5% and 26.1%. This work addresses two practical bottlenecks in industrial generative retrieval—preserving collaborative signals in SID tokenization and overcoming sparse RL rewards—and demonstrates measurable business gains with lower compute cost, suggesting a viable path for deploying generative retrieval in large-scale search systems. CoCo-VAE couples first-layer codebook refresh to reactivate underutilized codewords with fidelity anti-collapse regularization, penalizing collisions in deeper layers to produce balanced behavior-aware SIDs. DHPO progressively expands reward evaluation and policy optimization horizons for coarse-to-fine preference learning without auxiliary reward models, and the framework supports multiple search scenarios with tens of millions of daily page views.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Generative retrieval (GR) is a paradigm in which a model directly generates identifiers of relevant items (e.g., docids or semantic identifiers, SIDs) autoregressively, rather than matching queries to documents via traditional index-based retrieval. SIDs are discrete, multi-token representations obtained by quantizing continuous item features into structured code sequences, and their quality—especially balanced codebook usage and preserved collaborative signals—critically affects retrieval performance. Reinforcement learning is often used to prioritize high-value candidates, but sparse rewards occur when generated SIDs fail to match positive items, motivating horizon-expansion techniques like DHPO.

## [Model Capacity Frontier Framework Maps Performance Limits of LLM-Supported Social Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24168) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the Model Capacity Frontier (MCF), a framework that systematically explores the configuration space of LLM-supported agent-based models to estimate Pareto frontiers representing attainable performance. It demonstrates MCF in a crowd simulation of university commencement events with approximately 12,000 agents, showing that initial LLM integration improves flow direction but degrades temporal occupancy and spatial distribution, while subsequent architectural refinement recovers some performance. This work addresses configuration sensitivity—a practical challenge where subtle modeling choices like prompt design can substantially alter simulation outcomes—by providing a systematic method to identify performance limits and guide architectural development. It offers researchers empirical targets for refinement and helps understand the trade-offs when integrating LLMs into agent-based models for social simulation. The MCF characterizes empirical performance across admissible configurations and uses multi-objective optimization to estimate Pareto frontiers, revealing which performance dimensions expand and which remain limited. The case study shows that LLM integration initially harms temporal occupancy and spatial distribution while improving flow direction, and later architectural refinement of the LLM layer mitigates these trade-offs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Agent-based models (ABMs) simulate collective behavior by modeling individual agents and their interactions, and researchers are increasingly integrating large language models (LLMs) as agents to capture richer, context-sensitive human behavior. However, LLM-supported ABMs introduce an open-ended design space where choices like prompt design and environment coupling can significantly affect outcomes. The Pareto frontier is a concept from multi-objective optimization representing the set of solutions where no objective can be improved without worsening another, thus illustrating trade-offs between competing goals.

## [TrustAgent-RFF: Agentic Reliability Framework for RF Fingerprinting Under Domain Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24930) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TrustAgent-RFF introduces a prediction-level reliability framework for radio frequency fingerprinting (RFF) that operates with a frozen I/Q classifier and no target-domain labels. It combines counterfactual stability, prototype consistency, acquisition domain similarity, and signal quality into a source-calibrated trust estimator, and uses an LLM-based planner to adaptively select diagnostic actions before a deterministic decision layer outputs Accept, Reject, or Defer. RFF models often suffer severe accuracy degradation under domain shift while still producing high softmax confidence, making simple confidence thresholding unreliable for deployment. This work addresses a real-world trustworthiness gap in wireless security and device authentication, where incorrect predictions could lead to unauthorized access or misattribution. Experiments across five RFF datasets and multiple classifier backbones show an average source-to-target accuracy drop of 38.7%, and many incorrect target-domain predictions remain highly confident. The framework relies on a frozen classifier and source-calibrated lower-tail penalties and cross-module interactions to reduce trust when diagnostic evidence is weak, with an LLM planner balancing evidence, trust state, action costs, and budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Radio frequency fingerprinting identifies wireless devices by unique hardware imperfections in their transmitted signals, such as I/Q imbalance, and is used for authentication and illegal transmitter detection. Domain shift occurs when the data distribution changes between training (source) and deployment (target) conditions—here due to changes in receiver, distance, collection day, or environment—causing models to fail despite high confidence. Counterfactual stability measures whether a prediction remains consistent under small, semantically irrelevant perturbations, while prototype consistency compares predictions to learned class prototypes.

## [CA-MOPD: Capability-Aware Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26950) ⭐️ 6.0/10

CA-MOPD proposes allocating distillation strength across multiple teachers based on verifier-estimated capability gaps rather than behavioral discrepancy, and achieves an average score of 32.22 across six benchmarks, outperforming naive MOPD, Open-MOPD, and merged initialization by 0.86, 0.83, and 0.74 points respectively. This work addresses a real limitation of discrepancy-based allocation in multi-teacher distillation, where strong supervision may be applied even when the student already matches or exceeds teacher performance, and could improve the efficiency of integrating domain specialists into a single student model. CA-MOPD first merges reinforcement-learning specialists into a strong student initialization, then uses verifier-estimated teacher-student capability gaps to adjust domain-level distillation strength while keeping dense token-level guidance from teachers; controlled comparisons confirm the benefits under matched training budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) is a post-training paradigm that combines multiple domain-specialized reinforcement learning teachers into a single student model by distilling them on the student's own rollouts. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a teacher model to a smaller student, and in on-policy settings the student learns from its own generated trajectories rather than fixed datasets. Existing allocation methods often use behavioral discrepancy between teacher and student to decide supervision strength, but mismatch does not always indicate a capability deficit.

<a id="item-378"></a>
## [RayCo4D: Ray-Conditioned Continuous Motion Model for Monocular 4D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28351) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RayCo4D introduces a ray-conditioned continuous motion model that separates visual measurements from latent 3D motion states, using reference-time 3D queries constrained to camera rays and a white-noise-on-acceleration prior to couple sparse SE(3) motion nodes. The inferred shared motion is transferred to Gaussian primitives via dual-quaternion blending, with local SE(3) residuals capturing fine-scale non-rigid deformation. This work addresses the challenge of integrating fragmented long-range tracks into a shared continuous motion representation, improving structural and temporal consistency under rapid motion and incomplete tracks for monocular 4D reconstruction. It could benefit applications in dynamic scene novel-view synthesis and continuous-time rendering. The method maintains an SE(3) pose and body-frame velocity at sparse support times per node, and uses local many-to-many associations to constrain shared latent motion nodes from image and depth observations. Experiments show competitive novel-view synthesis quality and improved consistency, though the approach is incremental within a specialized niche.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 23, 04:00

**Background**: Monocular 4D dynamic reconstruction aims to infer 3D motion and geometry from a single video, which is challenging due to noisy and intermittent observations. SE(3) is the special Euclidean group representing rigid motions in 3D, commonly used in robotics and computer vision. A white-noise-on-acceleration prior is a continuous-time Gaussian process prior that couples pose and velocity states, enabling smooth trajectory interpolation.
